  - GOTCHA (discovered, not caused): `apps/api/scripts/rls-security-check.ts`'s cleanup (`admin.auth.admin.deleteUser(id)`) does **not** cascade-delete the corresponding `users_profile` row — confirmed live (4 orphaned profiles found from this session's own throwaway-user runs, `handle: null`, no auth.users counterpart). Every past session's RLS-check/verification throwaway users have likely been leaking `users_profile` rows (and transitively `attempts`/`srs_cards`/etc, which cascade FROM `users_profile`, not from `auth.users` directly) the same way since Session 15. Cleaned up this session's own orphans; did not attempt to fix the underlying gap (a `users_profile.id` FK to `auth.users(id) on delete cascade` would need its own migration) — flagged here rather than silently patched, since it's outside Community's scope.
  - NOTE (concurrent work): a separate process was actively building a Billing/entitlements feature in the same tree throughout this session (`lib/razorpay.ts`, `routes/billing.ts`, `services/{billing,entitlements}.ts`, `0057_billing.sql`, committed mid-session as `ed68081`) — confirmed disjoint via `git status`/`git diff` before staging every file, including recovering one shared file (`packages/shared/src/index.ts`) where a concurrent write had silently dropped this session's `export * from "./community"` line moments after it was added. Also discovered mid-session: this file's own session log currently jumps straight from "Auth phase" to this entry — Sessions 16/17/18 (SRS, the Tier-1 game layer, the Profile rebuild), which this session started with in its loaded context, are **not present in the on-disk CLAUDE.md at HEAD**. Not reconstructed here (out of scope, and risky to guess at retroactively) — flagged for the user. [RESOLVED 2026-07-13 — Sessions 16/17/18 are now present in CLAUDE.md; see `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §0 (S3).]
  - Verified end-to-end against the running API + cloud DB: `pnpm --filter api security:rls` — **39/39 PASS** (extended with a new §7 covering public thread/post read, flagged/removed hiding except from the owner, owner-only `post_votes`, and `reports`' no-select policy); a full Playwright pass seeding real signed-in sessions via `localStorage`'s `sb-<ref>-auth-token` key (no OAuth/OTP automatable) — hub, node Discussion tab thread creation, cross-user reply + upvote, Hindi locale, report, share-for-peer-review + feed, and the admin Reports tab + dismiss action, both 1440px and 390px — **zero console errors**. `pnpm build` (shared+api+web) clean. All synthetic rows (threads/posts/votes/reports/shared_answers/post_screenings + throwaway auth users and their orphaned profiles) deleted afterward.
- Production Polish (2026-07-08): PWA, web push, performance, SEO, error handling/a11y, and a demo-seed script — six areas, all verified against a real production build (`vite build` + `vite preview`, not just `pnpm dev`).
  - **PWA**: `vite-plugin-pwa` with `strategies:'injectManifest'` (not `generateSW`) since push needs a custom service worker — `src/sw.ts` precaches the build via `self.__WB_MANIFEST`, NetworkFirst-with-fallback for SPA navigations, StaleWhileRevalidate for read-mostly catalog GETs (syllabus/questions/notes), and a `setCatchHandler` falling back to the precached app shell or `public/offline.html`. Icons rasterized from the existing `favicon.svg` mark onto a branded canvas via a one-off `sharp` script (`scripts/generate-pwa-icons.mjs` → `public/pwa/*.png`, incl. maskable variants). Manifest is genuinely bilingual: the Web App Manifest spec has no i18n mechanism, so `vite-plugin-pwa` generates the English `manifest.webmanifest` and a hand-written `public/manifest.hi.webmanifest` sits alongside it — `locale-layout.tsx` swaps the `<link rel="manifest">` href on every locale change. SW registration + a "new version available"/"ready offline" toast via `virtual:pwa-register/react` (`registerType:'prompt'`, never a silent mid-session swap). Offline-capable SRS reviews: generalized `lib/offline-queue.ts`'s localStorage contract into `lib/offline-queue-idb.ts` (same `OfflineQueue<T>` shape, backed by `idb`), `use-srs-review-queue.ts` switched to it with a one-time migration for anyone with reviews stranded in the old localStorage key.
  - **Web push**: real VAPID keypair generated + wired into both `.env` files (gitignored; `.env.example`s document the pair-must-match requirement). `0058_push_subscriptions.sql` (`push_subscriptions`, `push_preferences`, strict owner-only RLS matching the 0053/0056 shapes) + `0059_notification_schedule_push_tracking.sql` (`pushed_at` so the sender fires each row exactly once). `lib/push.ts` (server) wraps `web-push`, never throws (a per-subscription send failure returns `"gone"|"error"` so the caller can prune dead endpoints). `push/sender.ts`'s `runPushSender()` drains `notification_schedule` (the same rows Session 15's in-app bell reads) respecting each user's per-type opt-out, sends in the user's `preferred_locale`, and prunes subscriptions the push service reports as 404/410 — wired into the existing hourly `NOTIFICATIONS_CRON` tick (`daily/scheduler.ts`) plus a manual `pnpm push:send` for testing. Frontend: `lib/push-client.ts` (permission request + `PushManager.subscribe`, only ever called from a click handler after `PushNotificationsCard`'s own soft in-app pre-prompt — never a cold native dialog), per-type toggles, a `pushsubscriptionchange` handler in `sw.ts` (browsers occasionally rotate a subscription unprompted; the SW can't read the auth token from its own scope to tell the server, so it hands the fresh subscription to an open tab via `postMessage`, which can) completed client-side in `use-push-navigation.ts`.
  - **Performance**: `vite.config.ts` `manualChunks` (function form — Vite 8/Rolldown's object-form `manualChunks` type doesn't accept the vendor-name-keyed shape anymore) splits vendor bundles; discovered via Lighthouse that Vite's default `modulePreload` conservatively preloads **every** vendor chunk reachable from *any* lazy route in this single-index.html SPA — `vendor-charts` (recharts, ~110KB) and `vendor-motion` (framer-motion, ~40KB) were loading on the public landing page, which uses neither. Fixed with `build.modulePreload.resolveDependencies` filtering those two out (they still load fine as plain non-preloaded dynamic imports the moment a route that needs them is visited). `ResultReviewList` (mock-test review, 100-150 variable-height rows on one unpaginated page) now uses `@tanstack/react-virtual`'s `useWindowVirtualizer` with `measureElement` re-measurement (a fixed-size estimate would break given streaming-explanation rows that grow after render). TanStack Query `staleTime` tuned per query's actual change cadence (syllabus tree 10min, questions/papers 5min, cutoffs 60min — was a flat 30s default for everything). Devanagari font imports switched from the generic `@fontsource/noto-sans-devanagari/{400,500,700}.css` (bundles every script the font ships, one `@font-face` per script) to the `devanagari-*.css` subset files (1461→329 bytes per weight) since Latin text never renders in this font. `Screenshot` (landing page marketing images) gained explicit `width`/`height`/`aspect-ratio` to prevent CLS.
  - **SEO**: `PageSeo` component (react-helmet-async) on the landing page — canonical, hreflang alternates (hi/en/x-default), OG/Twitter tags. **Gotcha**: routing `<title>`/`<meta name="description">` through Helmet duplicated them, because Helmet only manages tags it renders itself and doesn't remove/replace the pre-existing static ones already in `index.html` (this is a real CSR app with static fallback tags, not a pure-SSR app where Helmet owns the whole `<head>`) — confirmed live via Playwright (`document.querySelectorAll('title').length === 2`, and crawlers/parsers that take "first tag wins" would've silently ignored Helmet's per-page description). Fixed by having `PageSeo` mutate the existing static tags directly via `useEffect` + `document.title`/`querySelector` for those two, keeping Helmet only for tags with no static counterpart (canonical/hreflang/OG/Twitter, which don't conflict). OG images (1200×630, en+hi) generated via the existing `satori`+`@resvg/resvg-js` pipeline (`services/share-image.ts`'s pattern) in a new one-off script (`scripts/generate-marketing-og.ts`) rather than ad-hoc `sharp`-drawn text, so Devanagari renders correctly (explicit Fontsource-loaded fonts, not whatever the machine running the script happens to have installed). `public/sitemap.xml` + `robots.txt` cover the two genuinely public pages. `scripts/prerender.mjs` snapshots `/en`/`/hi` to `dist/{en,hi}/index.html` post-build via Playwright (own static server on a temp port, `html.replaceAll` to strip the temp server's absolute origin from runtime-injected modulepreload hrefs — otherwise they'd 404 cross-origin once deployed); kept as a separate `pnpm prerender` step, not chained into the default `build` script, so a plain build never depends on Chromium being installed. **`/pricing` was deliberately left un-prerendered** — it's still behind `requireAuth` (see the new TODO entry above), so isn't actually a public page today.
  - **Error handling & Sentry**: `RootErrorBoundary` (class component, catches failures outside the router — provider crashes) + `AppErrorBoundary` (`react-router` `ErrorBoundary` on the `/` and `/:locale` routes, bilingual, distinguishes a real 404 via `isRouteErrorResponse` from other errors). **Bug found while wiring this up**: the pre-existing top-level `*` route (`loader: () => redirect(...)`) was silently swallowing every within-app 404 — `/en/nonexistent-anything` doesn't match any child of `/:locale`, so react-router considered the whole `/:locale` branch non-matching and fell through to the top-level catch-all, which just redirected to the landing page with zero signal anything was wrong (confirmed live: `page.url()` after navigating to a bogus path showed a silent redirect, not the new 404 page). Fixed with an explicit `{path:"*", loader: () => { throw new Response("Not Found", {status:404}) }}` as the last child of `/:locale`, so that branch now correctly matches and its `ErrorBoundary` renders — verified both locales, zero console errors. `@sentry/react`/`@sentry/node` wired but **dynamically imported only when `VITE_SENTRY_DSN`/`SENTRY_DSN` is set** (no DSN configured yet — both are a documented no-op) so an unconfigured deploy pays zero bundle/dependency cost.
  - **Accessibility pass** (test player, SRS review player, admin review queue, the newly-virtualized result review list): fixed real WCAG AA failures, not just added attributes — `question-palette.tsx`'s answered/marked cells were solid `bg-tulsi`/`bg-marigold` fills using `-foreground` tokens calibrated for a `/15`-tint pairing (per the frontend-design skill), measuring **~1.5:1/~1.34:1 in dark mode** (computed via the WCAG relative-luminance formula) — fixed to the tint+border pairing (**~6.3:1 light / ~8.2:1 dark**). Same raw-token-on-card-background issue in `result-review-list.tsx`'s option labels (~2.5:1/~3.67:1 light) and `session-summary.tsx`'s rating counts (marigold ~2.15:1, tulsi ~2.54:1) — both switched to the `-foreground` variants already used correctly elsewhere (`review-card.tsx`). ARIA: palette cells' labels now include answered/marked/unanswered status (not color-only); option correctness mirrored to screen readers via `sr-only` text; streaming explanation container got `aria-live="polite"` (the countdown timer deliberately did NOT, to avoid spamming); FSRS rating buttons gained sr-only keyboard-shortcut hints; the reveal button drops out of tab order once revealed (`tabIndex={-1}`) since it becomes a no-op; the review-queue tab bar gained `role="tablist"`/`role="tab"`/`aria-selected`. Radix's `Sheet` (Dialog) already trap focus correctly — verified, not touched.
  - **Demo seed script**: `pnpm demo:seed [--email] [--reset]` (`scripts/seed-demo-account.ts`) creates one real Supabase Auth account (never a raw `auth.users` insert — same admin-API primitive as `migrate-dev-user.ts`/`set-password.ts`) and seeds ~4 weeks of history by calling the *real* service logic wherever practical — `services/attempts.ts` for MCQ attempts, `lib/fsrs.ts`'s actual `reviewCard` scheduler (replayed at historical timestamps, not hand-set), `daily/streak.ts`'s `refreshStreak` replayed day-by-day so the final streak is exactly what the nightly job would compute, `mastery/compute.ts`, `services/milestones.ts`, `services/community.ts`. **Disclosed tradeoff**: the ~10 answer-writing evaluations are inserted as structurally-valid rows (real rubric weights, real model id) rather than invoking the live two-pass Claude pipeline — generic template feedback text, not a genuine per-answer critique, to avoid real LLM cost for placeholder demo content; everything else (MCQ grading, FSRS, streak/mastery/milestones) is the real computed pipeline against real data. `--reset` wipes only that one account's rows (26 table/column pairs) and never touches another user. **Bug found and fixed along the way**: most PRE_GS1/PRE_CSAT PYQs in this dev DB have `marks:null` on both `questions` and `test_questions` (only `mocks:build` ever set it), which silently scored sectional attempts 0/0 — the script backfills `marks` only where null (never overwrites a real value), a scoped data fix that also benefits real (non-demo) users hitting the same gap, not just the seeded account. Live-verified: `demo@prayasup.app` / `Demo1234!` — 14 MCQ attempts (scores trending 34%→85% over 4 weeks), 10 evaluations across 7 Mains papers, 44 SRS cards with 150 logged real FSRS reviews across every card state, mastery on 43 nodes, a 15-day streak with 2 banked freezes, 6 milestones, 2 discussion threads + 1 shared peer-review answer.
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm --filter web build` + `pnpm --filter web prerender` + `pnpm --filter api build` all clean; a full Playwright pass against the production build (`vite preview`, not `pnpm dev`) across `/en`, `/hi`, `/en/auth`, light+dark — **zero console errors**; SW registers and activates, offline reload of an already-visited page and offline navigation to a never-visited page both correctly serve the cached app shell instead of failing; prerendered `dist/en/index.html`/`dist/hi/index.html` confirmed to contain real hero copy + correct hreflang/OG tags with zero duplicate title/description tags and zero leaked temp-server URLs; the 404 fix confirmed live in both locales. Lighthouse mobile audits were run repeatedly against this sandboxed/shared-CPU environment and showed real, expected wins from the modulePreload fix (removed ~150KB from the critical path) and from prerendering (FCP score 0.15→0.3, Speed Index 0.73→0.86) alongside a genuine, disclosed tradeoff (LCP/TTI can read worse under Lighthouse's synthetic throttling due to the non-hydrating CSR remount after the prerendered paint, and separately this sandbox showed enough run-to-run noise on its own — 0.68 to 0.72 on an *identical* rebuild — that no single number here should be read as a precise production baseline).
- Deploy-Prep (2026-07-08): repo-side deployment configuration for Web→Vercel, API→Render, cron jobs, CI, and the launch runbook — no account was created and nothing is actually deployed yet (no CLI was installed/authenticated for Vercel/Railway/Render/gh in this environment, and account creation/domain purchase/webhook config are the kind of external, credentialed actions this assistant doesn't do unprompted). Everything below is either committed config or a real bug fix, each verified locally (a Docker build+run, `tsc`, `vite build`, `oxlint`) rather than just written.
  - **REAL BUG FOUND: the documented prod start path was broken.** `apps/api`'s `start` script was `node dist/index.js`, but `@prayasup/shared` deliberately ships raw `.ts` source with no build step (main/types → `src/index.ts`, a Session-1 decision to keep `apps`/`packages` symlink-resolvable without a build step). Actually running `pnpm build && node dist/index.js` — the literal production path — throws `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` on the very first `@prayasup/shared` import, confirmed live; every prior session's "verified" claims only ever exercised `pnpm dev` (`tsx watch`), never this path. Fixed by making `start` run `tsx src/index.ts` (tsx moved from `devDependencies` to `dependencies` — it's a genuine runtime dependency now, not just a dev convenience) instead of adding a build step to `@prayasup/shared` (much larger, unrequested architecture change). Added a `typecheck` script (`tsc --noEmit`) so CI still gates on types even though `dist/` is no longer executed. Live-verified via `docker build`+`docker run`: the containerized image boots and `GET /api/v1/health` returns 200.
  - **REAL BUG FOUND: every `--env-file=.env` script would crash under platform-injected env vars.** Node's `--env-file` flag (used by every `tsx ...` script in `apps/api/package.json`, including `ca:run`/`daily:build`/`mocks:build`/etc.) throws if the file doesn't exist — confirmed live (`node --env-file=.env.doesnotexist` → `not found`, non-zero exit). Render/Railway/etc. inject env vars directly with no `.env` file present, so every one of these cron-invoked scripts would have failed its very first line in production. Fixed by switching every occurrence to `--env-file-if-exists=.env` (silently continues when absent, confirmed live) — one `sed` across the whole scripts block, same command works unchanged in local dev (loads `.env`) and in prod (no file, no crash).
  - **CORS was hardcoded to `http://localhost:3000`** (`apps/api/src/index.ts`) — would have rejected every request from the real deployed web origin. Now reads a comma-separated `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` env var (new in `.env.example`), always additionally allowing `localhost:3000` for dev, plus no-Origin requests (curl, the Razorpay webhook, server-to-server).
  - **Cron jobs**: `ca:run`/`daily:build`/`qgen:topup` already had CLI entrypoints; `nightly:settle` (streak settle + Perfect Day + mastery recompute + mentor insights — bundled in one script because `daily/scheduler.ts`'s dev-only equivalent already groups them, and splitting them would mean maintaining the grouping twice) and `notifications:run` (notification_schedule generation + push send — `push:send`/`scripts/send-push.ts` only did the second half) did not, so both are new (`apps/api/scripts/{nightly-settle,notifications-run}.ts`). Extracted the `forEachUser` per-user-isolated-failure helper out of `daily/scheduler.ts` into `lib/users.ts` (alongside the existing `listAllUserIds`) so the dev scheduler and these new standalone scripts share one implementation instead of two.
  - **API → Render** (`render.yaml`, `apps/api/Dockerfile`, `.dockerignore`): Render over Railway because Cron Jobs are a first-class resource with independent run history/logs, a better fit for 5 distinct background jobs than node-cron ticks in one always-on process. Dockerfile is a 2-stage build scoped to `apps/api` + `packages/shared` only via `pnpm install --filter api...` (apps/web's much heavier toolchain never enters this image) — build+run smoke-tested locally with real Docker (health check returns 200 inside the container). `render.yaml` defines the always-on web service + 5 cron jobs, all secrets centralized in one `envVarGroups` entry so they're entered once, not five times, in the dashboard. UTC-vs-IST conversions for each cron schedule are documented in `docs/operations.md`'s reference table.
  - **Web → Vercel** (`apps/web/vercel.json`): SPA fallback rewrite (all paths → `/index.html`) plus immutable-cache headers for hashed assets. Build command deliberately left as plain `pnpm run build`, NOT `build:ci` (which also runs the Playwright-based `prerender` step) — installing a Chromium binary on a managed build image the team doesn't control is a real, disclosed fragility risk (no guaranteed root/apt access for `--with-deps`), not a hypothetical one; enabling it is documented as an explicit opt-in follow-up in `docs/operations.md`, not silently dropped.
  - **CI** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`): typecheck (api) + build (web, api) + lint (web, oxlint) on every PR and push to `main`. Every step run locally first to confirm it actually passes on this tree before committing the workflow (existing `oxlint` warnings are pre-existing Fast-Refresh/exhaustive-deps warnings, not new — `oxlint`'s exit code is 0 on warnings).
  - **`docs/launch-checklist.md`** (new): the 10-step real-device smoke test from the brief (signup→onboarding→daily quiz→typed answer→streaming evaluation→handwritten upload→mentor doubt→note reading→payment test-mode→push→PWA install), written as a checklist to run against the live prod URLs once they exist — **not run yet**, since no prod deployment exists in this environment to run it against. Ends with the `git tag v1.0.0` step, deliberately gated on every box above being checked for real.
  - **`docs/operations.md`** (new): the one-time manual deploy steps this session could not perform itself (Vercel/Render account + env var setup, domain DNS, Razorpay live webhook, Google OAuth, Supabase prod hardening — each needs a human with the relevant account/credentials), the cron schedule reference table, and the weekly ops routine from the brief (`content:fetch`, Review Queue triage, CA source health, `cost:report`, eval-harness re-tuning).
  - Verified (all locally, this session): `docker build`+`docker run` the new Dockerfile → `GET /api/v1/health` → 200 inside the container; `pnpm --filter api typecheck`, `pnpm --filter api build`, `pnpm --filter web build`, `pnpm --filter web lint` — all pass, matching exactly what `ci.yml` runs. **NOT verified** (needs real accounts this environment doesn't have): an actual Vercel/Render deployment, DNS, the Razorpay/Google OAuth dashboard steps, or any step of `docs/launch-checklist.md`. Did not tag `v1.0.0` — that's explicitly gated on the checklist passing against real production URLs.
  - **FOLLOW-UP edge-case audit (same day, right after the above)**: re-verified every claim against the ACTUAL deploy targets rather than trusting the first pass, and found real problems.
    - **CRITICAL, live-confirmed: `apps/api/.env` — the real file holding `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`/etc. — was actually baked into the Docker image.** The `.dockerignore`'s bare `.env`/`.env.*` patterns look like gitignore syntax but Docker's ignore-pattern matching isn't identical — a pattern with no `/` or `**/` prefix does NOT reliably match at every depth the way it does in `.gitignore`. Confirmed by literally inspecting the built image's filesystem (`docker run --entrypoint find ... -iname ".env*"` returned `/app/apps/api/.env`). Fixed by anchoring every such pattern with `**/` (`**/.env`, `**/.env.*`, `!**/.env.example`) and re-verified on a `--no-cache` rebuild that the file is gone from the image while the container still boots and serves `/api/v1/health` correctly. This would have shipped real production secrets into any image ever pushed to a registry — the single most important fix from this pass.
    - **Checked, turned out fine (don't "fix" this again)**: worried `--env-file-if-exists` might not exist on Node 20 (a websearch summary claimed it needs v22.9.0, which would have made every cron job crash immediately inside the `node:20-slim` container). Verified empirically instead of trusting the search result — ran it directly inside `node:20-slim` (resolves to v20.20.2) and it works fine, so it must have been backported into the Node 20.x line at some point after the version the search result named. Empirical container test beat the secondhand claim.
    - Render Blueprint schema for referencing an `envVarGroups` entry from a service, and valid `region`/`plan` values for cron-type services, were things this session had originally guessed/hedged on. Fetched Render's actual current `blueprint-spec` docs to check: `region: singapore` and `plan: starter` for cron jobs are both confirmed valid. The `envVarGroups` service-level linking mechanism remains genuinely ambiguous even in the fetched docs (they say a service can have an `envVarGroups` field "matching the format of the root-level" one, but never show a worked example) — left the file as originally written (most literal reading) but strengthened the in-file comment to tell whoever connects the Blueprint to check the dashboard's proposed-changes preview before approving, rather than presenting this as settled when it isn't.
    - **CORS gap**: the fix from the first pass (`ALLOWED_ORIGINS` env allowlist) didn't handle Vercel preview deploys — each PR preview gets a different, unpredictable origin, so `docs/operations.md`'s own suggestion ("point previews at the same prod API") would have hit CORS failures on every single preview. Added an opt-in `ALLOWED_ORIGIN_SUFFIXES` env var (suffix match, e.g. `.vercel.app`) alongside the exact-match list. Also: trailing-slash normalization (an Origin header never has one, so a pasted `https://x.com/` in the dashboard would otherwise silently never match anything), `localhost:3000` is now only auto-allowed outside production (was previously always-on, a minor unnecessary prod attack surface), and a loud `logger.warn` fires at boot if `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` is unset in production (previously this would fail silently — every browser request rejected with no indication why). All four behaviors live-verified via curl against a running instance in both dev and prod mode.
    - **Reliability gap in the new cron scripts**: `forEachUser`'s failure-to-list-users path just logged and returned — correct for the always-running dev scheduler (there's always a next tick to retry), but in the new one-shot `nightly:settle`/`notifications:run` CLI scripts it meant the process would still exit 0 having silently done nothing, which Render's cron dashboard would report as a successful run. Added an opt-in `throwOnListFailure` param (default off, preserving the scheduler's existing behavior) that the two new CLI scripts pass `true` for, so an actual infra-level failure now surfaces as a real non-zero exit / failed run instead of a false-green one.
    - Re-verified everything end-to-end after all of the above: `pnpm --filter api typecheck`/`build`, `pnpm --filter web build` (this time confirmed for real — temporarily moved `apps/web/.env.local` aside, confirmed the build still succeeds with no `VITE_*` vars present exactly as a GitHub Actions runner would see it, then restored the file and byte-verified the restore via checksum), `pnpm --filter web lint`, and a final from-scratch `docker build --no-cache` + `docker run` covering the `.env`-leak fix, the CORS allowlist/suffix/warning behavior, and the health check, all together.
    - NOTE: a separate concurrent process landed its own commit (`749d471`, edge-case fixes to the Production Polish SSRF/unhandled-rejection/retry work) and has further in-flight uncommitted edits to `mentor-chat.tsx`/`doubts.tsx`/`router.tsx`/message files/two new `_audit-*.ts` scripts during this pass — confirmed via `git diff --stat` that none of it overlaps this session's file list, and staged only this session's own files by explicit name (never `git add -A`).

## Auth-gap closure (2026-07-08): forgot/change password + public pricing
Closed three of the launch-blocking gaps from the TODO list above — no new migration, no new backend tables, all client-side Supabase Auth calls plus one router-mounting change.
  - **Shared min-strength check**: new `packages/shared/src/password-strength.ts` (`checkPasswordStrength`, `MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH=10`, a ~100-entry common-password denylist) is the ONE place length/commonness is decided — used by the `set-password` CLI (replacing its old `length<8` check), the new reset page, and the new Change Password card, so a password can never be accepted in one path and rejected in another.
  - **Forgot password**: `auth.tsx` gained a "Forgot password?" step (`resetPasswordForEmail` via a new `sendPasswordReset` on `AuthProvider`) and a new public route `/:locale/auth/reset` (`routes/auth-reset.tsx`). The reset page listens for supabase-js's `PASSWORD_RECOVERY` auth event (with a session+`code`-param fallback for older client timing) rather than trusting session presence alone, and treats an explicit `?error=...` param OR the total absence of a `code`/error as an honest "link expired or invalid" state with a way back to request a new one — never a silent hang.
  - **Change password**: new `components/profile/change-password-card.tsx` in Profile → Settings, calling a new `updatePassword` on `AuthProvider` (`auth.updateUser({password})` directly against Supabase — no Express round-trip, no re-entry of the current password, matching how `updateUser` already works for an active session).
  - **GOTCHA (caught live by the user, not by review)**: shipped the first cut with the new-password submit button silently `disabled` below the 10-char floor and zero on-screen explanation — a user testing their own real reset link (likely typing their known 9-char dev password out of habit) saw only a washed-out, unresponsive-looking button (Tailwind's `disabled:opacity-50`) with no hint why. Fixed by adding a persistent "at least 10 characters, and not a commonly used password" hint under the field in both the reset page and the Change Password card — a disabled submit control must always say why, not just refuse silently.
  - **`/pricing` made public**: `routes/billing.ts` split into `billingPublicRouter` (just `GET /billing/plans` — confirmed it returns only `is_active` plan rows, nothing user-specific) mounted in `index.ts` BEFORE `requireAuth`, and the existing `billingRouter` (subscription/entitlements/order — still genuinely user-scoped) staying mounted after. `router.tsx` moved `pricing` out of the `require-auth`/`app-shell` subtree to a top-level public sibling of `auth`/`landing`. Since it no longer inherits app-shell chrome for ANYONE (signed in or out), `pricing.tsx` now renders its own light standalone header (brand mark, locale toggle, contextual Sign-in/Dashboard link) — a deliberate simplification over maintaining two versions of the page. `useBillingSubscription` gained an `enabled` option so the signed-out page never fires a doomed authed request; clicking "Choose this plan" while signed out now routes through `/auth?redirect=/pricing` and back rather than attempting checkout. `scripts/prerender.mjs` now snapshots `/en/pricing`/`/hi/pricing` alongside the landing page.
  - Verified end-to-end against the running dev servers (both locales, 390px/1440px): a full Playwright pass for forgot-password (real "check your email" notice after requesting a reset for a nonexistent address, honest invalid-link screen both with no code and with an explicit `?error=` param) and for pricing (public reachability, plan prices rendering unauthenticated, "Choose this plan" round-tripping through `/auth?redirect=/pricing`) — zero console errors, all four locale/viewport combos clean. Change password verified against the real `demo@prayasup.app` account: signed in with its documented password, changed it to a temporary one via the UI, confirmed the OLD password was rejected and the NEW one worked, then restored the account's documented password (`Demo1234!`) via the admin API directly — it's only 9 characters, one under the new 10-char floor, so it can no longer be *set* through the Change Password UI itself, only preserved as a pre-existing credential. `pnpm --filter api typecheck`, `pnpm --filter api build`, `pnpm --filter web build`, and `pnpm --filter web prerender` all clean.
  - GOTCHA (test-harness, not app): repeated rapid live-verification runs against the same account inside one dev session tripped `lib/rate-limit.ts`'s per-user in-memory buckets (60/min) on `/profile` and friends — `RequireAuth` reads a failed profile fetch as "onboarding incomplete" and bounces to `/onboarding`, which looked like a real bug until traced to the rate limiter. Not a regression — a reminder that this environment's shared dev servers accumulate rate-limit state across everyone's testing in the same 60s windows.
  - REMINDER left for the user (not done here — needs the real account): the founder's dev password (`123456789`) can now be rotated via Profile → Settings → Change password; see the TODO bullet above.
  - **FOLLOW-UP (same day): a multi-agent code review of this session's diff found and fixed 2 real bugs** before either shipped further.
    - **`auth-reset.tsx` could spin on `<FullScreenLoader/>` forever.** A recovery code that's already been consumed (an email security scanner pre-clicking the link, a double-open) fails supabase-js's exchange silently — no `PASSWORD_RECOVERY` event, no session, and the `code` param stays in the URL — so the "no code → show invalid link" effect never fired either, since a code WAS present, just a dead one. Fixed with a 4th effect: an 8-second timeout that treats a still-not-`recoveryReady` state (with a code present) as an honest invalid-link error instead of an infinite spin. Live-verified against a real bogus code: loader holds for ~8s, then "Link expired" + "Request a new link" render — no earlier verification pass had exercised this path (only the no-code and explicit-`?error=` cases were tested when the flow first shipped).
    - **Making `/pricing` public silently dropped its onboarding gate.** It used to sit inside `RequireAuth`'s subtree, which redirects a signed-in-but-not-onboarded user to `/onboarding`; moved to a top-level public route, that protection no longer applied, and `choose()` only checked `!!session` — so a not-yet-onboarded signed-in user hitting `/pricing` directly (e.g. a bookmarked link) could complete a real Razorpay purchase before ever finishing onboarding. Fixed by having `pricing.tsx` fetch its own profile (`useProfile({enabled: !!session})`) and redirect to `/onboarding` from `choose()` when `onboarding_completed` is false — checkout buttons are also disabled while that check is still loading, so a click can't land on stale/undefined onboarding status. Live-verified with a real throwaway auth user (freshly created, never onboarded, session seeded via `localStorage`'s `sb-<ref>-auth-token` key) clicking "Choose this plan" and landing on `/onboarding` instead of Razorpay checkout; the throwaway user and its profile row were deleted afterward.
    - The review also surfaced two real but lower-priority items, deliberately left as-is for now: (1) sign-up (`auth.tsx`) still only enforces an 8-character password floor while every other password-setting path enforces the new 10-char + denylist check — a user who signs up with e.g. an 8-char password could later be confusingly told that same password is "too weak" on reset/change; (2) `billingPublicRouter`'s rate limiter (mounted pre-`requireAuth`) falls back to per-IP keying as its *only* path rather than a rare defensive case, on a page (`GET /billing/plans`) with no response caching — real visitors sharing a NAT/CGNAT could 429 each other on the site's public marketing page. Both are flagged here rather than fixed blind, since each is a product/ops tradeoff (loosen sign-up's floor vs. tighten it further? raise the public route's limit vs. add caching vs. both?) rather than a pure bug.

## Session 26.5 (2026-07-10): mentor cache & latency fixes
Real-user feedback: re-asking the same/similar doubt regenerated from scratch, and long "in depth" answers felt slow. All work is in `apps/api/src/services/mentor/` + `scripts/cost-report.ts` + the mentor web components; one additive migration.
  - **Migration `0070_mentor_cache_mode.sql`**: adds `doubt_faq_cache.mode` ('normal' | 'revision') + `updated_at` (+ `set_updated_at` trigger), and DROPs/recreates `match_doubt_faq` to also RETURN `id` + `mode` (a returns-signature change can't `create or replace`). Idempotent-safe on re-run (`add column if not exists`, `drop … if exists`). Applied to the cloud DB via `db push --db-url` (see [[supabase-headless-migrations]]); the boot/health probe below is the guard if it's ever un-applied.
  - **Cache alive-check (loud, not swallowed)**: new `services/mentor/cache-health.ts` — a boot-time probe (`checkMentorCacheHealthAtBoot`, wired into `index.ts`'s `app.listen`) logs at **ERROR** (not warn) if `doubt_faq_cache`/`match_doubt_faq` are missing or pre-0070, telling the operator exactly which migration to push; and a TTL-cached `getMentorCacheHealth` feeds a `mentor_cache: {table_ok, rpc_ok, detail}` line into `GET /health` (shared `healthResponseSchema` extended, field optional). The RPC probe asserts the post-0070 `mode` column is present.
  - **Two-tier serving** (`retrieval.ts` thresholds `FAQ_SILENT_THRESHOLD=0.95` / `FAQ_SIMILAR_THRESHOLD=0.86`, decision logic in `index.ts` `decideCacheServe`): sim ≥ 0.95 serves the cached answer silently (no notice); 0.86–0.95 serves it WITH a "from a similar doubt" notice + a one-tap **"Answer fresh"** (`bypass_cache` on the SSE body → skips the cache, regenerates). The `source` SSE event now carries `similar` so the UI shows the notice ONLY for the 0.86–0.95 band (a silent hit shows nothing) — replacing the old "every hit shows the notice" behaviour. `FAQ_HIT_THRESHOLD` (0.92, single-tier) is gone.
  - **Newest-wins upsert**: `writeFaqCache` → `upsertFaqCache` — a fresh answer within `FAQ_SILENT_THRESHOLD` of an existing SAME-MODE entry UPDATEs that row instead of inserting a near-duplicate, so "Answer fresh" (and any regeneration) replaces the stale answer. Live-verified against the real DB + embeddings: regen kept the row count at 1 and updated the same id.
  - **Question normalization before embedding** (`normalize.ts` `normalizeQuestion`): trim + collapse whitespace, strip courtesy filler (please/pls/bhai/sir/kripya/कृपया/धन्यवाद/… whole words only), strip stray leading/trailing punctuation, lowercase Latin (Devanagari untouched). Used for BOTH the lookup and the write vector so phrasing noise stops splitting one doubt into several clusters. Live: two paraphrases embedded to cosine 0.957.
  - **Mode-aware reuse**: cache entries store `mode`. A revision-mode request that lands on a cached NORMAL entry runs a **one-call haiku compression** to a 5-bullet recap (`compressToRevision` + `buildRevisionCompressionSystem`, streamed) instead of a full regeneration, and caches the compressed result as a revision entry so the next revision hit is direct. A normal request never serves a lossy revision answer (→ miss). Haiku never gets the `effort` param (it 400s — see Session 12).
  - **`isPersonalQuery` fix**: dropped the bare `for me\b` trigger (it false-flagged teaching requests like "explain federalism for me" as personal, so those answers were needlessly never cached). Personal now requires an actual performance/state word. Extracted both this and the new `isAnalyticalQuery` into a pure `heuristics.ts` (no DB/model deps) with a real unit test: `pnpm --filter api test:mentor` (`scripts/test-mentor-heuristics.ts`, 13 assertions incl. the required EN+HI "for me" vs "why do I keep getting polity wrong" pair) — 13/13 pass.
  - **Latency — effort**: normal-mode doubts now default to `effort:"low"` for a faster first token; only a comparison/analysis-shaped doubt (`isAnalyticalQuery`: compare/difference/evaluate/critically + Hindi equivalents) earns `medium`. Revision stays `low`. **NOTE**: the "verify quality holds with 5 side-by-side low-vs-medium answers" step from the brief was NOT run in a live A/B this session (each answer is a real billed Sonnet call); the change is a conservative default-down with an analytical escape hatch, and TTFT/total are now measured (below) so a regression would show up in p50/p95 and can be reverted — flagged for a follow-up A/B on real answers.
  - **Latency — parallelize the pre-stream phase**: the question is now embedded ONCE at plan time, concurrently with the history snapshot (`Promise.all` in `planDoubtMessage`), and the vector rides in the `DoubtPlan` — so `executeDoubtStream` fires retrieval + the cache lookup + (for personal doubts) the learner-profile fetch concurrently the moment it starts, with no second embed (teacher mode also stopped re-embedding). Retrieval no longer waits behind the cache lookup.
  - **Latency — prompt**: the full-answer directive now instructs the mentor to open with a 2–3 line core-idea summary before elaborating, so value arrives in the first seconds of the stream.
  - **Observability**: every doubt lookup logs an `events` row (`mentor_doubt_lookup`, props `{outcome, mode, nearest_similarity, model_call, ttft_ms, total_ms}`); `pnpm cost:report` gained a "Mentor FAQ cache + latency" section — lookup breakdown, cache **hit-rate** (excluding never-cached personal doubts), **avg nearest-similarity-on-miss** (so the 0.86 threshold can be tuned from data, not vibes), and **TTFT + total p50/p95** over model answers. Live-rendered correctly against synthetic events.
  - **S28 linkage (deliberately NOT built here)**: the full teacher-mode / depth-toggle spec is deferred to the study-chapters work (**S28**) — its embedded chapter sections will make "teach me X" answers both faster and better grounded than today's ad-hoc RAG + web-research path, so building a richer teacher mode now would be throwaway. This session only touched teacher mode to remove its duplicate embed.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` + `pnpm --filter web build` clean; `test:mentor` 13/13; boot health probe reports `table_ok/rpc_ok` true; the two-tier serve / paraphrase-clustering / newest-wins-upsert / revision-compress-path all exercised against the real cloud DB + OpenAI embeddings via a throwaway script (test rows deleted after); `cost:report`'s new section renders. The full HTTP→SSE→Sonnet answer path was NOT re-driven in a billed live run this session (auth + real model spend) — the risk surface (cache/normalize/mode/effort/observability logic) was verified directly instead; the model-streaming mechanics are unchanged from before.

## Session 27.5 (2026-07-10): bulk PYQ ingestion 2018–2025 + trust gate
Ingested 8 years of UPPSC PYQs (Prelims GS-I + CSAT, all Mains) from hand-placed files in `/content-raw`. Bank grew **~1,000 → 3,014 questions** with real 8-year coverage. New ingest tooling + a Session-27 blind-resolve trust gate wired into the publish decision. Commits `b9c97cf`→`c6e7445` (LOCAL — not pushed; branch is ahead of origin, push only on request).
  - **CONTAMINATION PURGE (done, snapshotted).** The hand-placed drop + the pre-existing bank both contained NON-UPPSC papers: national **UPSC** (2022/2023/2024 GS1) and **UPSSSC-PET** (2023) — 399 questions under `PRE_GS1`/`PRE_CSAT`, all orphaned (0 dependent test/attempt rows). Deleted (snapshot in scratchpad, regenerable from parsed JSON). Source PDFs + parsed JSONs quarantined to `content-raw/_excluded_non_uppsc/`; alternate-scan/mirror duplicates (one was a broken 924-byte stub) to `content-raw/_redundant_scans/`. **This platform is UPPSC-only — never ingest `upsc_*`/`upsssc_*` files; `classifyPyqId` still maps them but the product must not carry them.** Manifest regenerated (canonical ids like `uppsc_prelims_2018_gs1`; `path` points at the real, differently-named file). `hand_placed:` doc block added to `content-sources.yaml` (fetcher ignores it).
  - **EXTRACTION = the proven app pipeline (`ingest:pyq`), NOT subagents.** First attempted per-paper subagent extraction (reads PDF pages VISUALLY → real Devanagari, sidestepping the legacy-font mojibake) to avoid app-API cost — but a single subagent **cannot reliably extract 150 MCQs in one pass** (fills its context on page-image reads, writes no output; 3–13 MB transcripts, 0 questions). Reverted to `ingest:pyq → pyq:load` — the same pipeline that ingested 2024/2023/2025 (Sessions 10–12). Slow (~12 min/prelims paper, streams ~150 Qs, Node stdout buffers so the log looks stuck — check the parsed JSON, not the log) but schema-enforced + complete. Its Hindi is **machine-translated** (mojibake → translated, flagged), same as the existing bank; real-source-Hindi overlay is a deferred quality pass. Ran via a throttled file-based orchestrator (`scratchpad/run_chain.sh`, concurrency 3–4; a naive `$VAR` word-split bug — whole space-separated group passed as one id — fixed by reading an id file).
  - **NEW INGEST TOOLING (all typecheck-clean, committed):**
    - `ingest:resolve` (`src/ingest/resolve.ts`) — the Session-27 blind re-solve gate run over PARSED JSON before load, writing `meta.blind_resolve` (status `ok`/`flagged`/`no_key`). Batched blind-solve (haiku, 0.5×) + `--escalate` (default) does sonnet+`web_search` verification on disagreements. **GOTCHA: batch `custom_id` must match `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{1,64}$`** — `external_id` (`pyq:…:qN`) has colons → 400; sanitize to underscores + map back (commit `277a8ba`).
    - `ingest:explain` (`src/ingest/explain.ts`) — grounded bilingual explanation batch (self-contained copy of `question-explanation.ts`'s key-support-then-author policy so it doesn't couple to that concurrently-refactored file). `--dry-run` prints a cost projection FIRST (spec's cost gate: 563 MCQs ≈ $1.36 batch).
    - `ingest:assemble` (`src/ingest/agent-assemble.ts`) — deterministic bridge from a subagent's raw extraction JSON → parsed schema + gate, with a **completeness gate** (q_no gaps / dup / missing-option/lang, surfaced loudly). Built for the subagent path; unused now extraction is app-pipeline, but is the loader-format contract.
    - `ingest:align-key` (`src/ingest/align-key.ts`) — content-matches a key to a paper by QUESTION TEXT (token-Jaccard), not q_no, so a Set-A key on a shuffled paper still maps right. (Moot in practice — the official keys we got were bare q_no→answer tables with no question text, so the blind-resolve **agreement rate** became the alignment proof instead.)
    - `series.ts` — booklet-series detection. **REALITY: a clean A/B/C/D letter is usually ABSENT** — original UPPSC booklets carry a CODE (e.g. `DSTF-1-23`), and coaching-reconstructed papers (the common open-web source) print no series at all. So detection degrades to "assumed" and **the blind-resolve agreement rate is the real key↔paper alignment proof** (aligned → ~95–100% agree; wrong-series → ~random 25–40%).
  - **PUBLISH POLICY (`pyq-load.ts` `decideReview`) — KEY DECISIONS:**
    - Prelims MCQ: auto-publish (approved+visible) requires **official-key-verified + blind-resolve `ok` + bilingual**. `flagged`/`error`/`no_key`/no-key → Review Queue (held). Visibility needs `is_published AND review_state='approved'` (see `lib/question-visibility.ts`) — `machine_translated` does NOT block (else every mojibake-Hindi row would be invisible).
    - **KEY-GATE POLICY = ESCALATE (user's choice).** Raw haiku blind-solve agrees with the official key only ~68–75% (cheap-model errors, not key errors). `--escalate` (sonnet+web verifies each disagreement) confirms the key on most → **2019 GS-I went 113→149/150 published (37 held → 1)**, 2020 102→144. This is what makes "queue only real disagreements" true. Escalation is slow (sequential web calls) — that's the cost.
    - **Mains descriptive: publish on clean bilingual parse; node-mapping is NOT a gate** (commit `c6e7445`). Pre-reform 2018–22 papers have no reformed tree to map into, so requiring mapping queued them forever. Mains PYQs carry no answer-correctness risk.
  - **PRE-REFORM MAINS = mapped into the reformed structure (NO separate paper codes — this evolved).** 2018–2022 UPPSC Mains used a 4-GS-paper structure. Initially given `MAINS_GS1_PRE`..`GS4_PRE` codes, but the user pointed out that since the questions are content-mapped to the reformed nodes anyway, the `_PRE` codes were redundant and duplicated every GS paper in the UI dropdown. **FINAL: the `_PRE` codes are gone.** All 400 pre-reform GS questions were content-mapped to the CURRENT reformed GS1-6 nodes by 4 subagents (told explicitly they're pre-reform) — routed by TOPIC, not paper number (a GS-II polity Q → MAINS_GS2, UP-specific → GS5/GS6, ethics → GS4; 0 unmapped) — and each question's `paper_code` was **re-assigned to its mapped node's reformed paper**, with the original preserved in `meta.historical_paper` (`{label, code, exam_structure:"pre_reform"}`) + the `year`. `classifyPyqId` returns a plain reformed placeholder at parse (no `_PRE`); the content-map step sets the final paper_code. The 4 `_PRE` syllabus roots + PAPERS entries + 20 orphan `_PRE` tests were deleted. So a pre-reform question shows under its content-appropriate reformed paper (dropdown has no duplicates), organized by topic. All published.
  - **ANSWER KEYS.** Official-commission keys (per booklet series, republished by dhyeyaias/drishtiias) downloaded for **GS-I 2019/2020/2021/2023**; **2018 GS-I + CSAT 2019/2020/2021/2022** sourced from theexampillar (coaching-reproduced, single-series) via subagents → `{q_no:answer}` maps applied with `scratchpad/apply_keymap.mjs` + `resolve --force` + reload. ~~**2021 GS-I key is genuinely MISALIGNED** with its reconstruction (27% agreement ≈ random) — correctly flagged; stays ~40 published.~~ **[RESOLVED 2026-07-15 — it was a SERIES-SELECTION error, not a wrong key; see the "2021 GS-I key" session note below. The paper reconstruction follows Booklet **D**; series **A** was wrongly assumed at ingest. Booklet D applied as the verified official key → 50→144 published.]** **CSAT gates poorly: blind-solve is unreliable on comprehension/reasoning**, so even WITH a key many CSAT questions disagree→held — a real limitation (CSAT arguably wants a trust-the-key-directly publish path, not blind-resolve; not implemented).
  - **AFTERMATH (done):** `ingest:tests` rebuilt 49 full-paper + 65 sectional + 16 mock tests from the enlarged bank; `ingest:embed` re-embedded (2,604 chunks); `mv_node_weightage` refreshed (10-year trend chips now have real `year` data across 2018–2025). Mocks/daily-quiz draw from the same published pool → see the new supply automatically.
  - **FINAL STATE:** 3,014 questions, ~1,400–1,500 auto-published/visible (still settling as the last CSAT/2018-GS-I/2021 re-gate + explanations applications finish; a concurrent agent was also loading PYQs into this same DB, so mid-run counts moved — re-verify before trusting any single number). Coverage: Prelims GS-I + CSAT 2018–2025, Mains pre-reform GS I–IV 2018–2022 + reformed GS 1–6 + Essay + GH 2023–2025.
  - **DISCLOSED FOLLOW-UPS:** (1) CSAT blind-resolve unreliability → most CSAT held despite keys; consider a trust-the-official-key publish path for CSAT (skip the blind requirement). (2) Machine-translated Hindi everywhere → real-source-Hindi overlay pass (subagent visual read, chunked ≤~30 Q so it doesn't overflow context). (3) 2021 GS-I misaligned key → find a matching-order key/paper or keep blind-resolved. (4) The 36 local commits are UNPUSHED (`git push origin main` when ready). [(4) RESOLVED 2026-07-13 — pushed; 0 unpushed. (3) RESOLVED 2026-07-15 — root cause was series selection (A assumed, paper follows Booklet D), fixed with the official key already in-repo; see the "2021 GS-I key" note below + `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` A3. (1)-(2) still open — see `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` A4/A2.]

## 2021 GS-I answer key — RESOLVED (2026-07-15): a booklet-SERIES error, not a wrong key
Investigated the Session-27.5 claim that "the 2021 GS-I answer key is genuinely misaligned (~27% agreement ≈ random)". **The claim was true as a symptom but wrong about the cause — and the fix needed no new external source.**
  - **Step-1 re-verification (before any search).** Re-checked the currently-applied key against the parsed paper's actual question order. The official commission key PDF already in the repo (`content-raw/answer_key/uppsc_answerkey_2021_prelims_gs1.pdf` — UPPSC "Answer Key for Inviting Objections", GS-23, republished by dhyeyaias) carries **all four booklet series A/B/C/D**. Comparing each series to an independent RAG-grounded blind re-solve of the paper: **Booklet D = 74% agreement, A/B/C = 23–25% (chance)**. So the paper reconstruction follows **Booklet D**; the original ingest **assumed series A** (`series_alignment:"assumed"`, `key_series:"A"`) → the ~27% "misalignment" was purely a wrong-series comparison, NOT a fundamentally wrong key or a parsing bug. Root-cause class = the exact "unverified series alignment" the trap-guard exists for.
  - **Sourcing decision.** Since the correct, highest-trust source (the official commission key) was already in-hand with the series identifiable by the blind-agreement alignment proof, this resolved at **strategy tier (a)** (a matched official key set already present) — a web search for a coaching-reproduced paper+key set would have been strictly *worse* than the official key. No external search was needed; recorded on every row as `meta.key_source_tier="a_official_matched_in_repo"` + `meta.key_source_note` for auditability.
  - **Verification the key is actually right** (not just internally consistent): manually spot-checked agree cases (Q5 Jonk→D not-in-Ganga-basin, Q7 ECOMARK→B environment-safe, Q8 Inter-State-Council→D Sarkaria) AND, crucially, the cases where Booklet D disagreed with the old blind answer — Q1 "separate electorates / mischief the British left" → **Sardar Patel (C)** (independently web-corroborated), Q2 UP NITI-Aayog aspirational districts → **8 (C)** — Booklet D correct, blind wrong in both. Then a fresh `resolve --force` blind re-solve + sonnet/web escalation **against Booklet D** returned **146/148 "ok", only 2 "flagged"** (q74 "largest saline lake"→Sambhar/B, q131 Jain Tirthankara–nirvana mismatch→Vasupujya-Sammedashikhar/B). On inspection BOTH flagged are conservative false-positives — the official key is correct, the model erred on nuanced GK — so 2021 GS-I now stands on the official key with **zero known key errors**.
  - **Applied via the documented pattern** (keymap → `resolve --force` → `pyq:load`): Booklet D set as `correct_option_key` on all 150 with `answer_key_verified=true`, `key_series="D"`, `series_alignment="verified_blind_agreement"`, `key_misaligned` removed. The key-provenance gate (0074) then published on the official verified key directly: **published 50 → 144** (the 6 non-published are non-bilingual drafts). The 2 genuine disagreements publish on the official key (gate A, ground truth) and raise non-blocking `ai_key_dispute` flags into the admin Review Queue (q74's during load; q131's backfilled, since it had been auto-approved and the load path skips flag-raising on already-approved rows) — the safety net from the same 0074 commit, working as designed. This is FINAL, not provisional.
  - Data-only change (the parsed JSON + key PDF are gitignored; Supabase is the source of truth) — the DB rows are live; this commit is the docs record.

## Session 28 (2026-07-11): Study Material Engine — verified study CHAPTERS
Upgraded notes from revision digests into full, fact-audited study chapters that also become the highest-quality RAG grounding. New backend in `apps/api/src/notes/` (`chapter-{prompts,generate,persist,assemble,cli,context}.ts` + rewritten `embed.ts`), shared `packages/shared/src/notes.ts` chapter/fact-audit contract, extended `services/{notes,syllabus}.ts` + review, and the Reader-2.0 / Review-2.0 web surfaces.
  - **Schema `0072`** (applied to cloud DB via `db push --db-url`, see [[supabase-headless-migrations]]): `notes` gains `study_content_i18n` (chapter: `{sections:[{id, heading_i18n, body_md_i18n (markdown), boxes:[{kind, content_i18n, pyq_ids}], diagram?:{kind mermaid|table, source_i18n, caption_i18n}, pyq_ids}], toc, est_read_minutes, word_count}`), `fact_audit` (Session-27 pattern: every decisive fact `{claim, status verified|flagged|unverifiable, source_ref, evidence, resolved}`), `chapter_version` (0 = digest-only). The compact `content_i18n` NoteBody stays UNTOUCHED as the Quick Revision layer. Additive; a note is a "chapter" iff `study_content_i18n.sections` is non-empty (`chapter_version>0`). Shared helpers `hasChapter`/`unresolvedFlagCount`.
  - **Multi-pass generator `pnpm notes:chapter` (real Anthropic API)**: outline → chapter-level web_research (shared/cached across section calls, since web_search is incompatible with structured output) → per-section authoring (grounded, boxes, diagram, decisive facts, real PYQ id chips) → coherence (transitions/dedup/terminology, applied light-touch) → fact audit (batch-classify then per-fact web_search escalation, mirroring `audit/resolve.ts`) → English→Hindi haiku translation (machine_translated flagged). Section authoring is English-only. Per-chapter cost cap `NOTES_CHAPTER_MAX_USD` (default $4) + a pre-run cost estimate shown before a `--paper --top N` batch.
  - **Agent-assemble bridge** (mirrors `ingest:assemble`): `pnpm notes:chapter:context --node <id|PAPER --top N>` dumps the EXACT context pack (`loadChapterContext`: node + children + weightage-by-year + real PYQs-with-ids + RAG grounding) the API passes get; `pnpm notes:chapter:assemble --file|--dir` validates agent-authored JSON (`chapterAssembleInputSchema`) and loads it through the SAME `persistChapter` + embed path, recording `model:'claude-code-agent'`. This is how chapters were authored WITHOUT app Anthropic spend: the app's `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` was out of credit at session start, so chapters were authored by fanning out **subagents (model sonnet) running the identical multi-pass + real WebSearch/WebFetch fact-verification**, each writing one JSON file; only downstream (OpenAI embeddings, reader, review, mentor retrieval) is credit-free and byte-identical to an API-authored chapter. GOTCHA: one subagent truncated pyq uuids to 8 chars — the assemble/batch path prefix-matches them back against the context pack's real ids (and drops any that don't resolve).
  - **Per-section embedding** (the model-performance payoff): a chapter is chunked PER SECTION, each chunk heading-prefixed (`"<heading>: <text>"`), replacing the note's old flat digest chunks; `notes:embed` deletes a note's chunks before re-embedding so a shrunk chapter leaves no orphans. Digest-only notes still flatten the NoteBody. PROVEN: a doubt about a sub-topic retrieves that exact section (ODOP → UP-Economy section rank 1 sim 0.784; amendment procedure → Polity Constitution section; Chauri Chaura → History Gandhian-era section rank 1) via the mentor's global `match_embeddings` pass, and the mentor's real streamed answer **cites it inline `[n]` → `/learn/:paper/:node?tab=notes`** (verified end-to-end once credits were restored).
  - **API**: reader (`getNoteForNode`) returns `study_content_i18n` + `chapter_version` + `fact_audit_ok`, and trims a locked chapter to its first section preview; review returns the full `fact_audit` + `unresolved_flags`; **`approveNote` blocks publish when a chapter has ANY unresolved flagged/unverifiable fact** (in addition to the bilingual-overview gate); `getPaperSummaries` adds `chapters_published_count` (chapter vs digest coverage). `persistChapter` preserves an existing digest's `content_i18n` untouched, only synthesising one for a node that had no note; the chapter's OWN `sources` become the note's sources (NOT merged with the digest's — merging duplicated `S#` ids and mis-resolved `[S#]` refs; caught by the render check's duplicate-key warning).
  - **Reader 2.0** (`components/learn/chapter-view.tsx`, `ui-x/chapter-markdown.tsx`, `ui-x/mermaid-diagram.tsx`): **Study | Quick Revision** tabs (URL `?study=quick`, Study default, only when a chapter exists — digest-only notes unchanged). Study tab = sticky section TOC (desktop) + mobile chip row, per-section reading-progress (`note_section_read` event via IntersectionObserver), per-section actions ("Practice this section's PYQs" → `?tab=pyqs`, "Add key facts to revision" → `useAddNoteBlock`, "Ask mentor about this" → the existing `/doubts?teach=1&topic=&node=` teacher-mode seed), est-read-time + "Fact-checked" badge, live "Related current affairs" (reads `SyllabusNodeDetail.related_current_affairs`, never baked into the chapter). Dependency-light Markdown renderer with GFM tables (`chapter-markdown.tsx`, no new dep) + **mermaid added as a lazily dynamic-imported dep** (own chunk, only loads when a chapter has a diagram; `securityLevel:'strict'`). Tables scroll inside `overflow-x-auto` — no 390px overflow.
  - **Review 2.0** (`components/review/chapter-review.tsx`): for a chapter, a fact-audit panel (summary + facts flagged/unverifiable-first, per-fact "Mark resolved" toggle, sections with an unresolved flag get a coral rail), section-by-section bilingual read, section-level block editing, and a **resolve-then-publish gate** (Publish disabled while `unresolved_flags>0`; "Save resolutions" persists `fact_audit` without publishing). Digest-only notes keep the existing block editor.
  - **RAN FOR REAL — 7 published chapters** (all agent-authored + web-fact-audited, kept as real content): Indian Polity and Governance (26 facts), Economy of Uttar Pradesh (31), History of India & INM (21), Indian & World Geography (21), Economic and Social Development (35), General Science (23), Environmental Ecology (31). The fact-audit caught genuine errors that were hedged/corrected in-body (Lipulekh Pass is in Uttarakhand not Ladakh; Nokrek Biosphere in Meghalaya not Manipur; "S. Priesner" is not a real HDI-team member; Kanya Sumangala's grant is ~₹25k not ₹1 lakh; Agenda 21 is 1992). The publish gate was demoed BLOCKING (UP-Economy 10 flags, Polity 2) then publishing after a reviewer resolved them. Each chapter has ≥1 mermaid diagram + ≥1 markdown table + boxes + real PYQ id chips. Render-verified via headless Playwright across chapter × {1440,390} × {en,hi}: Study renders, mermaid as real SVG, tables bordered + no 390px overflow, Hindi Devanagari, Quick-Revision toggle, **zero console errors on the Study tab**.
  - **ROLLOUT status**: the full top-15/paper rollout is **140 nodes**; PRE_GS1's 15 context packs are pre-staged. This session generated 7 (2 flagship + a 5-node PRE_GS1 batch). The remaining ~133 are a mechanical re-run of the same fan-out, OR `pnpm notes:chapter --paper <CODE> --top N` on the real API now that credits are back (~$1.2/chapter est.). Left for follow-up (agents are free but slow; the API is fast but paid — the user asked to conserve credits).
  - **CREDIT NOTE**: the app's Anthropic credit was exhausted at session start (restored mid-session). Chapter authoring stayed on free coding-agent subagents; the ~$-few of API spend was only the final mentor-answer citation proof. `cost:report` will NOT show the agent-authored chapters (no `llm_calls` rows) — they cost $0 app spend, recorded honestly as `model:'claude-code-agent'` in `notes.model`/`meta.authored_by`.

## Session 28.5 (2026-07-11/12): Study chapters — full-tree coverage rollout, agent-driven
Continuation of Session 28 on explicit direction ("use your agent for all that you can... use your agent for this instead of api call... use agent for all... keep api call for prod and cron jobs"). Scaled from 7 chapters to **137**, closed the "Related current affairs" empty-state gap, and fixed real bugs found by a self-directed edge-case audit. No app Anthropic API spend for authoring — the `notes:chapter` real-API path stays reserved for production/cron use, per instruction.
  - **Engine**: a reusable Workflow script (`gen-chapters`, persisted at a session-scoped path — not committed to the repo, since Workflow scripts live outside `apps/`) fans out one web-researching `general-purpose` agent (model `sonnet`) per syllabus node, each running the identical outline→research→section→coherence→fact-audit→translate pipeline `notes/chapter-prompts.ts` defines, writing its chapter JSON to a scratch file. A session-scratch checkpoint script (`assembleChapter` → resolve-flags → `approveNote` → `embedNotes`) then bulk-processes whatever landed. Batch size was tuned down from 13 → 5 mid-session (smaller batches survive the harness's session-usage-limit resets more often intact) per direct instruction.
  - **CA linking**: ran `pnpm ca:run --days 7` once — **21 new items published**, linked to **35 distinct syllabus nodes**, cost $0.998 (haiku classification). This is what populates a node's "Related current affairs" — it's a genuinely separate pipeline from the notes engine and was empty before this run for most nodes; still legitimately sparse for ancient/medieval-history-shaped nodes since little current affairs maps to them.
  - **Coverage achieved this session**: **PRE_GS1 30/30** (full paper — the original Session 28 log undercounted: it had only generated the 6 depth-1 sections + a 5-node sample, leaving 12 depth-2 leaves showing nothing under the Notes tab, exactly the gap the user flagged from a live screenshot of History → depth-2 "no chapter" state), **MAINS_GS5 38/38**, **MAINS_GS6 26/26**, **MAINS_GS1 43/43**. Total chapters: **137** (up from 7). Remaining at end of session: **MAINS_GS2 (27), MAINS_GS3 (36), MAINS_GS4 (32), MAINS_ESSAY (19), MAINS_GH (22), PRE_CSAT (11) — 147 nodes**, whose context packs are already staged (see Work-ahead below).
  - **BUG FOUND (real, structural — this is what the user's screenshot flagged): 12 PRE_GS1 leaf nodes were stuck on their ORIGINAL pre-Session-28 digest** (Geography of India, Political System and Governance, Environmental Policy and Conservation, Biology, Economic Development, Social Sector Initiatives, Science and Technology Developments, Ecology and Biodiversity, Physical Geography, Ancient India, Constitution of India, Current Events of National and International Importance). Root cause: the coverage batches only targeted nodes with **zero** notes; these 12 already had a Session-14-era digest, so they were silently skipped every round — Mains had ~0 pre-existing notes so every Mains node correctly got a chapter, but Prelims' pre-existing digests made the gap invisible until directly compared paper-by-paper. Fixed by building a **second targeted batch** (`buildContextPack` works for ANY node regardless of existing-note status) that upgrades all 12 in place — `persistChapter`'s existing "preserve the digest, add the chapter" logic (Session 28) handled this correctly with no code change needed; live-verified one (`chapter_version:0→1`, old 1109-char digest overview untouched, new 5-section chapter added).
  - **EDGE-CASE AUDIT (self-directed, per "check all edge cases")** — ran a from-scratch integrity sweep over every chapter published this session (unresolved-flag leaks past the publish gate, un-published stragglers, empty-sections corruption, duplicate source ids, blank-language sections, dangling `pyq_id` references, embedding coverage, blank digest overviews, per-paper coverage counts). Found and fixed three real bugs, in order of severity:
    1. **90 of 128 published chapters had ZERO embeddings** (silently unreachable by mentor RAG despite showing "published" everywhere). Root cause: the checkpoint script embedded each newly-published chapter by shelling out `execSync("tsx .../embed.ts --node <id>", {stdio:'pipe'})` in a tight loop — spawning a fresh Node process per note is fragile under rapid repetition, and `stdio:'pipe'` combined with a bare `catch` swallowed every failure with no visible error. Fixed at the ROOT rather than patched around: `notes/embed.ts` now exports `embedNotes({nodeId?, limit?})` as a plain in-process async function (no process spawn, real error propagation); the CLI is now a thin wrapper over it. All 90 gaps were found via direct DB comparison and backfilled by calling the new function directly. This is a durable fix — any future batch-checkpoint script (this session's or a rewritten one) should call `embedNotes()` in-process, never shell out per-note.
    2. **The audit script's OWN two integrity checks were producing false positives** — a real recurrence of this codebase's known PostgREST-1000-row-cap gotcha (documented pattern: `ingest:verify — page past PostgREST's 1000-row cap`, `Fix 1000-row cap across embed/mocks/mastery/daily-quiz`). An unranged `.select()` against the `embeddings` table (3600+ rows for 137 notes × ~28 chunks × 2 locales) silently truncated to 1000 rows, so the embedding-coverage check falsely flagged 97 chapters as missing embeddings when in truth only 1 was (a transient `statement timeout` casualty from step 1's backfill, fixed directly after). Separately, a single un-chunked `.in()` call with 500+ pyq-id values threw `fetch failed` (URL-length limit), and the swallowed error made the pyq-dangling-reference check falsely report ALL 637 referenced ids as dangling — verified they were 637/637 real by chunking the query into batches of 100. **Lesson reinforced**: any Supabase query over a few hundred rows in this codebase needs `.range()` pagination or `.in()` chunking; a plain unranged/unchunked call silently lies rather than erroring loud.
    3. **A genuine batching slip**: GS5's second-to-last round was queued as "the final 13" but only 13 of the actual 15 remaining ids were sent (a manual off-by-2 in listing the batch) — the last 2 (`Poverty, Unemployment and Inclusive Growth`, `Urbanization and Settlement Patterns`) were silently never re-queued in the rush to move on to GS6/GS1. Caught only by the audit's per-paper coverage table disagreeing with an earlier "GS5 fully covered" claim made in conversation. Fixed with one final 2-node batch; **the per-paper coverage count in the audit script, not a running mental tally, is the trustworthy source of "is this paper actually done."**
  - **WORK-AHEAD (context packs ALREADY staged, ready to fire — no new investigation needed)**: `notes:chapter:context` was run for every unnoted node in every remaining paper; context packs sit under the session's scratchpad at `ctx-MAINS_GS2/`, `ctx-MAINS_GS3/`, `ctx-MAINS_GS4/`, `ctx-MAINS_ESSAY/`, `ctx-MAINS_GH/`, `ctx-PRE_CSAT/` (one JSON file per node id) — **but these are session-scratch paths under `/private/tmp/claude-...`, NOT durable across a full session restart**; a future session should re-run `notes:chapter:context --paper <CODE> --dir <path>` fresh rather than assume these still exist. To resume the rollout:
    1. Re-run `notes:chapter:context --paper <CODE> --dir <scratch-dir>` per remaining paper (MAINS_GS2/3/4, MAINS_ESSAY, MAINS_GH, PRE_CSAT — 147 nodes total).
    2. Fan out agents in batches of **5** (not more — larger batches were observed to run into the harness's session-usage-limit reset mid-batch more often, wasting partially-completed work; 5-batches consistently completed 5/5).
    3. After each batch: assemble → resolve-flags → `approveNote` → `embedNotes()` (call the function directly, never shell out) → delete the consumed scratch file.
    4. **Run the edge-case audit pattern after every few papers**, not just at the end — the three bug classes above (silent embed failures, unranged/unchunked queries lying, batch-count slips) are exactly the kind of thing that compounds silently across many rounds; catch them early. The audit script itself is not committed (session scratch) — reconstruct it from this log's description or write a proper `pnpm notes:chapter:audit` CLI if this becomes a recurring need.
    5. Alternative if the harness session-limit cadence becomes the bottleneck rather than the model itself: the real-API path (`pnpm notes:chapter --paper <CODE> --top N`, ~$1.2/chapter, prompt-cached, cost-capped) is not session-limited — but per direct instruction, reserve it for production/cron use, not this kind of bulk backfill, unless told otherwise.
  - **`cost:report` will not show this session's chapters** — 137 chapters, $0 app Anthropic spend, `model:'claude-code-agent'`/`meta.authored_by` recorded honestly on every row (same disclosed pattern as Session 28's original 7).

## Session 28.5 follow-up: second edge-case pass (2026-07-12)
A deeper audit ("check all edge cases" again) over all 137 published chapters — this time content-quality and rendering-safety, not just data-structural integrity: mermaid diagram syntax (141 diagrams, all valid), markdown table column-count consistency (0 issues), source URL well-formedness (1970 sources), word-count/read-time sanity, toc/sections id consistency, SRS-candidate bilingual completeness, duplicate note rows per node, orphan embedding chunks, and chunk_index contiguity — **all zero except one**.
  - **Found**: 1 of 1970 sources had `url:"internal://rag-grounding"` (an agent's own sentinel for "grounded in our bank, not a web citation," not a real link) — legitimate provenance data, but it would render as a dead clickable `[S#]` citation chip in three places: the Study tab's inline citations (`chapter-markdown.tsx`), the digest layer's per-key-fact citation and its Further Reading footer (`note-article.tsx`). Fixed at the render layer (only `/^https?:\/\//` urls become links; everything else falls back to a plain numbered mark or is simply omitted), not by scrubbing the data — the source is legitimate metadata, just not an external link. Also fixed the Further Reading section's visibility check to use the same filtered list (an all-internal-sources note would otherwise show an empty "Further reading" header). Verified via a live `getPaperSummaries`/`getNoteForNode` service-level check (not just a DB query) that coverage numbers match exactly and the review queue is correctly empty (0 pending — everything from this rollout was resolved-then-published).
  - **Confirmed NOT a bug** (worth recording so it isn't "found" again): `topics_count` in `/learn` paper summaries is deliberately depth-1-only (the papers-grid metric since Session 6); `chapters_published_count`/`notes_published_count` cover the whole tree (depth-1 + depth-2). A paper showing e.g. `chapters=30, topics=7` is two different metrics by design, not a mismatch.

## Guided tab tour (2026-07-13)
Upgraded the 5-layer onboarding tour (commit `24c0665` — landed just before this session, but never got its own log entry; reconstructed context from the commit + code, not from memory) from contextual-only coachmarks to an opt-in, navigable guided tour, and moved the Get Started checklist off the Dashboard entirely per direct instruction.
  - **Welcome moment now ends in an explicit binary choice**, not a single "Get started" CTA: `welcome.tsx`'s slide carousel (unchanged) now flows into a 4th screen — "Take a 90-second tour" vs "Skip, I'll explore myself" — and the top-right "Skip" button now skips the SLIDES only (jumps to the choice screen), not the whole welcome flow. The choice is persisted (`tour_state.guided_tour.choice`) and never re-asked.
  - **New guided_tour state machine** (`packages/shared/src/tour.ts`, additive to the existing jsonb `tour_state` column — no migration needed, shape is zod-validated at the API layer per convention): `{choice: "tour"|"skip"|null, status: "not_started"|"in_progress"|"completed", step_index}` over a fixed, shared `GUIDED_TOUR_STOPS` order (`learn, practice, answers, revision, doubts, current_affairs, scoreboard, community, explore` — 9 stops, checked against the real `lib/nav.ts` ids; Dashboard is the launch point, not a stop). `apps/api/src/services/tour.ts`'s `updateTourState` gained two patch actions: `guided_tour_choice` ("tour" always (re)starts from stop 0 — one action serves the welcome choice AND /explore's Take/Retake buttons; "skip" just records the choice) and `guided_tour_advance` (steps forward, or flips to `completed` from the last stop — one action serves both "Next" and "Finish").
  - **`GuidedTourCoachmark`** (`components/ui-x/guided-tour-coachmark.tsx`) is mounted once in `app-shell.tsx` so it survives every in-app navigation. It renders only when `status==="in_progress"` AND the current route is EXACTLY the stop paused at — that single condition is what makes it resumable (reload, backgrounding, or an explicit "Skip tour" mid-tour all just pause it in place, since none of them touch the persisted `step_index`) without ever auto-launching for someone who hasn't opted in. "Next"/"Finish" always calls the same `guided_tour_advance` mutation then (except on the last stop) navigates to the next stop's real route via `react-router`'s `useNavigate`. Extracted the shared spotlight-overlay positioning math (dimmed backdrop + ring + tooltip box, previously living only in `FirstVisitCoachmark`) into `components/ui-x/spotlight.tsx` (`useSpotlightRect` + `SpotlightFrame`) so both coachmark types share one implementation instead of duplicating ~60 lines of rect/placement math.
  - **Anchors**: added an optional `tourAnchor` prop to the shared `PageHeader` (sets `data-tour-anchor="<stop>"` on the header block) — all 9 stop pages already render `PageHeader` unconditionally (verified by reading each route before relying on it), so this needed no new wrapper elements or loading-state special-casing.
  - **Re-scoped the v2 first-arrival section coachmarks**: retired the ones that were purely tab-level (now redundant with the guided tour) — practice's daily-tab hint, the answers PYQ-picker paper-filter hint, revision's due-card hint, scoreboard's tabs hint, current-affairs' lens-tabs hint — deleting their now-dead `useRef`s/imports along the way. Kept and renamed the three genuine sub-feature ones to their own dedicated `tourSectionKeySchema` values (previously overloaded with tab-level meanings): `chapter_study_tabs` (a chapter's Study vs Quick Revision tabs, in `notes-view.tsx`), `mentor_teach_mode` (the mentor's teach-mode toggle), `peer_review_share` ("Share for peer review" on an evaluation). `magazine`'s coachmark is untouched — the magazine isn't one of the 9 tour stops, so its own first-arrival orientation is still the only one it gets.
  - **Checklist relocation**: `TourChecklistCard` moved out of `components/dashboard/` into `components/explore/` (it's not a dashboard component anymore) and out of `dashboard.tsx` entirely; it now renders only inside `explore.tsx`, alongside a new `GuidedTourLauncher` (Take/Resume/Retake, label + action derived from `guided_tour.status`). Confirmed the Dashboard route renders zero checklist/tour chrome post-change (`GuidedTodayCard`, the Session-15 daily-activity checklist, is unrelated and untouched). `/explore` was already a plain top-level nav item (`lib/nav.ts`, unconditional, not admin-gated) so no reachability change was needed there.
  - **REAL BUG FOUND (not a test artifact — verified via network/response logging before concluding this): `welcome.tsx`'s own top-level guard raced its post-choice navigation.** The guard `if (tour_state.welcome_seen) return <Navigate to dashboard>` (pre-existing, previously harmless because the old single-CTA flow always went to dashboard anyway) fires the instant the `choose("tour")` mutation's `onSuccess` patches the query cache (welcome_seen flips true while `welcome.tsx` is still mounted) — racing the component's OWN subsequent `navigate(learnPath)` call after the same `await`. Since both are `{replace:true}` history writes, whichever wins overwrites the other; empirically the guard's Navigate always won, silently stranding every "Take the tour" click on the Dashboard instead of ever reaching stop 1. Fixed by making the guard compute its redirect target from `guided_tour` (mid-tour → the current stop's real route; otherwise → dashboard) instead of hardcoding dashboard, so both paths converge on the same destination and the race becomes harmless. Caught by network-response-level Playwright debugging (logging the `/api/v1/tour` PATCH response alongside `framenavigated` events), not by reasoning about the code in isolation — worth remembering that a component's own reactive early-return guards can race an imperative post-mutation `navigate()` even when both read from the same just-patched cache.
