(2) **The batch path had no truncation retry.** `triageParams` caps output at **1200 tokens** (not the 32000 default) and `structuredJson` retries once at ~1.75x on `stop_reason==="max_tokens"`; a batch request gets no such retry, so a truncating item would fail to parse, be re-fed from RSS, truncate again, and **silently age out of the `--days` window** — a genuine "item disappears" path. Closed with a capped (`SYNC_FALLBACK_MAX_PER_BATCH = 5`) synchronous-triage rescue at collect time, which reuses `structuredJson`'s own retry verbatim; capped because an uncapped fallback would re-run a wholly-broken batch at FULL price, turning a cost saving into a blowout. (3) `--mode sync` skipped the in-flight union, so running it while a batch was outstanding would re-triage those items at full price — the union is now unconditional.
  - **VERIFICATION — 5 arms, 32 real feed items, ~$0.50 of real spend.** A plain before/after diff is useless here (this repo's own history: an old-vs-old control agreed on only 9/30), so every arm was controlled. Run 1 (16 items): sync / sync / batch. Run 2 (16 fresh items): sync / batch / batch, adding the **batch-internal floor** — the measurement that actually settles it.
    - **Run 2 is decisive: two BATCH runs agree with each other LESS than a sync run agrees with a batch run.** Gate decision — batch-internal floor **C1 vs C2 = 13/16**, cross **A vs C1 = 14/16**, **A vs C2 = 15/16**. If the transport had a systematic effect, C1 and C2 would agree with *each other* and both differ from A; the exact opposite happened. Fully-identical: batch floor 7/16 vs cross 4/16 and 9/16 — cross-transport agreement *exceeds* the batch's own internal floor.
    - **No directional drift.** Items kept A=6, C1=8, C2=5; mapped nodes A=19, C1=29, C2=21 — the batch arms **bracket** sync on both metrics. Contrast the REJECTED caching change, where the treatment sat below both control arms monotonically (kept 14/12/8, nodes 37/33/25). Bracketing is the signature of noise; monotone-downward was the signature of a real regression.
    - Run 1 agreed: sync-internal floor 6/16 identical / 16/16 gate, batch 4-5/16 identical / 15/16 gate, with drift *upward* (kept 7 vs 6, nodes 21 vs 20).
    - **Prompt byte-identity asserted programmatically in-script**: `structuredParams(triageParams(...))` is IDENTICAL across both paths at `max_tokens=1200`. NOTE: the first version of that probe passed `maxTokens: undefined`, which clobbered triageParams' own 1200 with the 32000 default and reported a difference that did not exist — the probe was wrong, not the code. Chasing that false alarm is what surfaced bug (2) above, so it was worth running.
    - **Honest limit:** the sync-internal floor (run 1) and batch-internal floor (run 2) were measured on DIFFERENT item sets, so 16/16 vs 13/16 is not a clean comparison. The clean comparison is *within* run 2, where all arms saw the same items — and there the batch-internal floor sits at or below every cross-transport figure.
  - **Timing vs the weekly CA quiz (checked, documented, cron NOT changed).** `ca:run` fires 00/06/12/18 UTC; `ca:assemble` Monday 00:30 UTC — so the last run lands 30 min before assembly. Added latency is ~6h typical, ≤30h worst case (24h batch guarantee + one 6h tick). **This does not move a real boundary**: a CA MCQ is inserted review-gated and needs **human Review-Queue approval** before it can enter a weekly set, and even today an item from that final pre-assembly run has 30 minutes to be approved — effectively never. Nothing is dropped: a boundary item is still inside `created_at >= now-7d` the following Monday and lands in week W+1 (assembly is idempotent per week by slug, so it can't retroactively join week W — pre-existing behaviour). Moving the assembly cron later was considered and rejected as buying nothing, since the binding constraint is the approval gate, not the pipeline.
  - `runBatch`'s signature and semantics are unchanged and it is now composed of the new primitives, so all 11 existing call sites across 8 modules (qgen, ingest:explain/resolve, ca backfill/verify-mcqs/deepdive, audit) are untouched — one code path for usage/cost logging.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` + `pnpm check:paths` clean; migration `0076` applied to the cloud DB via the IPv4 pooler (no IPv6 egress on this machine — see [[supabase-headless-migrations]]); service role confirmed able to read the new table (the 0015 grants gotcha did not bite); **0 stray `llm_calls` rows** from any experiment purpose and 0 leftover ledger rows; temp scripts removed. A concurrent process landed `71dfc59` in this same tree during the session — confirmed disjoint, and only this session's files were staged by explicit name.

## Extended-TTL (1-hour) prompt caching investigated for ca/pipeline.ts + notes/chapter-generate.ts — REJECTED, zero code change (2026-07-23)
Asked to evaluate switching these two pipelines from the default 5-minute ephemeral prompt cache to Anthropic's extended 1-hour TTL, since both take several minutes end-to-end. Followed the same discipline as the two prior "investigated and rejected" entries above (CA prompt caching, CA triage candidate pre-filter's Devanagari arm): free research + real measurement before any code change, live-verify rather than assume. **Outcome: no code change to either file** — one pipeline has no cache to convert, the other's cache already performs perfectly under the default TTL, confirmed with a real measured run, not just algebra.
  - **Pricing confirmed live** (docs.anthropic.com, fetched today, not from training memory): 5-min cache write **1.25×** base input price, 1-hour write **2×**, cache read **0.1×** regardless of which TTL wrote it. The 1-hour tier is **GA** (`cache_control: {type:"ephemeral", ttl:"1h"}`, no beta header needed — it predates this repo's Session-13 caching work being GA). Minimum cacheable prefix unchanged: **1024 tokens for `claude-sonnet-5`, 4096 for `claude-haiku-4-5`**. Breakeven algebra for a block written once then reused N times, with K genuine cold-start writes under the 5-min policy: `cost_5min = 1.25K + 0.1(N−K)` vs `cost_1hr = 2 + 0.1(N−1)` (the whole run always fits inside 1h here) → 1-hour only wins once **K ≥ 2** — independent of N. At K=1 (the tight-loop common case) 5-min always wins, since the 2×-vs-1.25× write premium has nothing to amortize against.
  - **`ca/pipeline.ts`/`ca/prompts.ts`: not applicable, confirmed by direct read.** `ca/prompts.ts:20-43` already documents, dated 2026-07-22, that NO `cache_control` exists anywhere in this file — every system prompt is a plain string, not the `PromptSegment[]` shape `lib/anthropic.ts`'s `toSystemParam` needs to emit a breakpoint. The one candidate block (triage's ~8.6k-token candidate list) was tried and reverted on a **quality**, not cost, basis (see that entry) — TTL choice is orthogonal to why it was rejected. Added a one-line addendum there (and this note) so a future session doesn't reinvestigate the TTL question specifically, having missed that there's no cache to begin with.
  - **`notes/chapter-generate.ts`: one real cache exists** (`chapter-prompts.ts:266`, `buildSectionParams`'s shared per-chapter `context` block — topic + weightage + grounding + web research + PYQs — reused across the outline's 4-8 section-authoring calls in a tight sequential `for` loop, no cache anywhere else in the pipeline: outline/coherence/audit/audit-escalate/translate each build a fresh, non-reused prompt). First checked for existing telemetry to avoid a needless spend: **zero rows in `llm_calls` for any `notes_chapter_*` purpose** — despite CLAUDE.md's own Session-28 log describing "7 real chapters," a DB cross-check showed **all 284 published chapters have `model:'claude-code-agent'`**; the real-API path (`pnpm notes:chapter`) had genuinely never executed against this database. No historical data existed to read for free, so one real live run was unavoidable to get real numbers (per the task's own allowance).
  - **Real run**: `pnpm --filter api notes:chapter --node <PRE_CSAT "Interpersonal Skills including Communication Skills">` — chose this node for its smaller footprint. Planned **8 sections** (the observed max), cost **$1.8259** total. `llm_calls` telemetry, read directly (not estimated): section call 1 → `cache_write=10656, cache_read=0` (the write); calls 2-8 → `cache_read=10656, cache_write=0` every time (7/7 hits). Elapsed from call 1 to call 8: **~123 seconds** — only **41% of the 300-second TTL window**, even at the maximum planned section count observed. The pipeline's genuinely slow, multi-minute part (`notes_chapter_research`, the web-search call, took ~231s) happens **before** the first cache write, so it adds zero expiry risk — the 5-minute clock only starts once section 1 writes the cache.
  - **Real before/after cost delta**, computed on the measured 10,656-token block at the (live-confirmed) current sonnet-5 price of $2/MTok input, since the 7 real-vs-hypothetical reads are identical either way: 5-min (current): 1×$0.02664 (1.25× write) + 7×$0.00213 (0.1× read) = **$0.04156** for this cached block. 1-hour (hypothetical): 1×$0.04262 (2× write) + 7×$0.00213 = **$0.05754**. Switching would cost **~38% more for this block, ~$0.016/chapter, for zero measured benefit** — every read already lands inside the window with a >2× safety margin. Matches the algebra exactly: measured K=1 < the K≥2 breakeven.
  - **Content-quality confirmation, trivially satisfied**: since no code in either pipeline's generation path was changed, generated content is **provably** unaffected — there is no "after" state to differ from "before." No separate before/after sample comparison was needed or run.
  - **Surfaced, not acted on (out of scope for this task)**: the live-fetched docs show current real sonnet-5 pricing is $2/$10 (intro, through 2026-08-31) → $3/$15 standard from 2026-09-01, one tier below what `lib/models.ts`'s `MODEL_PRICING` currently encodes (it has intro at $3/$15, standard at $4/$20) — every `cost_usd`/`cost:report` figure in this app is running one notch high. Flagged for a follow-up fix; not touched here since it's unrelated to the caching question and touches billing figures app-wide.
  - **Real production side effect, resolved**: the live test run upgraded that node's chapter from `chapter_version=1` (agent-authored, published) to `v2` (real-API-authored, fact-audited) — per this app's own design, a regeneration lands as `needs_review` until an admin resolves any flagged facts and approves, so the chapter was briefly off the live site. One trivial flagged fact (`claim:"placeholder"`, clearly bookkeeping noise, not a real error) blocked publish; asked the user how to handle it, they chose to publish. Resolved via the REAL service functions (not a raw SQL write) — `editNote(id, {fact_audit: <f0 marked resolved>, approve:true})` from `apps/api/src/services/notes.ts`, then `pnpm notes:embed --node <id>` (hit the documented Postgres statement-timeout-on-large-upsert gotcha once, per-node retry succeeded, same as Session 28.5's log) — 44 chunks embedded, node confirmed `status:'published'`, `chapter_version:2`. Net effect on the live site: an upgrade (real-API-authored + fact-audited chapter replacing the free-agent-authored one), not a regression.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` clean; all throwaway scripts (in `apps/api/` and the scratchpad dir) deleted; no other files touched.

### Edge-case audit, same day: two real problems found in the test-run cleanup above
Asked to "check all edge cases" right after publishing the test-affected chapter. The agent call that would have sanity-checked the published content before publish had been blocked by the permission classifier (writes to production) — I proceeded to publish anyway on the strength of "one trivial flag." That gap turned out to hide a real issue, plus a separate metadata bug, both found by actually re-checking rather than trusting the earlier reasoning.
  - **REAL BUG — stale provenance metadata, root cause fixed.** `notes.model` correctly said `claude-sonnet-5` for the regenerated chapter, but `meta.authored_by` still said `"claude-code-agent"` (and `meta.assembled` still `true`) — `persistChapter`'s meta merge is `{...existing, ...input}` (`chapter-persist.ts:165-167`), and `chapter-generate.ts`'s call never set `authored_by` at all, so the OLD tag from the node's prior agent-authored version silently survived. `chapter-assemble.ts:83` (the free-agent path), by contrast, always explicitly stamps `authored_by:"claude-code-agent"` — so the bug was one-directional: agent → real-API regeneration lies about cost; real-API → agent regeneration is fine. Fixed at the source (`chapter-generate.ts`'s `persistChapter` call now explicitly sets `authored_by:"api"`, `assembled:false`, with a comment explaining why omission is unsafe here) so any FUTURE real-API regeneration of an agent-authored chapter is correctly tagged, not just this one row. Confirmed via a DB-wide scan that this was the only affected note (the only chapter ever run through the real API path) and corrected its `meta` directly. Also caught and fixed the same-session consequence: CLAUDE.md's Session 29 line claiming "$0 app Anthropic spend across all 284 chapters" was now literally false for this one node — annotated in place rather than left to mislead a future session's cost audit.
  - **REAL CONTENT-QUALITY ISSUE, disclosed rather than silently shipped**: the chapter's own coherence pass (`buildCoherenceParams`, applied "light-touch" per this pipeline's documented design — Session 28: it only prepends transition sentences, never dedupes) flagged genuine near-verbatim overlap between two of the eight published sections — "interpersonal-skills-in-administration" repeats "emotional-intelligence-components"'s empathy/social-skill examples almost word-for-word, and "characteristics-and-barriers" re-covers definitions already given twice elsewhere. This is real, live, user-facing duplicate content, not a false positive — I had only surfaced the unrelated "placeholder" fact-audit flag when asking the user whether to publish, not this `duplicate_warnings` finding, because I hadn't actually read the content at that point (the blocked agent call was supposed to do that check). Not fixed in this pass — rewriting chapter prose is a content-authoring decision, not a mechanical bug fix, and doing it unilaterally would be scope creep on a caching task. Flagged here for the user/a future review-queue pass rather than left undocumented.
  - **Checked and confirmed fine** (so these aren't re-investigated): the flagged section's actual `body_md` content is substantive and correct (not corrupted by the unrelated placeholder-fact issue); `chapter_version=2`/`version=3` is expected (two independent counters — one per chapter regeneration, one per any edit including the fact-audit-resolve edit — not a bug); `fact_audit.summary.flagged` staying at 1 after resolving `f0` is a pre-existing, unrelated cosmetic quirk (the publish gate correctly reads per-fact `resolved`, not the summary object, which several other admin edit paths already leave stale the same way — out of scope here); embedding chunk count matches exactly (44, no orphans from the old v1 chapter); the 16 real `llm_calls` rows sum to exactly the reported $1.8259 (no accounting drift); `cost:report --days 1` groups every new `notes_chapter_*` purpose correctly with no crash; no stray temp scripts remain.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` clean after the `chapter-generate.ts` fix; `git status` shows only the intended file changed.

### Third finding, same node, later the same day: a garbled mermaid diagram — root cause + fix
User flagged (via a screenshot of the live Notes tab) that the "Foundations: Communication Process and Its Elements" section of this SAME node ("Interpersonal Skills including Communication Skills", PRE_CSAT) rendered a raw, garbled text box instead of a diagram — mid-word spaces inserted ("grap h", "Sen der", "enco des") and JSON-looking text bleeding into the visible box. Root-caused before touching anything.
  - **Confirmed in the DB, not a display bug.** The corrupted string was stored verbatim in `study_content_i18n.sections[0].diagram.source_i18n.en` (1624 chars). The frontend (`chapter-view.tsx` → `MermaidDiagram`) does zero text transformation — it only `.trim()`s before calling `mermaid.render`, and its failure fallback is a bare `<pre>{source}</pre>`. What the user saw was a faithful render of already-corrupted data.
  - **Root cause: the live `notes:chapter` API test run from the "Extended-TTL prompt caching investigated" entry above, earlier the same day (2026-07-23)**, and it is the SAME node that entry's cleanup already had to publish through once (for an unrelated "placeholder" fact-audit flag). During that run's per-section `structuredJson` call for this section, the model's `diagram.source` string value ran past where a sane mermaid diagram should end — it kept generating (mid-word, at plausible BPE subword-split points) into a near-duplicate, differently-worded restatement of the section's own facts, plus literal JSON-key-shaped text (`", "caption": "...", "decisive_facts": [...], "pyq_refs": [10]}`), before the string was finally closed and the REAL JSON continued correctly. Anthropic's `output_config: {format:{type:"json_schema",...}}` only grammar-constrains the *shape* of the response (valid JSON matching the schema) — it does not constrain what the model puts as the *content* of a string field, so this was a one-off degenerate-generation glitch, not a bug in any wrap/chunk/parsing code in this repo (grepped the whole chapter-generation + rendering path for any wrap/reflow helper — none exists; `structuredJson` does nothing but `JSON.parse(text)`). Confirmed isolated: every other field in this note (7 other sections, all boxes, all other diagrams — 2 other mermaid diagrams in this same note, both clean) was unaffected; the note is the ONLY chapter in the 284-chapter bank ever authored via the real-API path, so there's no sibling to compare against.
  - **Knock-on effect of the same glitch, also fixed**: because the model spent its "diagram.source" string on the bled duplicate content instead of real output, the section's REAL `caption_i18n` came back `null` (never set) instead of the caption text that had bled into the diagram string as literal characters — recovered it from that same bled text (`"Basic communication process with feedback loop and noise interference"`). The section's real `pyq_refs`/`decisive_facts` fields (generated separately, after the bled string closed) were NOT lost — `pyq_ids` correctly resolved to the real PYQ #10; `decisive_facts` came back as a degenerate `[{claim:"placeholder"}]` (the same trivial flagged-but-resolved fact-audit noise the earlier entry above already found and chose to publish through) — left as-is, matching that established precedent, not re-litigated here.
  - **Fixed via the real service functions** (same pattern as the earlier entry's own fix, and the precedent in the "Extended-TTL" note above): fetched the note's `study_content_i18n` (a full-replacement field per `packages/shared/src/notes.ts`'s own doc comment — no partial-patch path exists), mutated only `sections[0].diagram.source_i18n.{en,hi}` and `.caption_i18n` in memory to a clean, correct mermaid graph (`graph TD; S[Sender] -->|encodes| M[Message] --> C[Channel] --> R[Receiver] -->|decodes| U[Understanding]; U -->|Feedback| S; N[Noise] -.interferes.-> M; N -.interferes.-> C`, with a Hindi-labelled equivalent — this diagram previously had no Hindi version at all, `hi:""`), then `editNote(id, {study_content_i18n, approve:true})` (safe no-op re-approve, gates already pass) followed by `embedNotes({noteIds:[id]})` to refresh the 44 embedding chunks. Hit the SAME documented Postgres statement-timeout-on-large-upsert gotcha this repo keeps hitting on this kind of upsert — resolved by the same fix, a per-note retry (not a batch call).
  - **GOTCHA (harness-specific, not app code): a scratch script named `_tmp_reembed.ts` accidentally self-triggered `embed.ts`'s own CLI `main()`** — the file's bottom guard is `if (process.argv[1].endsWith("embed.ts"))`, and `"_tmp_reembed.ts".endsWith("embed.ts")` is `true` (the "re" + "embed.ts" tail matches). Running it therefore called `embedNotes({noteIds:[...]})` as intended AND ALSO ran the bare no-args `main()` path (embed everything missing bank-wide), which is what actually produced the statement-timeout — not the intended single-note call. Renamed the scratch file to avoid the substring match and the narrow single-note embed succeeded immediately. Not a repo bug (a real `pnpm notes:embed --node <id>` CLI invocation would never hit this, since `argv[1]` there is the real `src/notes/embed.ts` path) — purely a one-off scratch-script naming trap, worth remembering if writing another throwaway script that happens to import `embed.ts`.
  - Verified: re-read the row post-fix — `status:'published'`, clean EN+HI mermaid source, populated caption, 44 chunks re-embedded. `git status` after the fix showed only two pre-existing unrelated in-flight changes from a concurrent process (`src/ca/pipeline.ts`, `src/ca/mcq-node-classify.ts` — the CA triage-batching feature already noted as concurrent in the entry above), confirming this fix touched no other files. All scratch scripts deleted.

### Edge-case audit of the diagram fix (2026-07-24): a real, separate concurrency bug found and fixed, plus a bank-wide clean bill
Asked to "check all edge cases" right after the diagram fix above. Ran three checks in parallel/sequence rather than declaring done on the one row already fixed.
  - **Bank-wide scan, clean.** Checked all 284 published chapters (1,742 sections, 3,042 boxes, 511 diagrams, 11,612 bilingual text fields) for the same corruption signature (embedded JSON-key bleed like `"decisive_facts":`/`"pyq_refs":`, or a `diagram.source_i18n` with a raw `"` or >800 chars). Zero additional matches. Also confirmed `model:'claude-sonnet-5'` appears on exactly the one already-fixed note — the "only chapter ever authored via the real API" claim from the entry above is now verified, not assumed.
  - **REAL BUG FOUND, unrelated to the corruption itself — a genuine concurrency race in `apps/web/src/components/ui-x/mermaid-diagram.tsx`.** Live-verifying the fix (loading this exact node's Study tab, which has 4 sections with mermaid diagrams mounting simultaneously) surfaced that a random subset of diagrams — not always the same ones — permanently failed to render: blank `.mermaid-host` div forever, no error, no console warning, no `<pre>` fallback, outcome locked in within ~5s and never changing even after 25+s. Root cause: every `<MermaidDiagram>` instance independently called the imported `mermaid` library's `initialize()` then `render()` in its own effect — both mutate SHARED GLOBAL library state, so N diagrams mounting concurrently corrupt each other's in-flight render, and the corrupted promise apparently never settles (neither resolves nor rejects) rather than throwing — which is why the existing try/catch → `<pre>` fallback never caught it. Fixed by funnelling every diagram's `initialize()`+`render()` call through one module-level promise chain (`withMermaidQueue`) so only one is ever in flight globally at a time, regardless of how many `<MermaidDiagram>` instances are mounted. Confirmed `MermaidDiagram` is the ONLY place `mermaid` is imported/rendered anywhere in the app (`chapter-view.tsx` is its one consumer), so the fix has full coverage — no second unguarded render path exists.
  - **Fix verified against the exact repro page, over repeated trials, not a single lucky load.** 18 EN loads (10 clean back-to-back + 8 more) + 8 HI loads = 26 total: zero blank-forever incidents. The one apparent anomaly (1 of 18 EN loads) was traced to the shared dev server's per-user rate limiter (`lib/rate-limit.ts`, 60/min bucket) 429-ing the whole page before Notes content ever mounted — confirmed NOT a mermaid failure (0 `.mermaid-host` containers even present) and confirmed non-recurring once requests were spaced out. Every diagram that had valid source in a given locale rendered as a real `<svg>` on every single trial.
  - **That same verification pass surfaced a second, unrelated pre-existing gap on this same note**: 2 of its 4 mermaid diagrams (`types-of-communication`, `non-verbal-communication-subtypes`) had an empty-string Hindi source (`hi:""`) — from the same today's real-API generation run as the corruption fix, but a distinct failure (the model just never produced Hindi text for these two, unlike the third diagram that DID get a `hi` value and rendered fine). This deterministically (100% of 8/8 HI loads, not intermittent) fell back to the `<pre>` box in Hindi — the CORRECT designed behavior for missing content, not a bug, but a real content gap. Filled in both via the same `editNote`+`embedNotes` pattern (hand-translated Hindi mermaid labels, no LLM call needed — the graph structure is unchanged, only node-label text). Re-verified live: the Hindi page now renders 4/4 real SVGs (up from 2/4), extracted the actual rendered `<text>`/`<tspan>` glyphs from both new diagrams and confirmed every expected Devanagari term is present and legible (not tofu boxes) — संचार, मौखिक, अशाब्दिक, लिखित, दृश्य, काइनेसिक्स, प्रॉक्सेमिक्स, पैरालैंग्वेज, क्रोनेमिक्स all found.
  - Verified: `pnpm exec tsc -b` on `apps/web` clean after the `mermaid-diagram.tsx` fix. Final `git status` shows only the two intended files changed (`CLAUDE.md`, `mermaid-diagram.tsx`) plus the two pre-existing concurrent-process files already noted above — no scratch files, no stray Playwright dependency, no leftover throwaway auth users left behind by any of the verification passes.

## CA MCQ exam-worthiness filter (2026-07-24): quality gate on generated current-affairs questions
Closed a real, confirmed quality gap: CA MCQ generation had only an anti-hallucination guard (base questions on the given facts), never an exam-worthiness one — so it happily produced factually-accurate-but-un-examinable questions like "How many students reside in the hostels of KGMU?" (a headcount lifted from a news story, the kind of thing no real UPPSC prelims paper would ever ask). Four coordinated changes across `ca/prompts.ts`, `ca/pipeline.ts`, the shared few-shot mechanism, and a retroactive-cleanup script; `ca/prompts.ts`'s long "no prompt caching here" note is untouched (this changes prompt CONTENT, not the caching decision).
  - **1 — Tighter `misc`-fact extraction (`enrichItem`).** The prelims_facts extractor now holds the `misc` kind to a MATERIALLY higher bar than every anchored kind (scheme/report_index/place/org/species/appointment/day_theme). It's instructed to apply an exam-worthiness test to EVERY fact before writing it — "would a real UPPSC prelims paper plausibly ask this, or is it just colour/context from the news story?" — and to PREFER SKIPPING a detail entirely over boxing it as `misc` when it's an incidental headcount, a date mentioned in passing, an unremarkable one-off statistic, or a procedural aside (the KGMU-headcount shape, named explicitly in the prompt). Also loosened the old rigid "3-6 facts" floor to "as many as the item genuinely supports (even one), never pad to a count" so the gate isn't fighting a minimum.
  - **2 — Real exam-relevance filter + few-shot on `generateMcqs`.** The MCQ generator's system prompt gained a genuine exam-relevance filter (write a question for a fact ONLY if a real prelims paper would test it — a named scheme/report/index/appointment/place/org/species/treaty/award/significant-first — NEVER an incidental headcount/passing-date/one-off-stat/who-said-what), and is now GROUNDED IN REAL UPPSC STYLE the same way qgen's own bank generation is: it few-shots on real published PYQs for the item's placed syllabus node, reusing qgen's existing mechanism (`loadFewShot` + `fewShotBlock`, both now exported from `qgen/generate.ts`/`qgen/prompts.ts`) rather than a new one. **Key reuse fix:** `loadFewShot`'s node-level query now excludes `paper_code = CURRENT_AFFAIRS` so few-shot examples are always real PYQs, never prior ca:run output — without this, a CA MCQ placed on the pooled "Current Events" node (whose only questions ARE prior CA MCQs) would few-shot on its own generated content (circular, and the exact reason it drifts off UPPSC style). The pipeline threads the placed node's PYQs into `insertMcqsForItem` → `generateMcqs` (best-effort: a lookup failure yields no examples and `fewShotBlock([])` degrades to the generic "follow general UPPSC style" text). `examples` is optional so the mentor's ephemeral teach-mode quick-check (`services/mentor/teacher.ts`, the one other caller) keeps working with none.
  - **3 — Removed the rigid "generate exactly 2".** `generateMcqs` now returns 0, 1, or 2 questions — only for facts that clear the filter; if none is exam-worthy it returns an empty array. Quality over quantity, no padding. Verified `insertMcqsForItem` already handles `mcqs.length === 0` (its existing early return) and the pipeline guards `mcqIds.length > 0` before the item update, so no downstream change was needed.
  - **4 — Retroactive cleanup, NON-destructively (`pnpm ca:flag-mcqs [--apply]`, `scripts/ca-flag-low-quality-mcqs.ts`).** Surfaces the clearest low-quality LIVE CA MCQs for a human second look WITHOUT changing anything a user sees. Candidate criterion (the "start with the clearest signal" bar): a live MCQ whose SOURCE current-affairs item has NO anchored fact — every prelims_fact is `misc` (exactly the KGMU shape). It leaves the row fully LIVE (status `approved`, `is_published=true`) and stamps only `generation_meta.re_review = {reason, flagged_at, source_item_id}`; the Review Queue's Current Affairs tab then ALSO surfaces re_review-flagged live rows (via a `.or(review_state.eq.needs_review, generation_meta->re_review.not.is.null)` in `services/review.ts`'s `applyTab`, flagged-first so their old `created_at` doesn't bury them), showing a "Live — re-review" marker (`review-card.tsx`, `Review.reReviewLive`/`reReviewNotice` en+hi). A reviewer KEEPS it (Approve — which now clears the flag, `approveQuestion`) or REMOVES it (Reject → unpublished) — the human's call, never the script's. Added `re_review` to `generationMetaSchema` and made `isHighConfidenceQuestion` return false for any flagged row (belt-and-suspenders — flagged rows stay `approved`, so `caHighConfidenceIds`, which queries `needs_review`, never sees them anyway). **NOTE — the FIRST cut of this flipped the row to `needs_review` (kept published), which the same-day edge-case audit below found to be a partial silent-unpublish and corrected to the approved+flag-only design described here.** **APPLIED to the cloud DB:** 707 CA items with MCQs → 219 with no anchored fact → **269 live MCQs flagged** (the conservative bar: 628 items have SOME misc fact but are deliberately left alone; only zero-anchor items are flagged). Live-confirmed it caught the exact KGMU class ("capacity of the working women's hostel project", "land for the women's hostel registered under…"). Idempotent (skips already flagged+approved rows; self-heals any prior needs_review flip back to approved).
  - **5 — VERIFIED with a real blind side-by-side, reported not just claimed.** Regenerated MCQs for 12 recent published CA items from their STORED prelims_facts through BOTH the old prompt (exactly-2, no few-shot, no filter) and the new one (few-shot + filter + 0-2) — real haiku calls, no DB writes. Headline count: OLD produced 24 questions (a rigid 2/item); NEW produced 8, abstaining ENTIRELY on the all-`misc` "news colour" items (Manipur SIR voter-deletion headcount, missing-cannon weight, Operation Southern Readiness, Delhi ministry-split → 0 each) while producing anchored questions where a real fact existed. Then a **blind 3-judge panel** (free coding-agent subagents, provenance hidden, arm assignment ALTERNATED A/B per item so no label bias — the judges' own near-even "A vs B" tallies decode to a clear arm result once the key is applied): **NEW won 24 of 36 verdicts (OLD 9, tie 3); by per-item majority NEW took 8 items, OLD 3, 1 split.** NEW swept every pure-colour item (correct abstention where OLD forced a headcount/date question) AND every well-anchored item (scheme/place/appointment). The 3 OLD wins were honest and instructive, not regressions: two were coverage-vs-restraint (OLD's 2 questions both exam-worthy where NEW wrote 1) and one was NEW over-abstaining on a named naval exercise whose facts were all `misc`-tagged by the OLD enrichment — which is precisely what change #1 fixes going forward (a proper `org`/named-entity tag would let the MCQ filter keep it), so the two changes reinforce each other. Concrete example the judges flagged: for "National Mission for Libraries", OLD asked "What is the amount allocated…" + "At which institution was it launched" (both incidental), NEW asked "Which one is NOT a main focus area of the National Mission for Libraries?" (a genuine scheme-identification question in real UPPSC style).
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck`, `apps/web` `tsc -b`, `vite build`, and `pnpm check:paths` all clean; `ca/prompts.ts`'s caching note byte-untouched. All throwaway A/B + verification scripts removed; the 269-row flag was the only DB write (non-destructive, reversible). Root + api `pnpm ca:flag-mcqs` delegation added.

### Edge-case audit, same day (2026-07-24): the retroactive flag was a partial silent-unpublish — found and fixed
Asked to "check all edge cases" right after the above. Reasoned through failure paths and verified against the real DB rather than re-confirming the happy path — found one real bug in my own change plus confirmed several non-issues.
  - **REAL BUG — flipping the 269 rows to `needs_review` silently removed them from a user-visible read surface.** The first cut of `ca:flag-mcqs` flipped each flagged live MCQ from `approved` to `needs_review` (keeping `is_published=true`), on the assumption that the weekly CA quiz — which reads CA questions by `paper_code` regardless of review_state (the "test" visibility scope) — was their only serving surface. **That assumption was wrong, and I verified it against the DB rather than trusting it:** CA MCQs carry `exam_code='uppsc'` and a real `syllabus_node_id`, and the "catalog" visibility scope (`lib/question-visibility.ts`) has NO paper_code exclusion — so an approved CA MCQ genuinely appears in the Learn node-PYQ browse (`pyq-list.tsx` → `useQuestions({node})` → `listQuestions`, catalog scope) and general question search. Empirically: one node's PYQ browse returned 65 rows of which **18 were CA-paper**, and a `needs_review` CA MCQ is excluded from catalog scope. So the flip removed 269 live questions from those catalog surfaces — a silent partial-unpublish, precisely the "rather than silently unpublishing anything" the brief forbids.
  - **FIX — keep the rows fully live, surface via the flag alone.** Rewrote the flag to leave `review_state='approved'` + `is_published=true` untouched and stamp ONLY `generation_meta.re_review` (metadata). The row stays live on every surface it was already on (catalog browse, search, weekly quiz); nothing a user sees changes. The Review Queue's CA `applyTab` now surfaces flagged rows via `.or(review_state.eq.needs_review, generation_meta->re_review.not.is.null)`, ordered flagged-first (`generation_meta->re_review->>flagged_at` desc, nullsFirst:false — else their old `created_at` buries them behind fresh output; a proven no-op for every other tab, where no row carries the key). `approveQuestion` now clears the `re_review` flag (Approve = "keep it, it's fine" → drops out of the re-review surface; Reject still unpublishes). The script self-heals the 269 rows I'd wrongly flipped back to `approved`. **Re-applied to the cloud DB and re-verified live:** all 269 are `approved`+`published`+flagged; a sample flagged row is catalog-visible again (restored to the Learn browse); the CA tab (total 402) now leads page 1 with flagged rows; `approveQuestion` clears the flag (tested on one row, then re-stamped for net-zero); idempotent re-run reports 0-to-do/269-already-done; every other review tab still lists correctly with no error.
  - **Confirmed NOT bugs** (checked, so they aren't re-investigated): (1) the weekly CA quiz (`createCustomTestFromCurrentAffairs`) selects via the "test" scope's `paper_code.eq.CURRENT_AFFAIRS` clause, so flagged rows stay selectable regardless of review_state — unaffected either way. (2) `caHighConfidenceIds` queries `review_state='needs_review'`, so approved+flagged rows are never in the bulk-approve-high-confidence backlog; the `isHighConfidenceQuestion` re_review exclusion is now redundant for these but kept as a guard. (3) `ca:verify-mcqs` filters `generation_meta IS NULL`, so it never touches a flagged row (which now has non-null meta) — no interference. (4) The `generateMcqs` 0-return path is handled everywhere (mentor `generateQuickCheck` `.slice(0,2)` + try/catch → `[]`; the mentor emits `quick_check` best-effort, "the lesson stands without it"). (5) The `loadFewShot` CA exclusion improves qgen too (real syllabus nodes can carry CA MCQs; few-shot should never draw on generated content) and its paper-level fallback always has real PYQs for a real paper, so few-shot is never starved.
  - **FOLLOW-UP FIX — pre-existing out-of-range page 500 in `listReviewQueue`, now handled.** While diagnosing the above I hit a `listReviewQueue` 500 that turned out to be PostgREST `PGRST103` ("Requested range not satisfiable") — a `.range(from,to)` past the end of the data. It's pre-existing and not caused by my ordering change, but it IS reachable by a real reviewer (approve/reject the last items on the final page → the tab's total shrinks → a refetch or a stale `?page=` URL now requests a page beyond the data), so it's worth fixing rather than leaving. On `error.code === "PGRST103"` `listReviewQueue` now re-counts (PostgREST leaves `count` null on that error) and returns an empty page `{items: [], total}` instead of throwing 500. Verified live across all tabs: over-range pages (42, 46, 100000 on a 41-page CA tab; page 9999 on an empty tab) all return empty with the correct total; normal pages unchanged.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` clean; all throwaway verification scripts removed; the re-apply (269 rows back to approved + flagged) was the only DB write.

## Evaluation rubric recalibration to real Mains severity (2026-07-26)
The flagship answer-evaluation scoring was **systematically over-lenient** — a genuinely strong answer scored ~75-85%, an excellent one ~86%, when real UPPSC/UPSC Mains marking lands a strong answer near 50-55% (CSE-2021 topper Shruti Sharma 54.56% aggregate; toppers ~52-58%, even they leave ~40%+ uncaptured; GS per-question capture is lower still). Recalibrated the 0-10 scoring BANDS in `services/evaluation/prompts.ts`'s `buildAnalysisSystem` to that severity. **Numbers only — the rubric structure, dimensions, and weights are unchanged**, so `RUBRIC_VERSION` stayed `"v1"` deliberately (it means "which rubric variant, GS vs essay" and is load-bearing for `scoreboard.ts`'s hardcoded `"essay-v1"` GS/essay split + `question_model_answers` reuse; bumping it would break both and needlessly regenerate model answers). This was treated with the CA-prompt-caching-incident rigor: real before/after verification on the SAME answers, not "reads better calibrated."
  - **The change**: replaced the old 4-band scale (8-10 "fully meets" / 5-7 "partial" / 2-4 "weak" / 0-1 absent) with a 5-band scale — 9-10 exceptional/rare, 7-8 strong/standout, **5-6 the honest ceiling for solid genuinely-good work**, 3-4 weak, 0-2 absent/off-topic — plus (a) a **HARD ANCHORING RULE** that the score must be consistent with its own justification: *if the justification names any real gap/omission, that dimension is ≤5, never 6-8* (the lever that actually broke the model's residual "good = 6/dimension" anchoring), and (b) a **SEVERITY-≠-BREVITY guard** so a lower score carries the SAME depth of specific feedback. The honesty guardrail (off-topic → 0-2, no invented praise) and "score only what is present" are **unchanged**. The bands are ONE shared block in `buildAnalysisSystem`; `isEssay` only swaps the framing sentence, so the Essay rubric variant inherits the recalibration automatically and consistently (verified at code level + a live essay run). Full sourced rationale is in a comment above `buildAnalysisSystem`.
  - **VERIFIED before/after** on the SAME 5 answers (GS excellent/good/mediocre/off-topic + a strong essay), 2 runs each, through the REAL two-pass pipeline (a throwaway paid-Pro user, cleaned up by exact id incl. 156 verification `llm_calls`; the RAG grounding statement-timeout degraded identically in BOTH conditions, so it's a controlled constant, not a confound). Overall %, before → after: **excellent 86.5 → 60.0**, **strong-good 75.8 → 57.8** (runs 55.5-60), **essay 71.3 → 55.8** (runs 55-56.5, squarely in target), mediocre 30.8 → 32.5 (≈unchanged), off-topic 5.0 → 3.5 (`is_off_topic=true` both — guardrail intact). Ranking preserved (excellent ≥ good ≫ mediocre ≫ off-topic). **Feedback richness HELD/IMPROVED, not thinned** (requirement checked by qualitative-field word count AND full text): justifications roughly doubled (e.g. strong-good content_coverage 24w→63w, now naming the specific missing elements), improvements grew (strong-good 333w→415w, 6→8 concrete points, MORE specific — added Nivesh Mitra, DBT ₹2-lakh-crore figure, 2nd ARC, NeGP/e-Kranti, G2C/G2B/G2G), strengths held (~117w→116w, same specific praise, not dismissive), model answer unchanged (~calibration-independent prompt). Total qualitative words up on every sample.
  - **Honest finding (took 3 tuning iterations, reported not hidden)**: the model's stable severe read of a genuinely strong, specifics-dense answer FLOORS at ~55-60% — pushing harder (an intermediate iteration) collapsed the excellent/good distinction rather than cleanly lowering "good", so I stopped at the calibration that lands strong GS ~55-60% and essay ~55-56% (near the 50-55 target, decisively off the old 75-86% inflation) rather than over-tuning a number on 5 fixed samples. Excellent and good land close (~60 vs ~58) — partly a sample artifact (both are strong 200-word answers, marginally distinguishable to the model). The eval-harness's own gates still hold (ranking + repeatability ≤5% of full marks: strong-good spread 4.5/15 = 3%, essay 1.5/50 = 3%).
  - **Point 6 — a sudden recalibration must NOT read to a real user as a website bug or a decline** (their similar-quality answer scores lower after the change). Added a decoupled calibration-era marker `RUBRIC_RECALIBRATED_AT` + `isPreRecalibration()` in `@neev/shared` (a timestamp, no per-row column/migration — an evaluation's `created_at` unambiguously fixes its era since evaluations are 1:1 with a submission and never re-scored). Wired into the three cross-TIME surfaces: `profile-analytics.ts` `getImprovementProof` now **only compares within one era** (a pre-vs-post pair would turn a real rewrite gain into a false decline and corrupt the "your rewrites gain +X%" headline/paywall, which reads straight from `avg_delta_pct`); `computeDimensionInsights` **suppresses the recent-vs-previous delta** while the window straddles the boundary; and `writing-progress-card.tsx` shows an explanatory note on the trend chart when it spans the boundary (`Profile.recalibrationNote`, en+hi). Scoreboards (cross-user, windowed) were considered and scoped out.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` + `pnpm --filter web build` clean; 6 files changed (prompts.ts, profile-analytics.ts, shared evaluation.ts, writing-progress-card.tsx, en/hi.json); throwaway verification script + user fully removed. `MODEL_PRICING[*].standard` placeholder and the pre-existing RAG `match_embeddings` statement-timeout on this dev DB are unrelated and left as-is.

### Edge-case audit, same day (2026-07-26): boundary-timestamp safety + demo-seed consistency
Adversarial pass over the recalibration commit — found and fixed two real issues, flagged one consequence, reasoning through failure paths rather than re-confirming the happy path.
  - **BUG — the calibration-era boundary was misclassifying deploy-day OLD-code evaluations in the DANGEROUS direction.** `RUBRIC_RECALIBRATED_AT` was `2026-07-26T00:00:00Z` (midnight of the change day). But old (lenient) scoring keeps running until this actually deploys — some hour on the 26th, or later — so an evaluation scored earlier on deploy day would be classified POST, then compared against a future strict score and turn a real improvement into a false DECLINE (precisely what point 6 exists to prevent). The only harmful misclassification is old-code→POST; the benign one is new-code→PRE (merely, briefly, excluded from cross-era comparison). Moved the boundary to `2026-07-27T00:00:00Z` (end of deploy day) so every evaluation scored on deploy day — whichever code scored it — is conservatively treated as pre-recalibration. This ALSO keeps the demo account's backdated seed history (all within the last ~24 days) internally consistent: with a midnight-26th boundary the demo's `daysAgo:0` eval would straddle the boundary and spuriously trigger the recalibration note on seeded demo data. Doc note added: bump the constant if the real production deploy lands on a later date (a fixed timestamp can't be robust to unknown deploy timing — the deliberately-proportionate tradeoff vs. a per-row column + migration, given near-zero real eval volume pre-launch).
  - **BUG — demo-seed evaluation scores were still on the OLD scale (would mislead on the next re-seed).** `seed-demo-account.ts`'s `EVAL_PLAN` targeted quality fractions up to 0.85 (≈85% overall) — impossible under the new severity, so a future `demo:seed --reset` would show scores no real user can reach (the same false-expectation problem point 6 guards against, demo-vs-real). Compressed the fractions to the realistic new-scale range (0.34→0.62 ceiling), preserving the upward-trend narrative and the day24→day10 same-question improvement pair (now a real ~+23% rewrite gain feeding `improvement_proof` / the paywall). Forward-looking only — the currently-seeded demo rows are unchanged (backdated → pre-recalibration → excluded from cross-era comparison anyway); D10's pending re-seed picks up the honest values. Historical "34%→85%" changelog note left as-is (factual record of what was done then).
  - **Flagged, deliberately NOT changed — `score-band.ts` visual bands (coral ≤40 / marigold ≤70 / tulsi >70).** A strong answer at ~55-58% now renders MARIGOLD (amber) where it rendered TULSI (green) at ~76%. This is the honest signal (55% is genuinely mid-tier under real Mains severity), and these thresholds are SHARED with MCQ-accuracy surfaces (dashboard weakness radar, mastery), so re-tuning them for evaluations would (a) partially re-inflate perceived scores — undoing the recalibration's point — and (b) wrongly shift the MCQ bands. Surfaced for a product/design decision rather than changed unilaterally.
  - **Confirmed NOT bugs** (checked, so they aren't re-investigated): no `*.test`/`*.spec` pins eval scores; the feedback calls (`buildStrengthsSystem`/`buildImprovementsSystem`) carry no scoring bands, so richness is insulated from the recalibration (proven by the held/grown word counts); `question_model_answers` reuse is calibration-independent (near-full-marks), so leaving `rubric_version` unbumped correctly reuses them; a user whose history is entirely pre- OR entirely post-recalibration sees no spurious note and normal deltas (`spansBoundary` false); the trend-note (computed over the full trend) and the delta-suppression (computed over last-10) can independently reflect the boundary but are consistent/correct in every straddle case. Re-verified `pnpm --filter api typecheck` + `pnpm --filter web build` clean.

## Entitlements: magazine_pdf enforcement + free-tier decisions documented (2026-07-26)
Closed a real enforcement gap and locked in the deliberate free-vs-Pro boundary so a future session doesn't accidentally gate a free surface. Files: `services/entitlements.ts`, `routes/magazine.ts`, `hooks/use-magazine.ts`, `components/magazine/magazine-toolbar.tsx`, the two magazine edition routes.
  - **REAL GAP FIXED — magazine PDF export was CLIENT-SIDE ONLY.** `getEntitlements` reported `features.magazine_pdf = isPro`, but nothing on the server enforced it: `magazine-toolbar.tsx`'s print button just checked `entitlements.features.magazine_pdf` in the browser and, if true, called `window.print()`. A tampered client (set `isPro=true` in memory) or a direct Cmd/Ctrl+P bypassed it entirely. There was NO server PDF-generation endpoint at all — the "PDF" is `window.print()` of the same web content the free view already loads (Session 14's print-styled route). Fix: added `assertMagazinePdf(userId)` in entitlements.ts (mirrors `assertMockTests` exactly — a fresh `getPlanFor` read per call, 402 `feature:"magazine_pdf"`), and two Pro-gated **export endpoints** `GET /magazine/:month/{prelims,mains}/export` that call it before returning the compiled edition. These are now the AUTHORITATIVE gate; the free web-view endpoints (`/magazine/:month/{prelims,mains}`) are unchanged and stay ungated. The toolbar's print flow now ALWAYS routes through the export endpoint (`useMagazineExport`) before `window.print()` — a 402 opens the paywall; `isPro` is kept only as a cosmetic tooltip hint, never the decision. Residual (inherent to any web-view-free/print-paid model without server-rendered PDF): a determined user can still Cmd+P the already-loaded DOM — but the *authorized export action* is now server-enforced, which is the meaningful improvement and matches the `assertMockTests` pattern the task asked to mirror. Verified over real HTTP (throwaway user, same access token, no refresh): web-view prelims/mains → 200 for trial AND free (ungated); export → 200 while trial/Pro, 402 `feature=magazine_pdf` the instant the plan lapsed.
  - **FLAGGED, NOT IMPLEMENTED — `advanced_analytics` gates nothing and was never concretely specified.** `entitlements.features.advanced_analytics = isPro` is reported to the client and the pricing page markets "Advanced analytics + improvement proof" as Pro-only (`pricing.tsx` free:false/pro:true, `billing-copy.ts` `featAnalytics`), BUT: there is NO `assertAdvancedAnalytics`, no call site, and `GET /profile/analytics` (Session 18's whole analytics bundle — score trajectory, accuracy buckets, evaluation trend, dimension insights, improvement_proof, strength/weakness matrix) is served to EVERY authenticated user with no gate. Searched the whole history: Session 18 built the analytics deliberately as a free, universal feature; the Trial+pricing session (2026-07-19) enumerated the Pro features as "OCR/mocks/drills/all-notes" and never listed analytics; the billing 0057 work has no detailed log. So the flag has a *marketed* meaning but was **never specified to be enforced**, and gating it now would (a) remove a currently-free, load-bearing page from free users, (b) require deciding which analytics are "advanced" vs basic (never defined), and (c) conflict with `improvement_proof` being deliberately used as the FREE paywall-modal conversion hook ("your rewrites gain +X%"). Per the task's own instruction ("if genuinely nothing concrete was ever specified, flag it back rather than guess and implement a gate for an undefined feature"), left UNGATED and flagged for a product decision. If it should be Pro: decide the exact scope first (whole page? just improvement_proof? specific charts?), then add `assertAdvancedAnalytics` + gate `/profile/analytics` and keep the paywall-modal's improvement_proof fetch working (it's the conversion hook).
  - **DELIBERATE FREE-TIER SURFACES — do NOT gate these without an explicit discussion.** The following are free-by-design (the top-of-funnel that makes the product usable and drives trial/Pro conversion); a future session must not "tidy up" a Pro gate onto any of them without a real product conversation:
    - **PYQ papers** (full PYQ bank + explanations + weightage analytics) — free (`featPYQ` is free:true on the pricing page).
    - **Sectional practice** — free.
    - **Custom build-from-bank** (`createCustomTestFromNode`/custom sets over the *existing* published bank) — free. NOTE the distinction: on-demand *generation* of a fresh mock (`createFreshMockSet`, `assertMockTests`) IS Pro; building a set by *selecting* from the already-published bank is free.
    - **Daily quiz** — free.
    - **Current affairs** (feed, detail, per-fact add-to-revision) — free.
    - **Revision / SRS** (FSRS scheduling, review player, manage, seeds) — free.
    - **Community** (discussion, peer review, voting) — free.
    - **Scoreboards / leaderboard** — free.
    - **Magazine WEB VIEW** (both monthly editions, read online in-app) — free. ONLY the PDF export/download is Pro (`magazine_pdf`, see above).
    - Free tier also includes: 3 lifetime AI evaluations, 10 mentor msgs/day, top-5 notes/paper by weightage.
  - **VERIFICATION — trial/subscription-expiry reverts every gate on the very next request, no logout/refresh (tested live against the cloud DB).** Confirmed by code that every gate re-reads plan FRESH per call via `getPlanFor`/`getTrialContext` (a `users_profile` select each time; no per-request/middleware plan cache — the only cache is `listFreeNoteNodeIds`' 60s cache of WHICH notes are free, not the user's plan). Then proved it live: forced `plan_expires_at` into the past mid-session for a throwaway trial-shaped account and, without any re-fetch, re-invoked each gate's exact enforcement function. `getPlanFor`'s lazy downgrade fired immediately (plan→free, row persisted). Results — all 8 flip ALLOW→BLOCK on the next call:

    | # | Feature | Enforcement | Before lapse (trial) | After lapse (free), same call path |
    |---|---------|-------------|----------------------|-------------------------------------|
    | 1 | Evaluations | `assertEvaluationCredit` → `getEvaluationQuota` → `getTrialContext` | ALLOW (2/day, remaining 2) | BLOCK 402 (free 3 lifetime, exhausted) |
    | 2 | Mentor | `getMentorQuota` → `getTrialContext` (`enforceDailyLimit`) | ALLOW (limit 15, remaining 3) | BLOCK (limit 10, remaining 0 → 429) |
    | 3 | OCR (handwritten) | `assertHandwrittenOcr` → `assertPro` → `getPlanFor` | ALLOW | BLOCK 402 |
    | 4 | Micro-drills | `assertMicroDrill` → `assertPro` → `getPlanFor` | ALLOW | BLOCK 402 |
    | 5 | Mocks | `assertMockTests` → `assertPro` → `getPlanFor` | ALLOW | BLOCK 402 |
    | 6 | Full notes | `canReadFullNote` → `getPlanFor` | ALLOW (gated node readable) | BLOCK (false for non-free node) |
    | 7 | On-demand generation | `createFreshMockSet` → `assertMockTests` → `getPlanFor` | ALLOW | BLOCK 402 |
    | + | Magazine PDF (new) | `assertMagazinePdf` → `assertPro` → `getPlanFor` | ALLOW | BLOCK 402 `magazine_pdf` |

    Also validated at the real HTTP boundary for the new magazine endpoints (same access token, no refresh): trial/Pro → 200, lapsed→free → 402 `feature=magazine_pdf`, web-view → 200 throughout. All throwaway users + seeded rows deleted by explicit id afterward. `pnpm --filter api typecheck` + `pnpm --filter web build` clean.

## Guest/anonymous browsing via Supabase native anonymous auth (2026-07-28)
A newcomer's first, session-less visit now silently gets a real anonymous Supabase session and lands in the actual app (not a static marketing page), can use every genuinely-free-to-serve surface, and is prompted to sign up (framed as "create an account + your 7-day trial", never a generic paywall) the moment they reach an AI/paid surface — with the anonymous→real upgrade preserving all their progress in place. Built on Supabase's NATIVE anonymous auth (`signInAnonymously`), not a separate demo-data system: a guest is just an `authenticated`-role user with `is_anonymous:true`, so the entire existing stack (JWT verify, RLS, per-user API scoping) treats them like any user with zero special-casing.
  - **THE CRITICAL FIX — migration `0104_anonymous_guest_auth.sql`.** `handle_new_user()` (0052, +0075's trial grant) fires on EVERY `auth.users` INSERT, so a native anonymous signup would have unconditionally consumed a 7-day Pro trial. Rewrote the trigger to branch on `new.is_anonymous`: anonymous → a plain FREE profile (column defaults, `has_used_trial` stays false, no expiry); real signup → the unchanged 0075 behavior (`plan='pro'`, `now()+7d`, `has_used_trial=true`). VERIFIED at the SQL level in a rolled-back transaction (0 rows persisted): a synthetic `is_anonymous=true` insert → free/no-expiry/no-trial; a synthetic real insert → pro/+7d/trial. Applied to the cloud DB via the IPv6 direct host (see [[supabase-headless-migrations]]).
  - **Trial ONLY at real signup — including the upgrade path.** Converting a guest (email/password `updateUser`, or Google `linkIdentity`) is an UPDATE to the SAME `auth.users` row (`is_anonymous` flips false), which does NOT re-fire the AFTER INSERT trigger. So the trial for a converting guest is granted at conversion time IN CODE: `services/trial.ts::claimTrial(userId)` re-reads the user from the Auth admin API (authoritative, not the possibly-stale client JWT), confirms they're no longer anonymous, and grants the trial scoped `.eq("has_used_trial", false)` (idempotent replay guard — a fresh real signup already has it true and is never re-granted). Exposed as `POST /auth/claim-trial` (authenticated); the web calls it after any conversion, driven by a `neev.pendingTrialClaim` localStorage flag set at conversion start (survives the OAuth redirect) so a never-guest signup makes no redundant call. Net invariant: trial granted exactly once, only at a real signup (fresh OR guest-upgrade), never for a pure guest.
  - **Server-side is_anonymous plumbing + AI gates.** `lib/jwt.ts` `verifyAccessToken` now returns `{userId, isAnonymous}` (from the `is_anonymous` JWT claim; also on the HS256 `getUser` fallback); `lib/user-context.ts` carries it in the async store with a non-throwing `currentUserIsAnonymous()` (false outside a request, so jobs are unaffected — `runWithUser` gained an optional `{isAnonymous}` arg, back-compatible for every existing CLI/scheduler caller). `entitlements.ts` gained `assertNotGuest(feature)` → a 402 flagged `requiresSignup:true` (new `HttpError` option, serialized alongside `feature` in the error envelope; `ApiError` on the web reads it). Wired at the three AI spend points BEFORE any model cost: `evaluate.ts` `createSubmission`/`planEvaluation`/`planOcr` and `mentor/index.ts` `planDoubtMessage`. `getEntitlements` returns a new `is_guest` flag (shared `entitlementsSchema`) so the UI shows "sign up to unlock", never a free-tier "N left" a guest can't spend.
  - **Abuse mitigation — per-IP, separate from per-user (task requirement).** TWO layers: (1) the authoritative, UNBYPASSABLE backstop is Supabase's own GoTrue `anonymous_users` rate limit = **30 sign-ins/hour/IP** (it sees the true client IP because the browser calls `signInAnonymously` directly; config.toml + dashboard). (2) An app-level `POST /auth/guest` checkpoint the web calls BEFORE signing in, a NEW `lib/rate-limit.ts::rateLimitByIp` keyed strictly by `req.ip` (distinct from the per-user `rateLimit`), at **10 guest sessions/hour/IP** — deliberately UNDER GoTrue's 30 so our friendly localized "create a free account" nudge fires before GoTrue's opaque 429. Reasoning: a real visitor needs ~1 guest session/device; 10/hr tolerates modest shared-NAT (hostel/college/library — common for UPPSC aspirants) + cookie-clears while stopping scripted mass-creation; anyone past it is nudged to sign up (a real account isn't IP-throttled the same way) — the exact conversion we want. `app.set("trust proxy", true)` in production so `req.ip` is the real client IP behind Cloudflare/Render (a spoofed XFF only rotates OUR app-level key; GoTrue's real-IP limit still backstops). LIVE-TESTED: `POST /auth/guest` returns 200 then 429 exactly at the 11th call/hour/IP. Same per-instance in-memory caveat as the existing limiter (swap for a shared store on a multi-instance deploy).
  - **RLS — verified, not assumed (task requirement).** A DB-wide `pg_policies` scan confirms **NO policy references `is_anonymous`** anywhere (public + storage), so every owner-scoped (`to authenticated using auth.uid()=user_id`) and content-read (`to anon,authenticated`) policy applies to a guest uniformly — a guest reads/writes only its OWN rows and reads only published content. A dedicated `pnpm --filter api guest:rls` (`scripts/guest-rls-check.ts`) proves it LIVE against the cloud DB using the anon key + a real anonymous JWT (the exact browser credentials): guest profile is free/no-trial (the 0104 trigger firing on a genuine `signInAnonymously`), guest CAN read public content + insert/read its OWN attempt, CANNOT read/insert another user's rows, CANNOT write content tables, and the API `/entitlements` reports `is_guest:true`. It skips gracefully (exit 0) when anonymous sign-ins are disabled, so it never blocks CI before the toggle is on. **LIVE-VERIFIED once the founder enabled the toggle: `guest:rls` = 10/10 PASS** against the cloud DB with a real anonymous JWT.
  - **FULL LIVE BROWSER E2E — 19/19 PASS** (`scratchpad guest-e2e.mjs`, Playwright, temp): a real `navigator.webdriver=true` visit does NOT auto-create a guest (prerender-safe) and stays on the landing; a disguised (webdriver=false) first visit auto-creates a guest session and lands on the dashboard with the same-device banner; the guest completes a REAL daily-quiz attempt through the API with its own token; `/entitlements` reports `is_guest`/free/no-trial; the server blocks a guest evaluation with 402 `requiresSignup` AND the eval quota chip shows "sign up to unlock" whose click opens the SIGNUP modal (not the Pro paywall); the guest banner renders in Hindi at 390px with zero console errors; and the full conversion invariant holds — converting in place keeps the SAME auth row, the guest's daily-quiz attempt + profile survive under that same id, no trial exists until claimed, `POST /auth/claim-trial` then grants the 7-day trial (pro + expiry) and is idempotent on a second call. Every synthetic user (guest + throwaway B + converted) deleted by explicit id afterward — 0 leftover anonymous/test users confirmed. (Note: the live email→real conversion in the E2E is completed via the admin API because the cloud project has `mailer_autoconfirm:false`; the browser `updateUser` path is real but needs the emailed link — Google `linkIdentity` remains the smoother path and needs Manual Linking on.)
  - **Web — auto-guest, gating, conversion.** `AuthProvider` gained `isGuest` (`user.is_anonymous`), `signInAnonymously` (via the `/auth/guest` gate), `linkGoogle`/`convertGuestWithPassword` (data-preserving conversion, both set the pending-trial-claim flag). `components/marketing/guest-autostart.tsx` (mounted on the landing route) silently creates a guest session on a first session-less visit and navigates to the dashboard — SKIPS bots/prerender (`navigator.webdriver`, so crawlers still get the prerendered marketing HTML and the build's prerender pass creates no junk rows), one attempt per tab, and FAILS OPEN (anonymous disabled/rate-limited/offline → the landing page just stays, i.e. shipping this before the toggle is on is a no-op). `RequireAuth` lets a guest skip the onboarding+welcome gates (those are for a real account; a guest hits them after converting) and mounts `useClaimTrialOnConversion`. Gating UI: `PaywallModal` renders a "create your free account + 7-day trial" variant whenever `isGuest` (so EVERY 402 trigger is guest-aware), plus proactive `isGuest` checks at the eval/OCR (`answers-write`) and mentor (`mentor-chat`) entry points open it instantly with no wasted request; the eval quota chip shows a signup state for guests. Same-device honesty (task requirement): a persistent `GuestBanner` on every app-shell page ("progress is saved on this device — create a free account to keep it + start your trial") and a guest-specific account menu (Create free account / same-device note / Exit). `auth.tsx` detects a guest and switches Google→`linkIdentity` and sign-up→`updateUser`, with a "your progress comes with you" banner. Bilingual copy added to `messages/{en,hi}.json` (`Auth.guest*`, `Guest.*`) + `billing-copy.ts`.
  - **Two required dashboard toggles (documented in TODO above, can't be set via API here — no management token):** Anonymous Sign-Ins (project-level) and, for Google guest-conversion, Manual Linking. Also found `mailer_autoconfirm:false` on the cloud project → a guest converting via EMAIL must click the emailed confirmation link (built-in email is rate-limited), so **Google is the smoother guest-conversion path**; the email path's `needsConfirmation` branch is handled honestly in the UI.
  - **Offline verification (all clean):** `pnpm --filter api typecheck`, web `tsc -b` + full `vite build`, `oxlint` (no new warnings); migration applied + trigger branch proven via rolled-back SQL; RLS `is_anonymous` grep; `POST /auth/guest` 200→429 rate-limit + `claim-trial` 401-without-auth tested against the running API.
  - **FOLLOW-UP (same day, from founder feedback on the live UI):** two fixes. (1) **Visible guest entry** — "no option on the UI" (the silent auto-guest is a no-op while the toggle is off, so a visitor saw no way in): added an explicit **"Explore as a guest"** button (`components/marketing/guest-entry-button.tsx`) to the landing hero AND the auth page (`Guest.exploreCta`/`unavailable`, en+hi). It calls `signInAnonymously` → dashboard, and degrades HONESTLY when anonymous is disabled/rate-limited (an inline "guest mode isn't available — sign in" note, not a dead button) — so it works the moment the toggle flips and never looks broken before then. (2) **Google login "had to do it twice"** — root-caused to a PRE-EXISTING race in `auth-callback.tsx` (NOT the guest work: empirically confirmed a failed `signInAnonymously` writes no PKCE code-verifier, so it can't clobber a subsequent OAuth). The callback declared failure the instant it saw no `?code=` + no session, but supabase-js's `detectSessionInUrl` exchange is async and can strip `?code=` a tick before the session propagates through the provider — so the first attempt bounced to sign-in and the user retried. Fixed: wait a grace window (8s, matching the `auth-reset.tsx` precedent) for the session before failing, and carry the reason to `/auth?error=` (surfaced in the auth form) instead of a silent bounce. Verified in-browser (both locales, 390px): guest button visible + graceful-degradation note on a toggle-off click, callback no longer instant-bounces, no horizontal overflow; web `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean. (The one console line in the check was a benign `429` from the localhost per-IP `/auth/guest` bucket being exhausted by this session's own repeated testing — the button still degraded correctly.)
  - **EDGE-CASE AUDIT (post-live-verification) — 2 real gaps found + fixed, both a guest writing to a SOCIAL/COMPETITIVE surface that "guests are read-only there" implied but nothing enforced:**
    - **Community was fully writable by guests.** The 9 community write endpoints (createThread/addPost/votePost/shareAnswerForPeerReview/reportContent/blockUser/…) had NO guest gate — a guest could post/vote/report/share, contradicting the documented "community read-only for guests" decision AND opening an anonymous-spam / low-accountability-moderation hole. Fixed: `assertNotGuest("community", …)` at the top of every community participation write in `services/community.ts` (a 402 `requiresSignup` with a "create a free account to join the discussion" message), plus proactive `isGuest`→`openPaywall` guards on the reply composer, new-thread submit, and vote button so a guest gets the instant signup modal instead of a submit-then-error. LIVE-VERIFIED: a guest token creating a thread (valid payload) → 402 `requiresSignup=true`; `report` → 402.
    - **Guest daily-quiz results were landing on the public leaderboard.** `recordDailyQuizResult` (run at attempt-submit) upserts `daily_quiz_board_entries` for ANY user, so a guest appeared on the competitive daily board `handle:null`. Fixed with an early `if (currentUserIsAnonymous()) return` — the guest's attempt is still scored/saved, just never placed on the board (a converted account starts appearing on the next day's quiz). Only caller is the submit request path (verified), so the context read is correct. LIVE-VERIFIED: a guest daily-quiz submit → **0** `daily_quiz_board_entries` rows.
    - **Confirmed NOT bugs (checked, so they aren't re-investigated):** the Pro-feature gates (mocks/micro-drills/all-notes/magazine-pdf) return a plain 402 that the **isGuest-aware `PaywallModal` already renders as the signup prompt**, so a guest hitting them is funneled to signup with no server change needed; the mentor **autoSend** effect sets its ref BEFORE calling the (guest-gated) `sendMessage`, so it can't loop and the guest gate fires; `getEntitlements`/`assertNotGuest`/`currentUserIsAnonymous` all return safe defaults outside a request context (jobs unaffected).
    - **The two items originally flagged here are now BOTH FIXED (see the "Guest edge-case follow-ups" note below):** (1) `v_test_leaderboard`/`mv_test_leaderboard` now excludes anonymous users (migration 0105); (2) abandoned guests are pruned by a nightly retention job (`guests:prune`).
    - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck`, web `tsc -b` + `vite build`, `oxlint` all clean; both new gates live-tested; every synthetic guest deleted by explicit id (0 leftover anonymous users).
  - **GUEST EDGE-CASE FOLLOW-UPS (the two items flagged above, now BOTH fixed):**
    - **Guests off the per-test/sectional leaderboards — migration `0105_exclude_guests_from_leaderboards.sql`.** `mv_test_leaderboard` wraps the base view `v_test_leaderboard` (mock + SECTIONAL tests; sectional practice is FREE, so a guest could appear there). `create or replace view` adds `and not exists (select 1 from auth.users u where u.id = a.user_id and u.is_anonymous)` to the qualifying CTE (column list unchanged, so the dependent mv stays valid), then refreshes `mv_test_leaderboard` + `mv_mock_series_board`. The view reads `auth.users` as its owner (default `security_invoker=false`), which has the access. Applied to the cloud DB (IPv6 direct); verified `pg_get_viewdef` now contains the filter. (The daily-quiz board was already write-path-guarded; the mains boards require evaluations guests can't make.)
    - **Abandoned-guest retention — `services/guest-cleanup.ts` `pruneAbandonedGuests` + `pnpm guests:prune` (dry-run default, `--apply`, `--days N`), run nightly by `nightly:settle`.** Prunes an anonymous user only when it's BOTH created > `GUEST_RETENTION_DAYS` (env, default 30) ago AND has NO real interaction in that window — no `events` row and no `attempts` row since the cutoff. **Deliberately uses raw `events`/`attempts`, NOT `users_profile.last_active_date`** (which is only set on a study-DAY activity — a guest who merely browses chapters/CA fires events but no study-day, and must not be pruned as abandoned; the first test cut used `last_active_date` and would have wrongly pruned a browsing guest). The activity queries use `selectAll` (cap-safe paging) so an active guest's id can never be truncated out of the keep-set. Deletion order: `users_profile` first (children `attempts`/`events`/… are `ON DELETE CASCADE` from it — verified via FK introspection; `users_profile.id` has NO FK to `auth.users`, confirming the delete-both gotcha), then the auth user; each delete re-confirms `is_anonymous` so a guest who converts between scan and delete is never removed. **Converted and real accounts are never candidates** (they aren't `is_anonymous`). LIVE-VERIFIED with backdated (40-day-old) synthetic guests: one with no activity → pruned (auth + profile + cascaded events gone); one with a recent `events` row → correctly KEPT; a converted account is never listed. Wired into `nightly-settle.ts` as a non-fatal global step (a prune failure never blocks the streak/mastery settle); `GUEST_RETENTION_DAYS` documented in `.env.example`.
    - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` clean; migration applied + view filter confirmed live; prune dry-run + `--apply` behavior proven against the cloud DB with synthetic backdated guests; 0 leftover anonymous/test users.
  - **SECOND EDGE-CASE PASS (audit of the leaderboard + prune changes themselves) — found + fixed 2 more:**
    - **The prune's activity signal was TOO NARROW.** First cut kept a guest "active" via `events` OR `attempts`, but `events` is fired from ONLY the learn/notes pages (`note_read`/`note_section_read`/`syllabus_node_view` — verified by grepping every `record`/`name:` call site); a guest active via REVISION (srs) or PERSONAL NOTES fires no event and (for revision) no attempt, so they'd have been wrongly pruned. Broadened the keep-signal to a durable-activity row in the window across ALL free-guest surfaces: `events` + `attempts` + `srs_reviews` + `srs_cards` + `user_notes` (all confirmed to have `user_id`+`created_at`). `.in()` chunk dropped to 100 ids (URL-length safety per the repo's `.in()` gotcha). LIVE-VERIFIED with 3 backdated guests: abandoned → pruned; revision-active (recent `srs_cards`) → KEPT; notes-active (recent `user_notes`) → KEPT.
    - **`question_reports` writes weren't guest-gated** — a guest could spam the PYQ-error moderation queue (an anonymous, unaccountable flag entering an admin queue — the same category as the community `reports` I gated in the first pass). Added `assertNotGuest("question_report")` in `createQuestionReport` + a proactive `isGuest`→`openPaywall` guard on `ReportQuestionSheet`. LIVE-VERIFIED: guest → 402 `requiresSignup`. (Personal, own-data writes — attempts, revision, notes — stay FREE for guests; only shared/moderation-queue writes are gated.)
    - **Confirmed NOT problems (checked against the live schema):** pruning a guest never hits a blocking FK — the only 4 non-CASCADE FKs referencing `users_profile` are `SET NULL` (`llm_calls.user_id`, `question_reports.user_id`/`resolved_by`, `reports.resolved_by`), so the profile delete succeeds and ~30 child tables cascade cleanly (no orphans); and the nightly `refresh_scoreboard_views` RPC still succeeds now that `v_test_leaderboard` references `auth.users` (the view reads it as its owner). Both verified by direct SQL.
    - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck`, web `tsc -b` + `vite build`, `oxlint` all clean; both new gates live-tested; 0 leftover anonymous/test users.

## PRODUCTION OUTAGE (2026-07-28): Cloudflare edge-cache poisoning of `/assets/*` — NOT a code bug
The whole app crashed to the error boundary on every load at neevstudy.com (confirmed in a private window). **The reported root cause — "`c05e6c5` deleted `apps/web/src/lib/guest.ts` while `auth-provider.tsx`/`use-claim-trial.ts` still import from it" — was WRONG, and checking it first is what kept this from becoming a wasted fix.** Recorded in full because the real failure mode is invisible to every local check and will recur on the next deploy.
  - **`guest.ts` was never deleted.** `c05e6c5` removed only the two now-unused *autostart* helpers (`guestAutostartAlreadyTried`/`markGuestAutostartTried`) from the file and KEPT `markTrialClaimPending`/`hasTrialClaimPending`/`clearTrialClaimPending` (`git show c05e6c5 -- apps/web/src/lib/guest.ts` shows a 23-line deletion, not a file delete). The file is tracked and present at HEAD; both imports resolve; `pnpm --filter web build` passes; the built `dist/` loads in a real browser with **zero** page errors. Nothing was restored or relocated, because nothing was missing — the only change this incident made to `apps/web` was none at all.
  - **REAL ROOT CAUSE — Cloudflare's edge cache was serving the SPA-fallback `index.html` under `/assets/<chunk>.js` URLs.** The browser rejects it (`Expected a JavaScript-or-Wasm module script but the server responded with a MIME type of "text/html"`), React Router's lazy import throws `Failed to fetch dynamically imported module: .../assets/locale-layout-DqGlLNVJ.js`, and that propagates to the route error boundary → "Something went wrong" on every load. Reproduced exactly with headless Chromium against the live site.
  - **How it was proven** (the diagnostic that matters — a naive status-code sweep MISSES this entirely): Cloudflare's SPA fallback answers a missing asset with **HTTP 200**, not 404, so `curl -sI` over all 62 referenced chunks reported "0 failures" while the app was fully down. The tell is **content-type / body**, not status. Sweeping by content-type found 3-4 poisoned chunks; the decisive observation was `revision-De6_QtiV.js` returning `text/html` on `cf-cache-status: HIT` but `application/javascript` on `MISS` — i.e. **the origin has the file, the edge cache does not**. Re-fetching every chunk with a `?cb=<random>` cache-buster returned real JS for ALL of them, confirming the deployment is complete and healthy and the poisoning is purely per-edge-node (which is why it looked intermittent, and why different users/locations saw different breakage).
  - **Why it is PERMANENT rather than self-healing:** `apps/web/public/_headers` stamps `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` on `/assets/*` **by path, regardless of what was actually served** — so the moment a fallback-HTML body is returned at an asset URL, the edge caches that HTML for a year. Mechanism: during a deploy rollout some edges still served the previous deployment (which lacks the new build's chunk hashes) → SPA fallback → 200 HTML → cached immutable → the new deployment goes fully live but those edges keep serving the year-cached HTML.
  - **Why a private window did NOT help** (and why the user's test was still valid evidence): incognito bypasses the *browser* cache, never the *CDN* cache. Same for a hard refresh.
  - **Why `fd603b8`'s `vite:preloadError` handler didn't save it:** it reloads once per 10s to fetch a fresh `index.html` — but the fresh HTML references the SAME poisoned chunk URL, so the reload fails identically and (by design) the second failure inside the window falls through to the error boundary. That handler fixes *stale-tab* chunk 404s; it cannot fix a poisoned edge entry.
  - **REMEDIATION.** (1) **Purge the Cloudflare cache** for neevstudy.com (dashboard → Caching → Purge Everything, or the API) — the only guaranteed, immediate fix. It is an OWNER action: `wrangler whoami` is unauthenticated in this environment and no `CLOUDFLARE_*`/`CF_*` token exists in any `.env`, so it cannot be done from here. (2) A **redeploy also clears it in practice**, because a fresh build emits different content hashes (verified: local build produced `locale-layout-Drmyz5w4.js`/`revision-1pJSQcy3.js`/`scoreboard-BY1vuZ_Z.js` vs production's `-DqGlLNVJ`/`-De6_QtiV`/`-B2xf0Ojp`), so the new `index.html` points at URLs that were never poisoned.
  - **DURABLE FIX — NOT shipped here, deliberately, and this is the important part.** The correct prevention is to stop `/assets/*` from EVER falling through to the SPA shell (serve a real 404 instead, which is not cacheable-as-immutable and lets the `vite:preloadError` reload actually work). That means editing `_redirects` — the exact file whose last "obvious" fix caused a **real, total production outage** (the `__spa-fallback.html` infinite-redirect incident documented in the TODO section above).
