    - **PRE-EXISTING, and my new reference would have inherited it: `docs/multi-exam.md` had TWO sections numbered `### 3d`** ("Found by the post-commit edge-case audit" and "Answer-writing boards + the rubric registry"), and both meanings were **already in live use** — CLAUDE.md's M1-M4 entry meant the first, while §3 item 5 and the M5-M9 entry meant the second. Renumbered only the first to **§3f** (so every existing "§3d" reference stays correct), left an inline HTML comment recording why, and updated the one CLAUDE.md pointer that meant it. All 19 headings are now unique and all 15 `§` references resolve.
    - **REAL SECOND GAP FOUND → M22: `notes/chapter-prompts.ts` hardcodes UPPSC in 8 places** — the outline persona (*"a senior UPPSC (Uttar Pradesh PCS) faculty member"*), the web-research persona, the section-authoring persona, the grounding-passage header, and **both** fact-audit personas; `notes/prompts.ts` has 8 more for the digest layer. Nothing takes an exam and there is no guard, so `pnpm notes:chapter --paper UPSC_…` would produce UPPSC-framed, UPPSC-fact-checked chapters for another exam **silently**. Not on the critical path for M15's decided workflow (free subagents run from a per-rollout instructions file, not this module) but squarely on the path for the paid route reserved for prod/cron. Recorded like M21 rather than fixed blind; both are now flagged in the sequencing note as **the first step of any real second-exam chapter rollout**.

## Multi-exam §8c: M11 decided, M14 fixed (2026-07-29)
Reconsidering **M11** *before* a second exam's ingest rather than after it, per its own "revisit if the invariant is ever relaxed" condition — which the next phase of work was about to make false. **The call: NO new DB-level constraint on `tests.paper_code`, `exam_cutoffs.paper_code` or `mv_mock_series_board`'s key. The `syllabus_nodes` invariant plus M1's `assertPaperCodeScoped` IS sufficient defense-in-depth for those three — but the audit that produced the decision found the real exposure is elsewhere, and that is the part worth keeping.**
  - **Why no constraint — measured live, not reasoned from the schema.** (1) `syllabus_nodes` already enforces global uniqueness *in practice*: across all 294 nodes `depth = 0 ⟺ path = ''` holds with **0 exceptions in both directions** and every paper code has exactly **one** depth-0 root, so `syllabus_nodes_paper_path_key (paper_code, path)` collides at `(code, '')` for any second tree reusing a taken code — a loud `23505` before a single node lands. (2) All three M11 surfaces hold only **derived copies** and never mint a code: `tests.paper_code` is read off the node/question row in the same statement that stamps `exam_code` from that *same* row (`createCustomTestFromNode` takes both from `resolveOrderedNodes`' already-exam-checked rows), `exam_cutoffs` has **no code writer at all** (`services/mocks.ts` only reads it, and its 20 rows are all `PRE_GS1`/`uppsc`), and `mv_mock_series_board` derives wholly from `tests`. A mismatch would need a writer to read two different rows; none does. (3) The only shape that makes the invariant DB-true is a `papers(paper_code PK, exam_code FK)` registry with FKs onto all three — you cannot FK `(exam_code, paper_code)` without a unique on that pair, and `syllabus_nodes` has many rows per paper — and **that registry is not free**: `tests.paper_code` carries `CURRENT_AFFAIRS` on **16 live rows** and `questions.paper_code` also carries `EDGE`, synthetic codes with no `syllabus_nodes` row, so a blanket FK turns today's working CA-quiz path into a `23503` unless synthetic registry rows are minted and kept in sync forever. That trades a hypothetical writer bug for a real new hard-failure mode. **Recorded revisit condition, deliberately narrower than "a second exam exists": reopen if a paper code is ever minted somewhere other than an ingest pipeline.**
  - **FIXED, because the decision is only honest if the reads that lean on the invariant are actually scoped: `getMockSeriesBoard` had no exam check.** `GET /scoreboard/prelims/mocks/series?paper_code=` takes an **untrusted** query param and filtered `mv_mock_series_board` by `paper_code` alone — global uniqueness makes that board internally *consistent*, it does not make it *access-scoped*, so a second exam's user could read another exam's series board just by passing its code. Its sibling `getTestBoard` already closes exactly this for `test_id` (`if (test.exam_code !== examCode) throw notFound`), as does `resolveOrderedNodes` for untrusted node ids — this was the one that had been missed. Now derives the paper's exam from `syllabus_nodes` (in parallel with the profile read, no added latency) and 404s a foreign paper, reported as "not found" rather than "wrong exam" for the same reason as `resolveOrderedNodes`. A code resolving to **no** node still falls through to its (necessarily empty) board rather than 404 — verified live that all 58 mock tests use one of the 10 real paper codes, so this only affects codes that can have no mocks anyway.
  - **⚑ THE REAL FINDING, recorded as M23 rather than fixed as a drive-by: the paper-code prefix rule is enforced in only ONE of the TWO pipelines that mint a paper code.** `ingest:syllabus` is guarded (M1). `ingest:pyq` is not — `classifyPyqId` (`ingest/_shared.ts`) maps `upsc_prelims_2024_gs1` to a **bare `PRE_GS1`** for every exam, and `pyq-load.ts`'s `resolveSyllabusId` then resolves a paper code to a node with `.eq("paper_code", …)` and **no exam filter**, so a second exam's PYQs would take UPPSC's paper code and attach to UPPSC's syllabus tree silently, with no constraint violation anywhere. Not fixed here because the fix needs a decision that does not exist yet: `classifyPyqId` returns the **provenance** `ExamCode` (domain includes `up_ro_aro`/`upsssc_pet`/`other`, which have no product exam and no tree) while `paperCodePrefixFor` takes the **product** `TargetExamCode`, and the mapping between the two enums has never been defined; changing the returned code also changes parsed-JSON contents and the `(paper_code, year)` test slugs. **Fix it as the FIRST step of a second exam's PYQ ingest (U5), before the first `ingest:pyq` run** — the exact mirror of how M21/M22 gate a chapter rollout. Today's only guard is the convention *"never ingest `upsc_*`/`upsssc_*` files"*, which U5 is by definition the act of relaxing. Written into `docs/multi-exam.md` §0a, next to the invariant itself, so a second-exam session reading that file finds it.
  - **M14 — `0106` is now hand-replayable, and both directions were verified rather than asserted.** `create table if not exists`; **drop-first** for the trigger and the policy, because Postgres has neither `CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS` nor `CREATE POLICY IF NOT EXISTS` (the literal instruction is impossible for those two — the repo's own 0052/0057/0066/0070 and 0053 convention is what to follow); plus a `drop constraint if exists` for the **new** `exam_cutoffs_exam_paper_stage_year_category_key` name, which the original dropped only under its *old* name and so would have failed on a re-run too. Verified against the live DB inside a rolled-back transaction: the pre-fix file fails with `relation "exams" already exists`, the fixed file replays end-to-end cleanly on an already-0106 database, rollback left zero persistent change. **Editing an applied migration is safe here specifically because `0106`'s ledger row was hand-inserted with EMPTY `statements`** (§0c's reconciliation), so there is no recorded-vs-file drift for the ledger scan to trip over — do not generalise this to a migration whose row carries real statements.
  - **M12 and M13 deliberately NOT touched**, per instruction. M12 (the `GET /daily/cutoffs?exam=` param that actually carries a *paper* code) stays recorded as-is. M13 belongs to the planned **U3 (Exam selection UX)** session — **U3's prompt is not in this repo** (only `U5` is referenced by name, in `docs/multi-exam.md` §2), so it could not be diffed directly; `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §8c now carries a 5-point requirements checklist to diff it against, plus the two things learned since 0106 that U3's prompt predates: M5-M9 landed (so a picker changing `target_exam` now changes boards, community and evaluations at once, not just the papers grid) and `assertSelectableExam` already exists (so U3 is smaller than when it was written). It also flags that a switcher would implicitly settle **M16**, the one product decision still open.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck`, `pnpm --filter web build`, `oxlint`, `pnpm check:paths` clean. `pg` was installed as a temporary devDependency for the rolled-back replay and removed afterwards (`package.json`/`pnpm-lock.yaml` confirmed back to clean); all probe scripts deleted. **Concurrent process:** another session committed `ce2e14b` (the M15/M18/M19 audit, which introduced M22) into this same tree mid-session — its docs edits were already committed before any of this session's docs edits were made, so the two are cleanly sequential; this session's files were staged by explicit name, never `git add -A`.

### Edge-case audit of the M11/M14 commit, same day (2026-07-29): one live bug found, plus a claim re-verified
Adversarial pass over `c334ec3` — checking my own claims against the live DB and sweeping for siblings of the read I had just fixed, rather than re-running the happy path.
  - **REAL, LIVE BUG — the SAME class I had just fixed, on the very table M11 names, one file away. `GET /mocks/cutoffs` served UPPSC's cut-offs to every user regardless of `target_exam`.** `getCutoffs(paperCode, examCode = DEFAULT_EXAM_CODE)` carries an exam filter and a doc comment promising it "keeps the lookup correct once a second exam seeds cut-offs" — but its **only** caller (`routes/daily.ts`) never passed one, so the filter was inert and pinned to `uppsc`. I had asserted in the M11 write-up that "`exam_cutoffs` has no code writer at all" and stopped there; I had not checked whether its one **reader** was scoped. **Fixed:** both parameters are now required and the route passes `getUserExam(currentUserId())` — note `paperCode`'s own default had to be dropped too, because TypeScript forbids a required parameter after an optional one (the route's zod schema still defaults it, so the public API is unchanged). Live-verified: uppsc + `PRE_GS1` → **20 rows, unchanged**; a second exam's user → **0 rows, fail-closed** rather than another exam's numbers.
  - **The general lesson, now bitten twice and recorded as such (M24): a trailing DEFAULTED parameter does not make a caller decide — it lets the caller silently keep the old behaviour.** The previous audit already hit this with `getMasteryMap`'s `targetExam` (where the fix was an options object because the parameter could not even be *made* required in place). Two more instances of the identical shape were found this pass, and one is a real correction to an earlier claim: **`qgen/topup.ts`'s `computeNodeTargets(..., examCode = DEFAULT_EXAM_CODE)` is passed by neither of its two callers, so M2's "`qgen/topup.ts` scoped" is only half true.** Recorded rather than rewired mid-audit (threading it changes the exported `reserveTargetsFor` and the CLI — a chain, and `qgen:topup` is UPPSC-only today like `ingest:tests`).
  - **Swept every route that takes a paper code from the request, and the answer is NOT "fix them all" — recorded so nobody does.** `getPaperTree`, `getPaperTrends` and `getTimeAttackTopics` all read by `paper_code` alone with an untrusted code, and that is the **intended** design: they serve public, published reference content (`syllabus_nodes` is a public-read content table under 0053), the UI never links across exams because `getPaperSummaries` is exam-scoped, and §0 explicitly blesses the pattern for ~30 call sites. The boards are genuinely different: `getTestBoard` **already** exam-checked `test_id`, so `getMockSeriesBoard` was inconsistent with its own sibling, and M17 decided boards/community are exam-separated — serving another exam's board contradicts a decision, not just a preference. Gating public syllabus browsing per exam would be a product decision, not a bug fix. Written into `docs/multi-exam.md` §0a.
  - **Re-verified my own "safe to edit an applied migration" claim against the live ledger rather than trusting the changelog** (the lesson the previous audit recorded): `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` shows `0106` with **0 recorded statements** — confirmed alongside its neighbours (`0102`/`0103` also 0; `0098`/`0101`/`0104`/`0105`/`0107`-`0110` all carry real statements, since `migration repair --status applied` parses the file). So editing `0106` genuinely creates no file-vs-ledger drift. **Do not generalise this** — the same edit to a migration whose row carries real statements WOULD drift.
  - **Also found while auditing the fix, recorded not fixed: `components/practice/cutoff-comparison.tsx` is UPPSC-shaped in three hardcoded ways** — `useCutoffs("PRE_GS1")`, `paper_code === "PRE_CSAT"`, and a literal 33% qualifying line (MPPSC's is 40%/30% **by category**, per 0106's own verified seed). With the server fix a second exam's user now sees an empty cut-off card instead of wrong numbers. Worth noting for U3: **this card is the concrete, already-built consumer for `exams.paper_structure`'s `minimum{role, pct, pct_by_category}`** — the field seeded by 0106 and still unread — which is exactly what makes the U3 checklist's item 4 more than hypothetical.
  - **Checked and confirmed clean** (recorded so they aren't re-checked): the two `scoreboard_rank_snapshots` reads are both `.eq("user_id", userId)` — the user's own history, no untrusted `board_key`, so no twin of the board gap; `mastery.ts`'s two routes DO pass `targetExam: await getUserExam(userId)`; all 11 rows of §8c's table carry a uniform 5 pipes; every `§` reference added this session resolves to a real heading (`§0a` is new and unique, `§3f` exists); `exam_cutoffs` has no writer anywhere in `apps/`/`packages/` (repo-wide grep, not just `services/`); and the `getMockSeriesBoard` guard cannot regress a UPPSC user, since all 10 paper codes and all 58 mock tests resolve to `uppsc`.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck`, `pnpm --filter web build`, `oxlint`, `pnpm check:paths` clean. `pg` re-installed and removed again for the ledger read; all probe scripts deleted.

## Exam-config prompt sweep (2026-07-30): every hardcoded "UPPSC" in `apps/api` behind one module, + the web onto the registry
Nine commits, `14a1493`..`27fd7d9`. `apps/api/src/lib/exam-config.ts` is now the single source of every per-exam string an LLM prompt interpolates, and `apps/web` reads the exam registry instead of literals via a **new public `GET /api/v1/exams`**. No schema change; `uppsc` is still the only `is_live` exam and `upsc`/`mppsc` still carry zero content. Closed `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` **M13** (no exams route), **M22** (chapter prompts hardcoded UPPSC) and **M24 residual (2)** (`cutoff-comparison.tsx`); opened **§8f** (U6 + M25-M29). Standing instruction for this layer: **`docs/multi-exam.md` §6**.
  - **THE VERIFICATION METHOD, and why it is the whole story.** `pnpm prompts:snapshot` (`apps/api/scripts/prompt-snapshot.ts`, landed FIRST as `14a1493`, before a single call site was converted) assembles every reachable model-facing prompt from **fixed fixtures** — no clock, random, DB or network — and diffs it against a committed baseline (`scripts/__snapshots__/prompts.baseline.json`). **Byte-identity is proven by string diff in code, never by a model call.** Multi-segment systems are captured as `{segments:[{text,cache}]}` so segment boundaries and cache flags are part of the baseline — the cached-prefix STRUCTURE is guarded, not just the text. **100 keys at baseline (57 of them containing the literal "UPPSC", which the fixtures deliberately never emit, so any occurrence provably came from the template) → 116 after the API+web slices → 128 today.** Negative-tested rather than assumed: a mutated baseline (renamed exam, one deleted key, one phantom key) correctly reported CHANGED/MISSING/NEW and exited 1 — a harness that always passes is worthless.
  - **THE STRONGEST PROOF IN THE SET, and the distinction worth internalising (`27fd7d9`).** Seven converted prompts sat in the baseline's honest `__not_reachable_without_editing__` list, so the sweep changed them and **nothing checked the result** — eyeball only. They were made reachable AND compared **against the PRE-REFACTOR literals, extracted MECHANICALLY from `git show 14a1493:<path>`** (anchor-located source slices, then eval'd, so a transcription slip could not fake a pass). **Snapshotting today's output would merely bless whatever the sweep produced; comparing against the pre-refactor text is a real check.** Result **15/15 exact, 0 differences**, with a negative control (the same string with one space deleted at a `Rules:\n- Each` concat boundary) confirming the check actually bites, and the original 116 keys re-verified 0 changed / 0 dropped at byte level. Both flagged risk areas came back clean: a mid-sentence substitution across an old concat boundary, and `deepDivePyqHeader`'s leading `"\n"` now living inside a config value (tested via both branches of `buildContext`).
  - **`UNAUTHORED` is a `unique symbol`, and that choice was verified empirically, not reasoned about.** TypeScript refuses to interpolate it (TS2731), concatenate it (TS2469), or pass it where a string is wanted (TS2345); even a forced cast renders `Symbol(unauthored-exam-config)`, not plausible examiner prose. **A sentinel STRING would have compiled and shipped into a live prompt.** uppsc is **98/98 filled**; upsc and mppsc are **74 unauthored each** (now tracked as **U6** — six source files already carried a `(U6 — docs/OUTSTANDING.md §8)` comment pointing at a row that did not exist; it does now). **Judgment is never derived by substitution:** building any converted prompt for `"upsc"` throws naming the exam and the exact field — the severity anchor encodes empirical findings about how Mains is actually marked (topper percentages, the 45-55%-per-answer calibration), and the qgen/CA/mentor slots encode one commission's examiner judgment; neither transfers by renaming.
  - **`Record<TargetExamCode, ExamConfig>` makes a fourth exam a COMPILE ERROR until it is configured** — verified by temporarily adding `"bpsc"` (TS2741), then reverting. Do not weaken that type to a partial. Correspondingly, `TargetExamCode`/`TARGET_EXAM_CODES`/`DEFAULT_EXAM_CODE` are **imported from `@neev/shared`, never redeclared**, and **`paper_structure`/`launch_scope_i18n` are NOT copied into TypeScript** — they stay DB-authoritative (0106, verified against commission notification PDFs), with `assertExamConfigMatchesRegistry()` as the drift guard. This is not caution for its own sake: `ingest/_shared.ts` once redeclared `ExamCode` as a local copy and **silently drifted on the very first extension, with no typecheck error**.
  - **CACHE BOUNDARY — the rule, and the refinement that is easy to get wrong.** 14 executable `cache: true` flags; **exam text PARTITIONS every one of them and destroys none** (per-exam text turns one entry into one entry per exam; per-request text in a cached prefix would make the prefix vary per call and destroy the entry outright — nothing per-request was added). **⚑ The refinement: in the four two-segment builders (`buildMcqGenParams`, `buildDescGenParams`, `buildNoteGenParams`, `buildSectionParams`) the flag sits on segment `[1]`, so the cached prefix is `[0]+[1]` — the "uncached-looking" persona segment `[0]` is INSIDE the cache key.** Per-exam text there is fine; anything per-request there is fatal. `sharedFeedbackContext` (evaluation strengths/improvements, cached `[0]` shared byte-identically by both sibling calls) **deliberately gained no exam text** — interpolating there would kill the N=2 sibling hit; its byte-identity between the two calls was re-verified in the audit. Separately, **`ca/prompts.ts` was left byte-untouched**: its A/B-validated item-text-first ordering is load-bearing for QUALITY (hoisting the candidate list to cache it DID cache at 98.9% `cache_read` but regressed gate survivors 14→8 and mapped nodes 37→25, ~3× beyond a 3-arm control's noise floor). Evidence it was respected, not just intended: `git diff -U0` shows **no changed `system:`/`content:` property lines** and there are **zero executable `cache: true` in `src/ca/`** (all 5 grep hits are prose comments).
  - **THE HAIKU-4096 FACT, and why `chars/4` will lie to you.** A `cache: true` whose prefix is below the model's minimum simply does not cache — no error, no warning, `cache_creation_input_tokens` just stays 0 forever. **`claude-haiku-4-5`'s minimum is 4096 tokens, FOUR TIMES `claude-sonnet-5`'s 1024**, and the minimum is **not monotonic across generations** (newest Opus 512; Opus 4.6/4.5 and Haiku 4.5 are 4096), so it **cannot be inferred from model recency — look it up.** MEASURE, do not estimate: `chars/4` under-counts by ~30% at this codebase's real ~2.85 chars/token and will tell you a failing prompt passes; use `messages.countTokens` (free). Measured this way, **5 of the 14 flags are no-ops** — qgen `verifySystem` **107** (haiku, ~38× short), notes `CRITIC_SYSTEM` **206**, OCR `buildTranscribeSystem` **270/280**, qgen `criticSystem` **611**, `buildTeacherPersona` **879 (en)/888 (hi)**. **These are PRE-EXISTING and byte-identical before and after; what the sweep briefly added was comments asserting they work.** Each site now carries its own measured count and says plainly it caches nothing today; flags left in place (a below-minimum prefix is not billed the 1.25× write premium either, and they become correct for free if a prompt grows). The canonical write-up lives on `PromptSegment` in `lib/anthropic.ts`, where someone typing `cache: true` actually looks. Noted honestly and left uncorrected: **Session 13's log claims the OCR prompt was "LIVE-VERIFIED" to hit the cache — at 270 tokens it cannot have been.**
  - **⚑ A REAL SECOND-EXAM TRAP: the mentor persona has +22 tokens of headroom.** `buildMentorPersona` is the ONLY cached segment on the generic-doubt path and measures **1046 tok (en) / 1055 (hi)** against sonnet-5's 1024, of which **102 tokens now come from config** (`testingLens` 81, `platformFraming` 21). **A second exam whose framing is ≥23 tokens TERSER silently drops under the minimum and Anthropic just stops caching** — no error, every mentor doubt re-billing the full persona forever. The old comment warned only that per-request text kills a cache; **shorter per-exam text kills it too.** Guarded by a 2950-char floor in the harness that **also runs in `--write` mode**, so `--write` cannot bless a persona that fell below the line, plus a note at both config keys where a second-exam author is actually typing. The WARN it prints for uppsc is accurate, not noise — the margin genuinely is 22 tokens, and the honest fix if it becomes irritating is to **lengthen the persona, not lower the threshold**.
  - **THE WORST SINGLE INSTANCE FOUND — `lib/anthropic.ts`'s `translate`/`translateBatch` `domainHint` DEFAULT PARAMETERS.** `domainHint = "UPPSC exam-prep content"` / `"UPPSC exam questions"` meant **every caller that omitted the argument emitted "UPPSC" with nothing at the call site to grep for**; converting only the explicit call sites would have left the majority silently wrong. Every caller was grepped first, and **the two silently-defaulting callers were the MAJORITY of `translate()` call sites** (`ingest/syllabus.ts` ×4 → `paper.exam`; `ingest/pyq.ts` → `INGEST_PRODUCT_EXAM`). Both defaults are now REQUIRED. Same M24 lesson applied to the three temporary cross-slice defaults (`generateQuickCheck`, `fewShotBlock`, `buildVerifyParams`) — all made required in `5cfff21`, with `ca/verify-mcqs` falling back to `DEFAULT_EXAM_CODE` **at the call site, where it is greppable**, rather than hidden in a signature default. Zero `NOT PARAMETERISED` / `OPTIONAL ONLY FOR BACKWARD COMPATIBILITY` markers remain in `apps/api/src`.
  - **PERSISTED IDENTIFIERS THAT MUST NEVER BE RENAMED — this was the specific trap to avoid, not a hypothetical.** (1) **`uppsc_tone`** — a `CRITIC_SCHEMA` property sent to the model as `output_config`, a required field of `CriticVerdict` in `@neev/shared`, AND a key inside every stored `questions.generation_meta`. (2) **`up_angle`** — a `NoteContentI18n` field, a `NOTE_GEN_SCHEMA` property (and in its `required` list), and a key in every stored `notes.content_i18n`. (3) **`is_up_specific`** — a live DB column. Renaming any of them breaks parsing of live rows; **only the prose around them was parameterised** (`uppsc_tone`'s criterion text after the colon; `up_angle`'s rendering label), each with a "NEVER rename it" comment at the line.
  - **DELIBERATELY NOT CONVERTED, each with a reason rather than an omission.** (a) **~57 marketing/legal i18n values** (`Features.*`, Landing, About, Faq, Terms, Privacy) — these render on **public, PRERENDERED, SEO-indexed pages where there is no signed-in user and so no target exam**, and they are claims about what the product covers TODAY; templating them would assert coverage that does not exist (`upsc`/`mppsc` have zero syllabus nodes and zero questions). (b) **`exam-chip.tsx`'s `EXAM_FALLBACK` / `ingest/_shared.ts`'s `EXAM_LABELS`** — both keyed by **PROVENANCE** (`up_ro_aro`, `upsssc_pet`, `other`), none of which is a product exam or has an `ExamConfig`; routing them through the product config would **mislabel a RO/ARO question as the viewer's own exam**. They need a provenance-keyed table, which is what they already are. (c) **`billing-copy.ts`'s paywall mocks line** stays UPPSC-named because **mocks genuinely ARE UPPSC-only today** (`services/mocks.ts` filters on `exam_code`; a second exam needs its own builder, not a threaded parameter) — templating it would promise mocks that cannot be generated. Residual: **17 non-comment "UPPSC" hits in `apps/api` outside `exam-config.ts`, ZERO of them model-facing** — `UPPSC_EXAM_CODE` and `.eq("exam_code", …)` data filters, the `UPPSC_PRELIMS_MARKING` const name binding numbers, the provenance label table, and one comment.
  - **THE WEB SLICE (`76d88f3`) — and the highest-value fix in it.** The web app previously had **no access to exam config at all**: no route, no hook, and `users_profile.target_exam` arrived in every profile response and was discarded (a grep found only `target_exam_year`, a different field), which is exactly why the UI still carried literals. `GET /api/v1/exams` is mounted **before `requireAuth`** alongside `billingPublicRouter`, backed by a **new `listExamsDetailed()` kept separate from `listExams()`** (the latter runs inside the CA triage loop and needs two columns; this one drags the whole jsonb), parsed with the shared `examSchema` so **malformed jsonb is a loud 500 here rather than wrong marks and thresholds rendered in the UI**. Non-live exams are INCLUDED so `launch_scope_i18n` can be rendered honestly; `is_live` travels with the row **for copy, never authorisation** (the server still enforces `assertSelectableExam`). New `useExams`/`useCurrentExam`/`usePaperCatalog` + `lib/exam-label.ts`. The payoff: **`cutoff-comparison.tsx`** hardcoded `PRE_GS1`/`PRE_CSAT`, a 33% threshold, the /200 scale in three places, and silently dropped every category row except "general" — **a single number is provably wrong across exams, and the endpoint proves it with live data** (UPPSC CSAT is a `stage_gate` 33; UPSC Paper-A/B are `evaluation_gate` 25 — miss one and your OTHER papers are never evaluated; MPPSC is `pct_by_category` `{UR 40, rest 30}`). It now honours all three `minimum.role` values and per-category thresholds; the old literals survive only in a header comment documenting what they replaced.
  - **FOUR WEB REGRESSIONS, FOUND BY BROWSER VERIFICATION, ALL REPRODUCIBLE ON A HEALTHY SERVER — and the one lesson behind all four.** Slice 3's promise was "a UPPSC user sees exactly what they saw before"; a browser pass falsified it. **Root cause: in TanStack v5 `isLoading = isPending && isFetching`, so a DISABLED query reports `isLoading:false` with `data:undefined`.** Components derived a paper code from the async registry, got `""`/null while it was in flight, passed that to a dependent query which was therefore disabled, and rendered that disabled query's `isLoading:false` as "no data" — i.e. **"registry still loading" was being rendered as "registry says nothing"**. Fixed as a PATTERN, not case-by-case: new `apps/web/src/lib/query-state.ts` exposes `isAwaitingData()` built on `isPending` (true both while fetching AND while disabled) and states the rule — *when a query's `enabled` depends on a still-loading derived value, gate on BOTH the input's own loading flag and `isAwaitingData(query)`; checking only the query strands a skeleton at the moment an empty state becomes correct*. **BUG 1** Scoreboard→Mocks: a permanent never-resolving skeleton, and the Prelims dropdown listed **59** options including Mains Essay and General Hindi mocks instead of 7 GS-I ones (`useScoreboardMockTests` had no `enabled` guard, and **an empty `?paper_code=` reads server-side as "no filter"**). **BUG 2** Time Attack was **100% unreachable** — it fell straight to "Not enough published questions yet… try the other paper" **while the paper toggle was itself hidden**, so the copy contradicted itself. **BUG 3** the CSAT cut-off card **silently vanished** when `/exams` failed, because `PRE_CSAT` has **ZERO `exam_cutoffs` rows** and the card hung entirely on the registry minimum — now an honest "Couldn't load the qualifying details for this paper." **BUG 4** paper tabs rendered a provisional wrong order and visibly reshuffled, and **the wrong default STUCK because `defaultValue` is uncontrolled** (CSAT stayed selected after the tabs reordered to GS-I first). Also restored a product decision the refactor had silently reversed: **mock sub-tabs and scoreboard mock pills render the LATIN abbreviation in BOTH locales** ("GS-I"/"CSAT", not जीएस-I/सीसैट) — the old `paperLabel()` said so in an explicit comment ("how UPPSC aspirants refer to the papers"); the registry still supplies the paper SET and ORDER, only the short label is latin, via a new `latinLabel`, with the reasoning in a comment so it is not reversed again.
  - **AUDIT FINDINGS ALSO CONFIRMED FINE, recorded so they are not re-investigated:** `memoisePerExam` returns one instance per exam and **cannot grow unbounded** (`exam_code` is FK-constrained to 3 rows), and no memoised builder depends on locale under an exam-only key (the locale-dependent ones are deliberately not memoised); **no import cycle** — `exam-config`'s app-internal closure is `exam-papers`/`logger`/`supabase` and `exam-papers` imports nothing, i.e. a graph sink, with a whole-graph scan of **198 files finding 0 cycles**; and **all 41 `getExamConfig` call sites pass an FK-constrained value, with ZERO passing `questions.exam_code`** (provenance) — every question path routes through `examCodeForNode` instead. Separately, `assertExamConfigMatchesRegistry` had **ZERO callers repo-wide** while `getExamConfig`'s docstring promised it as the backstop; now wired into boot beside the mentor cache-health probe with three independent guards against crashing boot, verified both ways (real DB → ok, 0 problems; with `SUPABASE_*` unset → logs ERROR, no throw, no unhandled rejection, exit 0).
  - **NOT DONE, deliberately** (all recorded in `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §8f): **U6** — upsc and mppsc still have 74 unauthored slots each, which is real research work, not a rename, and is now the gate before ANY second-exam model call. **M25** — the 5 no-op `cache: true` flags were annotated, not removed (qgen `verifySystem` at 107 vs 4096 will never cache and is the one reasonable future deletion). **M27** — `scoreboard.tsx`'s daily/mains panels keep a pre-existing `board.isLoading || !board.data` (permanent skeleton on ERROR, not on disable), outside the four fixed bugs; the sectional board in the same file WAS converted, being the identical shape as the mocks panel that had to change. **M28** — four visible-but-arguably-correct UX diffs left for a founder call: the PYQ picker's paper order (the registry follows the commission's own order, where **General Hindi is Mains Paper I**, so it now precedes Essay, where the old hardcoded list put it last), `GS I`→`GS-I` and `Gen. Hindi`→`General Hindi`, the daily-quiz chip `GS`→`GS-I`, and the cut-off card's new category selector. **M29** — Time Attack's paper toggle is still localized (जीएस-I) while the mock sub-tabs were deliberately restored to latin-in-both-locales; whether it counts as a compact selector is a one-line call nobody has made. Also untouched: the `ca/prompts.ts` ordering (see above) and the CA magazine (M20).
  - Verified: `pnpm prompts:snapshot` **128 byte-identical**; `pnpm --filter api typecheck` + build, a **FULL web production build** (not a dev server, per the hard rule), `oxlint` at **40 warnings — delta 0 against a git-stash-measured baseline**, and `pnpm check:paths` all clean. Browser verification of the web slice ran against a production build with **5s latency injected on `/exams`** (and a forced 500 for bug 3), each bug reproduced BEFORE and confirmed gone AFTER, plus a DoD sweep of **5 touched pages × {en,hi} × {1440,390} = 20 loads with 0 console errors, 0 horizontal overflow, 0 stuck skeletons**. **CONCURRENT PROCESS:** another session owns the exam-switcher/U3 feature (`magazine`/`profile`/`answer-set`/`daily-progress`/`onboarding` + two new web components + migration `0111_user_exam_streaks.sql`) — untouched throughout; every file staged by explicit name, never `git add -A`, and `index.ts` (the one shared file) diffed to confirm it carries only the lines this work added. **⚑ That concurrent switcher is precisely the thing §8c's U3 checklist item 5 warns about: shipping it settles M16 (one exam per user vs several) implicitly — it should be recorded as a founder decision, not inherited from an implementation.**

## U3: Exam selection UX — shipped and verified live (2026-07-30)
The exam-switcher/U3 feature the prior entry saw as an untouched concurrent process — a picker in onboarding, a switcher in Profile, both reading the exam-config sweep's `GET /exams` — was already code-complete and typecheck/build/lint-clean at the start of this session. This session's job was purely to finish the live UI verification a first attempt at it had died mid-run on (two prior attempts hit transient connection errors), and to write up the docs. **It also found and fixed two more real, live bleed bugs** the earlier interrupted pass's own real find (`tests.ts`/`attempts.ts startAttempt`/`profile-analytics.ts`/`digest.ts`, all already fixed before this session started) had not reached.
  - **What was already built, confirmed by reading the diff before testing:** `components/ui-x/exam-picker-list.tsx` (`ExamPickerList` — live exams selectable, non-live shown "coming soon" with real `launch_scope_i18n` covered/not-covered, no dates), a new onboarding step 2 of 4, `components/profile/exam-switcher-card.tsx` (confirmation dialog, `queryClient.clear()` on success — deliberately blunt since most exam-scoped query keys don't carry the exam code), and `services/profile.ts`'s `updateProfile()` gaining a **per-exam streak park/restore swap**: the active exam's streak stays on `users_profile`'s own scalar columns (every existing reader — dashboard greeting, TopBar flame, `GuidedTodayCard`, milestone triggers — needed zero changes), and a new `user_exam_streaks (user_id, exam_code)` table (migration `0111`, owner-only RLS) parks the OUTGOING exam's values and restores the INCOMING exam's (or fresh defaults) in the SAME statement that changes `target_exam`, so a client never observes a half-applied state.
  - **VERIFICATION, against a throwaway account with genuine seeded UPPSC history, not a blank one** — a real MCQ attempt driven through the actual test-player UI (start → answer via keyboard shortcuts → find "Submit test" scrolled off the palette bottom → real result page), a directly-seeded streak (5, today, 1 freeze), and one manually-created bilingual SRS card, all created before ever touching `is_live`. Onboarding's exam-picker step confirmed at 1440px/390px, en/hi: UPPSC preselected+selectable, UPSC/MPPSC shown "Coming soon" with real covered/not-covered copy, zero date-like strings anywhere in the rendered DOM. Profile switcher confirmed UPPSC-only (0 clickable non-live exams) AND a direct `PATCH /profile {target_exam:'upsc'}` with a real, valid token still 400s server-side ("upsc is not available yet…") — defense in depth, not just a hidden button. `exams.upsc.is_live` flipped `true` (the same sanctioned throwaway pattern M1-M9's own verification used), switched via the REAL switcher UI (not curl): confirmation dialog copy exactly matches the code, `target_exam` flips, streak resets to a fresh 0/null/0 state, and **zero** UPPSC content survives anywhere — dashboard, practice (PYQ/sectional/mock/custom/history), learn ("this exam's syllabus hasn't been added yet"), current-affairs, magazine (index + `/prelims`/`/mains` direct), scoreboard, revision. The mentor (`/doubts`) was asked a real question and, rather than answering, **refused outright** — see below. Switched back: `streak_count`/`last_active_date`/`streak_freezes`/`streak_freeze_used_on`/`days_to_exam`/`next_exam_label_i18n` all restored **byte-identically**, real UPPSC content reappeared (Practice History showed the real attempt again), and — the concrete proof the deck really is shared, not per-exam — the SRS card added *while on the second exam* was still present on the Manage tab after switching back to UPPSC. Confirmation dialog re-checked at 390px in Hindi: no horizontal overflow, all 3 bullets legible. `exams.upsc.is_live` restored to `false`; the throwaway account and every row it touched (`users_profile`, `user_exam_streaks`, `attempts`, `srs_cards`, the auth user itself) deleted by the explicit id captured at creation — confirmed 0 leftovers via direct queries, not just the delete call's own "no error" response (one of those queries — `user_exam_streaks` via `.select("id", {head:true})` — itself errored on a non-existent `id` column, since that table's PK is `(user_id, exam_code)`; caught and re-verified with a correct `select("*")`, a bug in the verification script, not the app).
  - **TWO REAL BUGS FOUND — an actual browser check, not a code read, exactly the class this repo's own convention treats as important.** (1) **`services/dashboard.ts`'s `getPerformanceAndWeakness`**: the "Last 5 scores" sparkline (`submitted` attempts query) had NO exam filter at all, and "Accuracy by paper" was built from the exam-agnostic `getGradedAnswers(userId)` (shared with the papers grid and the mentor's learner profile) with no cross-check against the current exam — live-visible result, a UPPSC CSAT attempt's `PRE_CSAT 50%` rendered on a UPSC dashboard's Performance Snapshot card, right beside a Weakness Radar that was correctly empty (its node lookup was already exam-scoped by M2) — the two cards on the SAME page silently disagreed. Fixed: `submitted` now joins `tests!inner(exam_code)`; `accuracyByPaper` is filtered against a `validPaperCodes` set derived from the exam's own `syllabus_nodes` roots, **plus `CURRENT_AFFAIRS_PAPER_CODE` always included** — that synthetic paper code has no `syllabus_nodes` row for ANY exam, so a naive "must have a real root" check would have silently zeroed out every exam's current-affairs accuracy, caught before shipping by checking the data rather than assuming the filter was safe. (2) **`services/attempts.ts`'s `listAttempts`** (backs Practice's History tab) — zero exam scoping, so a switched-away user's whole MCQ attempt history kept listing (live-confirmed: "PRE_CSAT" and "General Mental Ability (Sectional)" rendered on a UPSC account's History tab). Fixed identically to `listTests`/`startAttempt`: `tests!inner(exam_code)` + `.eq("tests.exam_code", examCode)`. Both fixed with the same pattern M5/M6/M24 already established; `pnpm --filter api typecheck` clean before and after each fix; both re-verified live post-fix (honest empty states on the second exam, real content back after switching to UPPSC).
  - **Confirmed pre-existing, NOT a U3 bug:** asking the mentor a real question ("What is the structure of the Indian Constitution…") while on the second exam does not answer — it renders `exam-config: "mentor.teacherPlatformFraming" is UNAUTHORED for exam "upsc". This slot carries examiner judgment and must be researched and authored for upsc — never derived from another exam's text by substitution (U6).` as the chat reply. Traced to `lib/exam-config.ts`'s `UNAUTHORED` gate, committed in the exam-config prompt sweep (`27fd7d9`/`ac83785`) before this session started. This is a **stronger** safety guarantee than "answers without citing UPPSC content" — a total, loud refusal rather than a soft degraded answer that might still carry UPPSC-flavoured framing — and is explicitly out of U3's scope; U6 (74 config slots per non-UPPSC exam) is tracked separately.
  - **Residual, deliberately untouched:** the CA magazine's `gs_papers`/`is_up_specific` taxonomy stays UPPSC-shaped per the standing M20 deferral (`docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §8b) — there is zero non-UPPSC current affairs to generalise it against, and the magazine index/prelims/mains editions already degrade to an honest "No magazine yet" for a second exam (verified live in this same pass) rather than showing anything wrong, so the deferral remains safe.
  - Docs: `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §8c's U3 checklist marked all 5 items ✅ done, M16 updated to note the shipped switcher implicitly settles it (flagged for an explicit founder call, not silently inherited), a new finding block added under the U3 status update. `docs/multi-exam.md` gained §3g (this session's write-up, mirroring §3f's format) and its top status header now mentions U3; the stale §3f note claiming `getScoreTrajectory`'s cross-exam fallback was "unreachable — no exam-switching UI" was corrected (the query itself is now exam-filtered, AND the switcher now exists, closing the claim from both directions). All `§` cross-references in both docs re-verified to resolve to real headings.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` + build, `pnpm --filter web build` (full production build), `pnpm --filter web lint` at **40 warnings — unchanged from the confirmed baseline**, `pnpm check:paths` — all clean. Working tree left uncommitted for review, exactly the pre-existing diff plus the two bug fixes (`apps/api/src/services/dashboard.ts`, `apps/api/src/services/attempts.ts`).

### Edge-case audit of U3, same day (2026-07-30): a real race condition, two more sibling-function bleed gaps, and a UPPSC content leak in Time Attack
Ran two parallel adversarial audits over the just-committed U3 diff (`0da0e3c`) rather than re-confirming the happy path — one over the backend files the live-verification pass had already touched (hunting for sibling functions with the identical unscoped-query shape the fix next to them just closed, exactly the bug class this repo keeps re-finding), one over the switcher/onboarding UI error paths and adjacent surfaces (milestones, time-attack, ghost battle, scoreboards) nobody had explicitly checked. Both ran fully in parallel by construction: the second agent was told to never touch `exams.is_live` and to simulate a failed switch via Playwright network interception instead, so it couldn't collide with the first agent's live concurrency test.
  - **REAL RACE CONDITION FOUND in the streak park/restore swap (`services/profile.ts`'s `updateProfile`).** The read→park→restore→write sequence is four separate round trips, not one statement. A same-target double-click is harmless (both requests compute byte-identical writes), but two requests racing to *different* destination exams from the same source had a genuine data-loss window: the second request's final `UPDATE` could silently clobber whatever the first had just restored for its own destination exam, with zero trace — concretely, a concurrent `refreshStreak` advancing that exam's streak in the interim would vanish unrecorded. Fixed with an optimistic-concurrency guard rather than a migration/RPC/full transaction (documented in-code as disproportionate for a low-frequency personal-settings action): the final `UPDATE` now includes `.eq("target_exam", <the value this request observed>)` whenever a swap occurred; a 0-row match is checked against the actual winner — if it already landed on the exam this request wanted, return success (idempotent, no spurious error on a double-click), otherwise a clean 409. Live-verified with two throwaway users, 24 checks: sequential integrity, the same-target race, and the different-target race (exactly one 200 + one clean 409, correct final DB state, no corrupted snapshots).
  - **Sibling-function sweep — 6 more real gaps found and fixed, matching the exact shape of the bugs already fixed in the same files:** `tests.ts`'s `createCustomTestFromCurrentAffairs` had no `exam_codes` filter and never stamped `exam_code` on the test it created — the latter meant its own closing `getTestDetail` call would 404 the test it had JUST created for any non-default-exam user. `dashboard.ts`'s `getContinue` had two gaps — an unfinished attempt from a switched-away exam, and a `syllabus_node_view` deep link with **no exam check at all**, a live counterexample to this codebase's own stated assumption that "the UI never links across exams" — plus `getAnswerSpotlight` (latest evaluation, no exam filter). `profile-analytics.ts`'s `fetchRecentEvaluations` (feeds `evaluation_trend`/`dimension_insights`) and `getImprovementProof` both mixed evaluations/pairs across exams. `digest.ts`'s `getLeaderboard` (`GET /leaderboard` — hidden from nav, per Session 15, but not from the router, so genuinely reachable) had zero exam scoping, mixing every user's streak/accuracy regardless of exam. All six fixed with the established `getUserExam`/`tests!inner(exam_code)`/`.overlaps` pattern. **One gap found but deliberately NOT fixed**: `profile-analytics.ts`'s `getAccuracyTimeBuckets` reads through the `profile_accuracy_time_buckets` SQL function, which has the identical unscoped shape — the fix has to live inside the function body, i.e. a migration, out of this audit's scope; recorded rather than patched around. `attempts.ts`'s other functions (`getOwnedAttemptRow`/`getAttemptDetail`/`submitAttempt`/`getAttemptResult`) and `magazine.ts` were spot-checked and confirmed already correctly scoped or exam-agnostic by design.
  - **`current_affairs_items.exam_codes` NULL/empty check: clean.** Paged through all 1,844 published rows (avoiding the 1000-row cap trap this repo keeps hitting) — zero rows with a NULL or empty array, so no pre-existing content is invisible to every exam.
  - **RLS on `user_exam_streaks`: verified live, not just re-read.** Two throwaway users, anon key + real JWTs (this repo's established RLS-verification pattern): user A cannot SELECT/INSERT-as/UPDATE user B's row, can read/write its own directly.
  - **Onboarding: a stale-error bug found and fixed, unrelated to the race above.** The final-submit error state (correctly shown inline with a real server message on a 400) was rendered outside the per-step conditionals with nothing clearing it on navigation — so after a rejected submit, clicking "Back" left the exam-selection error message visible all the way back to step 1 (name entry), which has nothing to do with exam selection. Confirmed live before/after. Fixed: both Back and Next handlers now clear the error before changing steps.
  - **Time Attack: a real UPPSC content leak found and fixed, the most concrete bleed of this whole audit.** `getTimeAttackTopics` had **zero server-side exam check** — since its schema only accepts the two bare UPPSC codes, ANY signed-in user regardless of `target_exam` could call it directly and get real UPPSC topic titles/counts back, content otherwise fully inaccessible to them anywhere else in the app. Live-confirmed: a `target_exam='upsc'` account got 8 real UPPSC topics with a 200. `startTimeAttack` compounded it — it validated the paper code shape but not that the node's exam matched the caller's, and was only rescued downstream by `startAttempt`'s own (already-fixed) exam check, but only AFTER creating real `tests`/`test_questions` rows, leaving an orphaned UPPSC test behind on every blocked cross-exam attempt (confirmed live, cleaned up). Fixed: `getTimeAttackTopics` now returns an honest empty list for a non-UPPSC caller; `startTimeAttack` now fetches the node's exam up front and 404s before any write, matching the established "exam match, not just paper match" convention used elsewhere. Re-verified live post-fix: 0 topics + clean 404 + 0 rows created for a mismatched exam, unchanged 8-topic/201-start behavior for a real UPPSC caller.
  - **Confirmed NOT bugs, checked live rather than assumed** (recorded so they aren't re-investigated): the switcher's `queryClient.clear()` is called only inside the mutation's `onSuccess` — no `onError`/`onSettled` exists to fire it on failure, confirmed via a Playwright network-interception test (forced 500 → dialog stays open with the real error text, target_exam server-side unchanged, and a request-count probe over the following 2.5s showed exactly the one failed PATCH, no cache-wide refetch burst); milestones are correctly GLOBAL/one-time by design (matching the SRS-deck precedent), not per-exam achievements; ghost battle's ownership check plus the already-fixed `startAttempt` exam check together block a cross-exam ghost race with a clean 404 and zero orphaned rows; every `scoreboard.ts` function not already covered by M5-M9 already enforces exam match correctly.
  - Both audits ran against the same live cloud DB with a THIRD, unrelated concurrent process active in the tree throughout (an ingest/seed data-import stream touching `apps/api/src/ingest/_shared.ts`/`syllabus.ts`/`seed/` + both `package.json`s) — confirmed fully disjoint via `git status` before every commit and left completely untouched; every file staged by explicit name, never `git add -A`.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` + build, `pnpm --filter web build` (full production build), `pnpm --filter web lint` at **40 warnings — baseline unchanged**, `pnpm check:paths` — all clean, run once more against the combined diff from both audits before committing. Files touched this pass: `apps/api/src/services/{profile,tests,dashboard,profile-analytics,digest,time-attack}.ts`, `apps/web/src/routes/onboarding.tsx`.

## U5 (2026-07-30/31): UPSC PYQ ingest — 72 papers, and a live production bug it uncovered
The full UPSC Civil Services PYQ bank, ingested through the documented two-step flow. **2,791 questions across 72 papers (2016-2026), 2,760 visible**, every source file from the official `www.upsc.gov.in` host; **22/22 official answer keys, Set A confirmed**; 5,670 embeddings; 927 `mv_node_weightage` rows. `upsc` is still **`is_live: false`** — content is not a launch. Full actionable index in `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` **§8i**; multi-exam write-up in `docs/multi-exam.md` **§3h**. Commits `eab7e56`, `9c03da1`, `f3aac32`, `af67aa7`, `e13e7d2`, `17770f9`.
  - **M23 FIXED FIRST, before the first `ingest:pyq` run**, exactly as `docs/multi-exam.md` §0a prescribed. `classifyPyqId` mapped `upsc_prelims_2024_gs1` to a **bare `PRE_GS1`** and `pyq-load.ts`'s `resolveSyllabusId` resolved it with **no exam filter** — so UPSC PYQs would have attached to UPPSC's tree silently, with no constraint violation to notice. **The bug and its own concealment were the same line.** The missing decision (provenance→product exam) is now `productExamForProvenance` in `ingest/_shared.ts`: a provenance exam that IS a product exam ingests into itself with exam-prefixed codes; a provenance-ONLY exam (`up_ro_aro`/`upsssc_pet`/`other`) keeps mapping onto the DEFAULT exam's shared prelims tree — the pre-existing deliberate overlap, not a fallback invented here. **Byte-identity proven over all 70 pre-existing manifest ids** (their prefix is `""`), so UPPSC is untouched. A paper the exam does not have (`upsc_mains_*_gs5`) now returns null loudly instead of minting a code with no syllabus behind it. `pyq.ts` also stopped hardcoding the product exam — `cls.productExam` is threaded as a REQUIRED parameter into every prompt path (the M24 lesson). Live after loading: **0 UPSC questions on a UPPSC node, 0 UPPSC on a UPSC node, 0 questions on a `UPSC_*` code with a non-`upsc` exam.**
  - **THREE silent content-loss bugs in the extractor, ALL caught by one check: summing `marks` per paper.** A UPSC Mains GS paper is always 250, so the total is a free invariant — and it was the *only* thing that saw any of them. Every structural check (counts, q_no gaps, duplicates, bilingual completeness, option keys) passed on all three. (1) **Sub-parts collide on `q_no`**: 2020 GS-IV prints Section A as separately-scored `1.(a)`/`1.(b)` sharing one integer `q_no`, and `extractWhole` merged with a blind `byNo.set()` (last write wins) → **one sub-part of every Q1-6 discarded**, the paper loading as a clean-looking 12 questions summing to 180. (2) **Section-restarted numbering**: 2017 Essay numbers Section A 1-4 then Section B 1-4 → collided → **4 of 8 topics discarded**. (3) **`marks` declared `integer`** while UPSC Mains 2016 is 20 × **12.5** = 250 → rounded to 260/240 (`questions.marks` has been `numeric(6,2)` since 0062, so the DB was never the constraint). Fixes: the prompt states the sub-part contract and requires `q_no` unique across the whole paper; `marks` is `number`; `extractWhole` keeps the MORE COMPLETE copy on a collision, matching `extractByPages`. **All 50 Mains papers re-verified — every GS paper sums to exactly 250, zero dropped-sub-part signatures. KEEP THE MARKS-TOTAL CHECK; it is the only thing that has ever seen this bug class.**
  - **⚑ A LIVE PRODUCTION BUG, on the LIVE exam, that this ingest only happened to uncover: the mentor was answering UNGROUNDED.** Every `match_embeddings` call through PostgREST failed with `57014 statement timeout` at ~8s — every filter, even `match_count=1` — while the identical SQL on a direct connection ran in ~100ms. `retrieveGrounding` degrades gracefully on an RPC error, so it returned **0 chunks silently** instead of erroring, which is exactly why nothing had ever surfaced it. **Root cause: a `language sql` function is INLINED into the caller's statement.** PostgREST runs RPCs as prepared statements, so Postgres switched to a GENERIC plan where `query_embedding` is an unknown parameter — and a generic plan cannot use the HNSW index for the ORDER BY, falling back to a seq scan + sort over 28k × 1536-dim vectors (measured **20.6s** with a 60s function-level timeout; the 8s `statement_timeout` on `authenticator` was only the messenger). Migration **`0113`** converts both vector RPCs to **plpgsql + dynamic EXECUTE**, making the body opaque to inlining. **The negative results are the useful part, recorded so nobody re-derives them:** `pg_stat_activity` showed **ZERO active backends** during an 8s failure (ruling out "the DB is slow"); RLS was excluded (`service_role` has `bypassrls`, and the same query under that role via `EXECUTE` was fast); and `ALTER FUNCTION ... SET plan_cache_mode='force_custom_plan'` did **NOT** help — itself the confirmation, since a SET clause attaches to function *execution* and an inlined SQL function never executes as a function at all. After: `retrieveGrounding` returns **8 exam-correct passages for both exams**.
  - **`0113` fixes a SECOND, quieter bug in the same place: HNSW silently returned FEWER rows than asked.** With a post-filter, plain HNSW keeps only those of its `ef_search` (40) candidates that survive, so a selective filter under-returns — `filter_exam_code='upsc'` requested 8 and got **5**, i.e. the mentor grounding on 5 passages while believing it had 8. **This bites the smallest partition hardest, which is always the newest exam.** Fixed with pgvector 0.8 `iterative_scan`. The two RPCs pick **different modes on purpose**: `match_embeddings` → `relaxed_order` (callers consume the whole top-k, so membership matters and intra-k order does not; 101-225ms vs a 4.1s cold spike for strict), `match_doubt_faq` → `strict_order` (its TOP-1 similarity drives hard 0.95/0.86 cache thresholds). `match_doubt_faq` was converted too — identical shape, fast today only because that table holds ~40 rows. Applied via `set_config(..., is_local => true)` inside the body, because this DB's migration role is not superuser and `ALTER FUNCTION ... SET hnsw.iterative_scan` is rejected `42501`; `is_local` scopes it to the transaction so it cannot leak onto a pooled connection (verified: reads `off` after the call). **RESIDUAL to watch: the HNSW index is 221 MB against 224 MB of `shared_buffers`**, so the first call for a new query vector can still spike to ~1.5-4.7s (cold page faults) — no longer a timeout, but the headroom shrinks as the bank grows.
  - **A cross-exam embedding leak this ingest itself introduced, then closed.** `collectQuestionChunks` derived an embedding's exam from the question's syllabus node and fell back to the DEFAULT exam when there was none — under a comment reading *"every such row is UPPSC"*, true when written and false the moment UPSC PYQs landed. `ingest:embed`'s own warning moved 40 → 97; the extra ~57 are UPSC questions, and `match_embeddings` filters on exactly that column, so they would have surfaced in **UPPSC users'** grounding. Now falls back to `paper_code` (globally unique across exams per §0, exam-prefixed by M23) — deliberately **not** `questions.exam_code`, which is provenance and includes exams nobody can select. Verified **5,670/5,670 correctly stamped, 0 reverse leaks**.
  - **Answer keys — 22/22 official, Set A confirmed, 0 mismatches on every paper.** **Three naming families, none guessable:** 2018-2023 `AnsKey-CSP-…`; 2024 `AnsKey-CivilServicesPExam-2024-…`; 2025 `AnsKeyCivilServicesP-Exam-2025-…`; and **2016 and earlier use `CSP_16_GS_Paper_I.pdf`, containing no `anskey`/`answer`/`key` token at all** — so a Wayback CDX filter on `anskey` provably cannot find it and the filename is indistinguishable from a question paper. 2026's filename contains a literal **EN DASH (U+2013)** (`%E2%80%93`). **UPSC's own answer-key pages render ZERO rows**, so these URLs are linked from nowhere and cannot be rediscovered by crawling — hence pinned in `content-sources.yaml`.
  - **`loadAnswerKey` is now SERIES-AWARE — the trust gate this task actually turned on.** Every official UPSC key is a 4-page scan carrying **all four booklet series, one per page (p1=A)**. The old prompt just said "extract the answer map", which on such a document is ambiguous and can silently return another series' answers — the exact failure mode behind this repo's 2021 GS-I incident. It now takes the paper's OWN detected series, asks for that series only, makes the model **name** the series it read from, and **discards the key on a mismatch** (→ blind re-solve). Non-option tokens are dropped in prompt and again in code: `X` marks a dropped question, tables are padded with `0`, and neither may become a "verified" answer. Result: all 22 papers report `series A / key series A / aligned`, and the per-paper key-verified counts (98, 99, 79, 99 …) match the sourcing report's dropped-question counts **exactly** — an independent confirmation of the `X`-omission logic.
  - **2026 uses a PROVISIONAL key and is deliberately held to a HIGHER bar** (founder-confirmed). This overturned the starting assumption that no 2026 key could exist: UPSC published one **two days after the exam** (27-05-2026), a change in practice from 2017-2025 where keys appeared about a year later. It self-identifies as `[Prov. Ans. Key]` and can still change, so 2026 is **deliberately absent from `OFFICIAL_COMMISSION`**: listing it would grant gate (A) — publish on the key ALONE — which is the wrong bar. Omitting it routes 2026 through gate (B): the provisional key IS applied, but a question publishes only where an independent blind re-solve agrees. **167 of 180 published, 13 held.** **Re-check for a FINAL 2026 key and promote it then.**
  - **Sources are reproducible now, not hand-dropped.** Every one of the 72 papers had its original official URL recovered from **its own embedded PDF Title metadata**, verified to return `application/pdf` (72/72), and registered tier A. Also fixed a pre-existing entry recorded as *"unreachable from ingest machine"* — **the host was never the problem, the missing `www.` was**: the bare host 307s to the homepage and, followed, returns `200 + text/html`, a failure that masquerades as success. Zero bare-host `upsc.gov.in` URLs remain. **Measured, worth not re-testing: these are IMAGE-ONLY scans** — re-fetching the pristine originals was tried and does NOT recover a text layer (UPSC simply publishes scans), so UPSC extraction is ~100% vision, unlike UPPSC where English extracted cleanly and only Hindi was mojibake. Sole exception: `upsc_prelims_2023_gs1`. The booklets ARE bilingual, so vision reads authentic exam Hindi — **100% bilingual, 0 machine-translated across all 2,791**.
  - **Cost visibility was missing entirely, and is now fixed:** `structuredJson` writes an `llm_calls` row only when `purpose` is set, and the PYQ extract/classify/key/translate calls had none — so **the entire UPPSC bank's extraction is absent from `cost:report`** and there was no per-paper baseline for any exam. Five purposes added. **This session: $61.56** (extraction $41.17 over 72 papers, 2026 blind-resolve escalation $18.07, keys/classify/series $1.93).
  - **`0113` edge-case audit — 15/16** (the one failure was my probe guessing a return shape; re-run correctly after): every filter combination honoured (`locale`, `source_type`, `source_id`, `exam_code`) and combined; `match_count` and its default; an exam with no rows returns empty rather than erroring; similarity in range and non-increasing; **three SQL-injection probes return 0 rows with the table intact** (all values are bound via `USING`, never interpolated); the GUC does not leak to the session; and the real `retrieveGrounding` returns 8 exam-correct passages for BOTH exams. Retrieval isolation re-verified 3 probes × 2 exams with every hit resolved back to its actual `exam_code`; the unfiltered control returns `uppsc=7 upsc=1`, proving the filter is load-bearing rather than cosmetic; a UP-only probe (ODOP) returns **0 hits for `upsc`**. `mv_node_weightage` is sane, not flat: **Polity 203 > Economy 185 > History 175** in Prelims GS-I.
  - **TWO corrections I had to make to my own reporting, same root cause both times:** intermediate status figures (question counts, then spend) were understated because the query hit **PostgREST's 1000-row cap** — it returned exactly 1000, which is the tell. This file warns about that trap repeatedly and it still caught me twice in one session. **Any count over a few hundred rows needs `selectAll`/`.range()` paging or an exact `head:true` count.**
  - **Scope note + a non-defect worth not re-investigating:** the brief said 2018-2025; files existed for 2016/2017/2026 too, flagged rather than silently widened or dropped, and ingested on explicit approval. 2016/2017 Mains GS-IV have 14 questions and 2019 has 19 where other years have 12 — **not a defect**: UPSC genuinely restructures that paper by year and every one still sums to 250. Essay papers sum to 1000 because all 8 topics carry 125 marks and a candidate attempts 2 → 250.
  - **Still open after U5:** **M21** (chapter `pyq_ids` validated for existence, not exam) gates a UPSC chapter rollout, and **U6** gates every model-facing path — U5 needed only **6** of U6's 74 slots, authored per-slot from directly observed evidence (UPSC prints a large standalone series letter AND a separate T.B.C. code whose own letters mislead — a booklet coded `HGY-D-LKUV` is Series **A**), so the other 68 are untouched and still throw. `ingest:tests` was deliberately NOT run for UPSC: it would hit `requireAuthored(...pyqTestTitlePrefix)` and throw, which is U6 working as designed.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` + `build`, `pnpm --filter api prompts:snapshot` (**128 byte-identical**), `pnpm check:paths` — all clean. `pg` was a temporary devDependency for the direct-connection diagnostics and was removed (`package.json`/lockfile confirmed clean). A concurrent process was building a PYQ-archive feature in the same tree throughout; every commit staged only this session's files by explicit name, never `git add -A`.
